3.2.
Proof of Lemma
3.2

The wanted estimates will be proven via induction over
q\to q+1
. Recall that

\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}=\bar{\Psi}_{q}+\bar{\psi}_{q+1},

where by definition (
2.3.10
)

\bar{\psi}_{q+1}=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}H_{12}\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big).

(3.2.1)

3.2.1.
Initial remarks

Let us start with explaining why we propagate all space derivatives and a finite number of mixed derivatives. Very briefly, it is caused by (standard) loss of space derivative. More precisely, there is

(i. Loss of space derivative)

Indeed, recall from its definition (
2.3.4
), that
\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}
(the local, backwards flow) satisfies
\partial_{t}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}=\,0
, which we denote briefly as
D_{q}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}=0
. Taking space derivative, we have

\displaystyle D_{q}\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}

\displaystyle=-\nabla u_{q}\cdot\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}

(3.2.2)

\displaystyle=-\nabla\nabla^{T}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\cdot\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota},

the latter via the definition (
2.3.15
) of
u_{q}
. Since
\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}=1
at its initial time
t_{q,\iota}
, the standard Gronwall-type estimate for the forced transport equation (
3.2.2
) yields:

\|\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}(t)\|_{\infty}\leq\exp\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\|\nabla\nabla^{T}\bar{\Psi}_{q}(s)\|_{\infty}ds

(3.2.3)

In particular, estimates on
\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}
require knowledge of estimates of
\nabla^{2}\bar{\Psi}_{q}
. Taking space derivative in (
3.2.1
) we have

\nabla\bar{\psi}_{q+1}=\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t)(\partial_{\xi}H_{12})\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big).

which together with (
3.2.3
) means that
estimates on
\nabla\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
and thus on
\nabla\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
require knowledge of estimates of
\nabla^{2}\bar{\Psi}_{q}
. This is the mentioned before standard loss of derivative phenomenon. It persists for any higher space derivative, since the above reasoning via the computation

\displaystyle D_{q}\partial_{x}^{n}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}

\displaystyle\sim_{n}\partial_{x}^{n}u_{q}\cdot\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}+\partial_{x}^{n-1}u_{q}\cdot\nabla\partial_{x}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}+\dots+\partial_{x}u_{q}\cdot\nabla\partial^{n-1}_{x}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}

(3.2.4)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}^{n}\nabla^{T}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\cdot\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}+\partial_{x}^{n-1}\nabla^{T}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\cdot\nabla\partial_{x}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}+\dots+\partial_{x}\nabla^{T}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\cdot\nabla\partial^{n-1}_{x}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}

and chain rule applied to (
3.2.1
), shows that estimates on
\nabla^{n}\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
require knowledge of estimates of
\nabla^{n+1}\bar{\Psi}_{q}
.

(ii. No loss in mixed derivatives)

However, if we want to estimate
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
with
{\boldsymbol{\omega}}
containing at least one
t
(i.e. with at least one advective derivative), the situation is different. Assuming for a moment that the commutator
[D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}},D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}]
causes no problems, we want to estimate
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
. In the simplest case
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}=D_{q}\nabla
we see from (
3.2.2
) that when estimating directly its l.h.s. with r.h.s., there is no loss of (order of) derivatives. This holds for any
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}
. Indeed, any
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}
is a sequence of blocks of space and transport derivatives. The case of the innermost derivative being the transport derivative yields
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}=0
, so we are always in the case
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}=D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}D_{q}\nabla^{n}
,
n+1+|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=|\boldsymbol{\omega}|
. The expression
D_{q}\nabla^{n}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}
is as in (
3.2.4
). In particular, we see that this (mixed) derivative of
n+1
-order (l.h.s.) is equal to a sum of products of derivatives, where
\bar{\Psi}_{q}
appears in at most
n+1
-order of derivative (r.h.s.). Outer
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
derivatives induce at most further
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|
derivatives of
\bar{\Psi}_{q}
. All in all, we expect no loss of derivative in the mixed derivative case.
2
2
2
Of course, the estimate of
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}=D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}(\bar{\Psi}_{q}+\bar{\psi}_{q+1})
requires dealing with
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}
and not with
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}
, full flow
\bar{\Phi}_{q}
and not
\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}
, etc. This initial discussion indicates only that in the case of mixed derivatives, there is no derivative loss mechanism, which appears when dealing with pure space derivatives.

(iii. Propagation of derivatives)

To sum up, we have to deal with loss of derivative in case of pure space derivatives, and not in the case of mixed derivatives.

There are two standard remedies to the derivative loss. Either one mollifies the quantities involved, which allows appropriately high derivative estimates to be given not by (lossy) inductive assumptions, but also by mollified quantities; or one chooses to propagate all derivatives. In the former case, one needs to quantify the difference between the original and mollified quantities, in the latter one needs to quantify and control the arising combinatorial coefficients (appearing for instance in product rule, see e.g. (
3.2.4
) with its
\sim_{n}
, or in the chain rule). We will follow the latter approach, which has been used in
[
ArmstrongVicol
]
.

Therefore we propagate all space derivatives, but we don’t have to propagate all mixed derivatives, which allows us also to be less careful about constants in the mixed derivative case.

Remark 3.11

(Amplitude and magnitude)
.

We will use interchangeably ’magnitude of
f
’ or ’amplitude of
f
’ for
\|f\|_{\infty}

3.2.2.
Propagating pure space derivatives

This section is analogous to section 2.3 of
[
ArmstrongVicol
]
. We provide our proof for the sake of reader’s convenience, in particular, since our language and that of
[
ArmstrongVicol
]
differ, and it seems our proof is straightforward.

Remark 3.12
.

(Comparison of name of scales with
[
ArmstrongVicol
]
)
The name of the step in our case is
q
, in the case of
[
ArmstrongVicol
]
is
m
. Keeping this in mind, the translation of scales is

\varepsilon_{m}\sim\lambda^{-1}_{q},\quad a_{m}=\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}_{q}\lambda_{q},

in particular
a_{m}\varepsilon^{2}_{m}=\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}_{q}}{\lambda_{q}}
is the amplitude of the potential
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
.

We want to reproduce (
3.0.13
), i.e.

\|\nabla^{n}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\|_{\infty}\leq\,\frac{(n-1)!}{n^{2}}\tilde{C}(C{\lambda}_{q})^{n-1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\qquad\text{for any }n\geq 2

with
q\to q+1
. Since

\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}=\bar{\Psi}_{q}+\bar{\psi}_{q+1},

it is crucial to prove an appropriate estimate on space derivatives of

\bar{\psi}_{q+1}(x,t)=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}H_{12}\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big),

recall (
3.2.1
), where
the main point is to control carefully the combinatorial coefficients that arise when taking derivatives of an arbitrary order. To this end let us recall and tailor the setting and two results of
[
ArmstrongVicol
]
.

(i. Tools for dealing with an arbitrary space derivative)

Introduce analytic-norm-type quantity for
n\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\llbracket f\rrbracket_{n,R}=\frac{(n+1)^{2}}{n!R^{n}}\sup_{|i|=n}\ \lVert\partial^{i}f\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\llbracket f\rrbracket_{R}=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\ \llbracket f\rrbracket_{n,R}\,.

(3.2.5)

where
\partial^{i}
denotes any space derivative of order
|i|
.

In order to control space derivatives of
\bar{\Phi}_{q}
, let us use Lemma B.7 of
[
ArmstrongVicol
]
with its parameters given as follows:

Y:=\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}-\mathrm{Id},\quad\mathbf{f}:=u_{q},\quad\mathbf{g}:=-u_{q},\quad d:=2,

initial time shifted to
t_{0}:=t_{q,\iota}
, and
N
arbitrary. We get

Lemma 3.13
.

Assume there exist
C,R
and such that for any
n\geq 1

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\ \llbracket u_{q}(t,\cdot)\rrbracket_{n,R}\leq C

(3.2.6)

Then,
\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}
(solving (
2.3.4
)) satisfies for any
n\geq 1

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}\ \frac{1}{|t|}\llbracket\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}(t_{q,\iota}+t,\cdot)-Id\rrbracket_{n,R_{\bar{\Phi}}(t)}\leq 16C

(3.2.7)

where

R_{\bar{\Phi}}(t):=R(1+\frac{|t|}{T})\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad T:=\frac{1}{8CR}.

(3.2.8)

In order to deal with the composition appearing in the definition of
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
, we recall Proposition B.6 of
[
ArmstrongVicol
]
(using there arbitrary
m
)

Proposition 3.14

(Composition estimate)
.

Assume that there exist positive constants
C_{h}
,
C_{g}
,
R_{h}
,
R_{g}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\llbracket h\rrbracket_{n,R_{h}}\leq C_{h}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\llbracket g\rrbracket_{n_{1},R_{g}}\leq C_{g}

for all
0\leq n
and
1\leq n_{1}
. Then, for every
0\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle\llbracket h\circ g\rrbracket_{n,R}\leq C_{h}\,\qquad\mbox{where}\qquad R=R_{g}(1+dC_{g}R_{h})\,.

(3.2.9)

(ii. Estimate on the flow map)

Since
u_{q}=\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\Psi}_{q}
our assumption
(
3.0.13
) is

\|\nabla^{n}\bar{u}_{q}\|_{\infty}\leq\,\frac{n!}{(n+1)^{2}}({C\lambda}_{q})^{n}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tilde{C}\qquad\text{for any }n\geq 1

i.e.

\llbracket u_{q}\rrbracket_{n,C\lambda_{q}}\leq\tilde{C}\delta_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\qquad\text{for any }n\geq 1.

(3.2.10)

This used in Lemma
3.13
gives that for
n\geq 1

\llbracket\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}(t_{q,\iota}+t,\cdot)-\mathrm{Id}\rrbracket_{n,2C\lambda_{q}}\leq 16\tilde{C}\delta_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}|t|

(3.2.11)

for any
|t|\leq\frac{1}{8\tilde{C}C\delta_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{q}}
; where above we replaced
R_{\bar{\Phi}}(t)
given by Lemma
3.13
with its upper bound:

R_{\bar{\Phi}}(t)=C\lambda_{q}(1+\nicefrac{{|t|}}{{T}})\leq 2C\lambda_{q},

which is allowed because the norm-type-quantity
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{n,R}
is monotone in
R
by definition.
In particular any

\mu_{q+1}\geq 8\tilde{C}C\delta_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{q}

(3.2.12)

is admissible as the upper bound for
|t|
and gives

\llbracket\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}(t_{q,\iota}+t,\cdot)-\mathrm{Id}\rrbracket_{n,2C\lambda_{q}}\leq 16\tilde{C}\delta_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\mu_{q+1}}

(3.2.13)

which translated into the standard norm is

\lVert\nabla^{n}(\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}(t_{q,\iota}+t,\cdot)-\mathrm{Id})\rVert_{\infty}\leq 16\tilde{C}\frac{n!}{(n+1)^{2}}(2C\lambda_{q})^{n}\delta_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\mu_{q+1}}\leq\frac{n!}{(n+1)^{2}}(2C\lambda_{q})^{n-1},

(3.2.14)

where for the latter inequality suffices

\mu_{q+1}\geq 16\tilde{C}\delta_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{q}.

(3.2.15)

Both assumptions (
3.2.15
) and (
3.2.12
) on relation between
\mu_{q+1}
and
\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}
are valid in our scheme, since they are our CFL (Gronwall) condition
\mu_{q+1}\gg\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}
.

Recalling that the global flow map
\bar{\Phi}_{q}=\sum_{\iota}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}
by its definition (
2.3.6
), and using the properties of
\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}
(which here, unlike in certain convex integration schemes, does not depend on space variable at all), we therefore have for
n\geq 1

\begin{cases}\lVert\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q}\rVert&\leq 1+\frac{1}{4}\leq\frac{1}{4}5(2C\lambda_{q})^{1-1},\\
\lVert\nabla^{n}\bar{\Phi}_{q}\rVert&\leq\frac{n!}{(n+1)^{2}}(2C\lambda_{q})^{n-1},\quad\text{for }n\geq 2,\end{cases}

i.e. for
n\geq 1

\llbracket\bar{\Phi}_{q}\rrbracket_{n,2C\lambda_{q}}\leq 5(2C\lambda_{q})^{-1}

(3.2.16)

(iii. Estimate on the potential)

Having the estimate (
3.2.16
), we use it now to estimate space derivatives of

\bar{\psi}_{q+1}=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}H_{12}\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big).

To this end choose in the composition Proposition
3.14

h(\cdot,t):=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}H_{12}(\cdot,t),\quad g(x,t)=\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t)

From the definition of
H_{12}
(observe that its space derivatives cost
2\pi
each and, importantly, they are much more tame than its time derivatives) and from (
3.2.16
) we have

\llbracket h\rrbracket_{n,2\pi}\leq\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\llbracket g\rrbracket_{n_{1},2C\lambda_{q}}\leq 5(2C\lambda_{q})^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}

for all
0\leq n\leq m
and
1\leq n_{1}\leq m
. Then, for every
0\leq n\leq m
, Proposition
3.14
gives for every
n\geq 0

\displaystyle\llbracket\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\rrbracket_{n,R}\leq\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}

(3.2.17)

with

R=2C\lambda_{q}(1+d5(2C\lambda_{q})^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}2\pi)=2C\lambda_{q}+\lambda_{q+1}20\pi\leq C\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{q+1}

where the last inequality holds thanks to (initial) scale separation and choice of
C>40\pi
. In other words for
n\geq 0

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla^{n}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n!}{(n+1)^{2}}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(C\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{q+1})^{n}\lambda^{-1}_{q+1}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\frac{n!}{(n+1)^{2}}2^{-n}}_{\leq\frac{(n-1)!}{n^{2}}}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(C\lambda_{q+1})^{n-1}C

which is the wanted estimate (
3.0.14
). The estimate on
\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
follows by adding the inductive assumption (valid for
n\geq 2
) to (
3.0.14
), to obtain for
n\geq 2

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla^{n}\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lVert\nabla^{n}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\rVert_{\infty}+\lVert\nabla^{n}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(n-1)!}{n^{2}}\tilde{C}(C{\lambda}_{q})^{n-1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\frac{(n-1)!}{n^{2}}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(C\lambda_{q+1})^{n-1}C

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(n-1)!}{n^{2}}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{n-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{C}C^{n-1}+C\right)

where the
\frac{1}{2}
in the second line above is given by the scale separation. Consequently, choosing
\tilde{C}
so that
\left(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{C}C^{n-1}+C\right)\leq\tilde{C}C^{n-1}
, for which suffices
\tilde{C}=2
, we have the desired inductive step for
\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
, i.e. (
3.0.13
).

Remark 3.15
.

(lower bound on number of derivatives)
The estimate on
\nabla^{n}\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
is restricted for
n\geq 2
, whereas the estimate on
\nabla^{n}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
holds for
n\geq 0
. The lower bound
n\geq 2
follows from the fact that estimate for sum of terms cannot be small (
\bar{\Psi}_{q}=\sum_{i\leq q}\bar{\psi}_{i}
), whereas estimates for a single summand (
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
) can be small. (We could also state the inequality without the restriction
n\geq 2
as
\|\nabla^{n}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{n}1\vee{\lambda}_{q}^{n-2}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})
.)

3.2.3.
Interlude. Non-commutative product and chain rule

Recall our multi-index notation
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=(\alpha(1),\dots,\alpha(n_{0}))
, where each
\alpha(i)
denotes one derivative (of certain type, in our case: partial spatial derivative or transport derivative). Denoting by
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\prec{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
any subsequence of
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
without permutation (i.e. the order of entries of
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
is conserved) we have

D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(fg)=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\prec{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}(D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f)(D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\setminus\boldsymbol{\beta}}g),

(3.2.18)

which is a ’primitive’ of the binomial formula, without commutativity properties between derivatives.

In case we have a smooth function
H:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, the following non-commutative chain rule (or ’Faá di Bruno formula’) holds

D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(H(f))=\sum_{i=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}H^{(i)}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1..i\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}|\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+1-i\\
\cup_{j}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}\\
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\prec\boldsymbol{\alpha}\end{subarray}}D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}}f,

(3.2.19)

where
H^{(i)}
is the
i
-th derivative of
H
,
\cup_{j}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}
denotes that the elements of the choice of sequences
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}
constitute together, after possible rearrangement, the sequence
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\prec\boldsymbol{\alpha}
denotes as before that the sequences
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}
are chosen from
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
respecting the order of elements of
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
.

In case
H:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
, the multivariate version of (
3.2.19
) reads

D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(H(f_{1},\dots,f_{n}))=\sum_{\zeta=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}=1..n\\
k_{2}=1..n\\
\dots\\
k_{\zeta}=1..n\end{subarray}}\partial_{k_{\zeta}}\dots\partial_{k_{1}}H\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1..\zeta\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}|\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+1-i\\
\cup_{j}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}\\
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\prec\boldsymbol{\alpha}\end{subarray}}D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}}f_{k_{j}},

(3.2.20)

We will usually specify these formulas to simpler cases, when applied.

3.2.4.
Propagating mixed derivatives. I. Flowmap derivatives

We begin proving estimate (
3.0.15
). Now, the total number of derivatives is bounded from above by
Y
, consequently the combinatorial constants resulting from composition and product estimates are unimportant: as usual in similar iterative schemes, they will be uniformly stabilised at the step
q\to q+1
, when applied.

Crucially, there is no loss of total number of derivatives in the step
q\to q+1
, recall Section
3.2.1
(ii).

We already know, thanks to (
3.2.16
), that for each local flow
\Phi_{q,\iota}
and for the global flow
\bar{\Phi}_{q}

\displaystyle|\nabla^{i}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}|_{supp\;\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}}\quad+\quad|\nabla^{i}\bar{\Phi}_{q}|

\displaystyle\lesssim 1,\qquad i=0,1

(3.2.21)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{i}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}|_{supp\;\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}}\quad+\quad|\nabla^{i}\bar{\Phi}_{q}|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\lambda}^{i-1}_{q},\qquad i=1,\dots,L

Let us analyse
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}
with respect to how many advective derivatives there are in
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
. Recall (
3.2.4
) and consider the case of one advective derivative present in
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
, i.e.
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\nabla^{m}D_{q}\nabla^{n}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}
. We have

\displaystyle D_{q}\nabla^{n}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}

\displaystyle\sim_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\underbrace{\nabla^{n+1-i}\nabla^{T}\bar{\Psi}_{q}}_{\text{by assumption, costs }{\lambda}_{q}^{n+1-i}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}};}\underbrace{\nabla^{i}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}}_{\quad\text{costs }{\lambda}^{i-1}_{q}\text{ on }supp\;\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}}

(3.2.22)

Since for both terms the cost of one space derivative is
{\lambda}_{q}
,
\nabla^{m}D_{q}\nabla^{n}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}
costs

{\lambda}^{m}_{q}{\lambda}_{q}^{n+1-i}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\lambda}^{i-1}_{q}=\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\lambda}^{m+n}_{q}.

In other words, we have

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}|_{supp\;\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}}\lesssim{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}-1}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}},\qquad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=1,\quad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|-1\;(=m+n)

(3.2.23)

Remembering that the transport derivatives of the global flow involve also
\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}
, whose (time) derivatives cost
\mu_{q+1}>\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}
each, we obtain

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}|\lesssim{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}-1}\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\qquad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=1,\quad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|-1

(3.2.24)

Further

\displaystyle\nabla^{m}D_{q}\nabla^{n}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\underbrace{\nabla^{n+1-i+m-j}\nabla^{T}\bar{\Psi}_{q}}_{\text{costs }{\lambda}_{q}^{n+1-i+m-j}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\underbrace{\nabla^{i+j}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}}_{\text{costs }{\lambda}^{i-1+j}_{q}\text{ on }supp\;\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}}.

(3.2.25)

Applying above
D_{q}
, in view of the already established case of one advective derivative acting on the flowmap, i.e. (
3.2.23
), we see that
\nabla^{k}D_{q}\nabla^{m}D_{q}\nabla^{n}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}
costs
{\lambda}^{m+n+k-1}_{q}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\lambda}_{q})^{2}
on the supports of
\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}
, i.e.

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}|_{supp\;\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}}\lesssim{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}-1}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}},\qquad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=2,\quad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|-2\;(=m+n+k)

Taking into account as before the derivatives of the cutoffs appearing for the global flowmap, we have

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}|\lesssim{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}-1}\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\qquad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=2,\quad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|-2.

(3.2.26)

Repeating this reasoning for three, etc, up to
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|-1
advective derivatives, we reach

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}|\lesssim{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}-1}\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\quad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}\geq 1.

(3.2.27)

(The case of
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|
-many advective derivatives, i.e. solely advective derivatives present in
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}
yields nonzero quantities only from cutoffs, since
\Phi_{q,\iota}=0
is a flowmap. Consequently
\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}
appears as r.h.s. in (
3.2.27
). We leave this case out, because it is not needed in what follows, and not consistent with (
3.2.21
)).

Observe that the estimate (
3.2.27
) is consistent with (
3.2.21
), so we can write

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}|\lesssim{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}-1}\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\quad\text{ for any }\quad|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y,\;|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}\geq 1.

(3.2.28)

Remark 3.16
.

One can argue equivalently using commutator estimates. Most of them, available ’from the shelf’, seem to induce however some loss of derivative.

3.2.5.
Propagating mixed derivatives. II. Estimates for
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\psi_{q+1}
.

Recall

\bar{\psi}_{q+1}=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}H_{12}\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big)

First, we make the following structural observation

\displaystyle D_{q}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}D_{{q}}H_{12}\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mu_{q+1}(\partial_{\tau}H_{12})\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big)+\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(D_{q}\bar{\Phi}_{q})\cdot(\nabla_{\xi}H_{12})\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big).

Importantly, the last term above vanishes, because

(D_{{q}}\bar{\Phi}_{q})\cdot(\nabla_{\xi}H_{12})()=\left(\sum_{\iota}\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}D_{q}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}+\sum_{\iota}\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}\right)\cdot(\nabla_{\xi}H_{12})()=0,

(3.2.29)

indeed
D_{q}\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}=0
and the (time) supports of derivatives of partitions of unity
\tilde{\eta}
are disjoint from the (time) supports of
H_{12}
, since
H_{12}
involves in its definition appropriate cutoffs
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}
. Therefore we have

D_{{q}}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mu_{q+1}(\partial_{\tau}H_{12})\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big).

(3.2.30)

Observe that the higher advective derivatives of
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
behave identically, thus

D^{n}_{{q}}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mu^{n}_{q+1}(\partial^{n}_{\tau}H_{12})\big(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big).

(3.2.31)

Without the cancellation (
3.2.29
), and the resulting (
3.2.30
), (
3.2.31
), the obtained estimates would prove insufficient.

For the general case of
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
we may use directly the multivariate chain rule (
3.2.20
) specified to three dimensions, with choices:

H(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot):=H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\cdot,\lambda_{q+1}\cdot,\cdot),\quad(f_{1},f_{2}):=\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\quad f_{3}:=\mu_{q+1}t

It is more tractable, however, to make several simplifying observations first. Recall we are now interested only in derivatives up to order
Y
. Consequently the size of any
\partial_{k_{\zeta}}\dots\partial_{k_{1}}H_{12}
is bounded above by a uniform constant, and changes of size between different derivatives of
H_{12}
are thus negligible. Moreover

(i) While taking a (space or advective) derivative of

H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t),\text{\quad or of any \quad}(\partial_{k_{\zeta}}\dots\partial_{k_{1}}H)(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)

(3.2.32)

the space derivative acts only on the inner functions
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t)
and the advective derivative acts only on
\mu_{q+1}t
. This is the already observed cancellation (
3.2.29
).

(ii) Further, the cost of an advective derivative acting on (
3.2.32
) is
\mu_{q+1}
(in view of (i), it is now pure time derivative), which is consistent with the cost of the advective derivative of
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t)
, cf (
3.2.28
).

(iii) In view of the chain rule (
3.2.20
),

D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}}\left(H(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t))\right)

(3.2.33)

is a sum of products of terms of type (
3.2.32
) and advective derivatives of
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t)
. Consequently, via (ii), an advective derivative of (
3.2.33
) costs
\mu_{q+1}
.

(iv) Each space derivative applied to (
3.2.33
) results in the chain rule present only via space derivative of
\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t)
(and costs up to
\lambda_{q+1}
, which will be clear in the following computation).

The above simplifications show that
D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
can be modelled with the following combination of (simpler) one-dimensional chain rules (compare (
3.2.19
))

\displaystyle|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}(x,t)|

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)|

(3.2.34)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{Y}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0..|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x},\\
k=0..|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t},\\
i+j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\lambda^{i}_{q+1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1..i+k\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}|\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+1-(i+k)\\
\cup_{j}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}\\
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\prec\boldsymbol{\alpha}\end{subarray}}\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}\|_{\infty}

use (
3.2.28
)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{Y}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0..|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x},\\
k=0..|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t},\\
i+j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\lambda^{i}_{q+1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1..i+k\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}|\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+1-(i+k)\\
\cup_{j}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}\\
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\prec\boldsymbol{\alpha}\end{subarray}}{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}|_{x}-1}\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}|_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{Y}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mu^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}_{q+1}\sum_{i=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}\lambda^{i}_{q+1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1..i\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}|\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{x}|+1-i\\
\cup_{j}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{x}\\
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\prec\boldsymbol{\alpha}\end{subarray}}{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}|_{x}-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{Y}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mu^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}_{q+1}\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}_{q+1},

where the last inequality follows from the fact that in the sum (in the penultimate row) the last summand is the largest (since
\lambda_{q+1}\gg\lambda_{q}
, up to combinatorial coefficients, taken over by
\lesssim_{Y}
).
We obtained

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}(x,t)|\lesssim_{Y}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mu^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}_{q+1}\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}-1}_{q+1}

(3.2.35)

for any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y
. Observe that even though the used estimate (
3.2.28
) has restriction
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}\geq 1
, it does not appear in (
3.2.35
). Indeed, in (
3.2.34
) application of chain rule does not need (
3.2.28
) with
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=0
, and there is eventually
\lambda^{-1}_{q+1}
appearing thanks to
\nicefrac{{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}{{\lambda_{q+1}}}
.

3.2.6.
Propagating mixed derivatives. III. Estimates for
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\psi_{q+1}
.

In order to deal with transport derivatives with respect to
u_{q+1}=u_{q}+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
, let us shift the flow as follows

D_{q+1}f=D_{q}f+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla f.

(3.2.36)

We will use the estimate

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}(x,t)|\lesssim_{Y}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}-1}_{q+1}\quad\text{ for any }|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y

(3.2.37)

which follows from
(
3.2.35
) and (
3.0.14
), with
\mu_{q+1}\leq\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}
, see Remark
2.1
.
In particular, choosing
f=\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
in (
3.2.36
), we have

|D_{q+1}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}|\lesssim\delta_{q+1}=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})

(3.2.38)

which is the wanted (
3.0.15
) for
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=0
and
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=1
. One more advective derivative on
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
yields:

\displaystyle D^{2}_{{q+1}}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}

\displaystyle=(D_{q}+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla)(D_{q}\psi_{q+1}+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\bar{\psi}_{q+1})

\displaystyle=D^{2}_{q}\psi_{q+1}+(D_{q}\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1})\cdot\nabla\bar{\psi}_{q+1}+(\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1})\cdot(D_{q}\nabla\bar{\psi}_{q+1})+

\displaystyle\;+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla D_{q}\psi_{q+1}+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\bar{\psi}_{q+1}+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot(\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla^{2}\bar{\psi}_{q+1})

(where
\cdot
needs to be interpreted appropriately). Using (
3.2.37
) we consequently have

\displaystyle|D^{2}_{q+1}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\mu^{2}_{q+1}+\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mu_{q+1}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

which in view of the recently invoked
\mu_{q+1}\leq\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}
gives

|D^{2}_{q+1}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}|\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{2}.

We computed
D^{2}_{q+1}
to clearly make the point the the expected cost of higher-order advective derivatives is not
\mu_{q+1}
as one could expect from (
3.2.35
), but
\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}
, due to
\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla
.

The general estimate for
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
follows the same lines. Indeed, let us observe first that any block of advective derivatives present in
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
is a block of
(D_{q}+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla)
. Application of
(D_{q}+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla)
to any function
f
with magnitude
M
, whose advective derivative cost is (no bigger than)
\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}
and space derivative cost is
\lambda_{q+1}
results in a function whose magnitude is

M(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}+\|\nabla\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\|\lambda_{q+1})=M(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}+\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})\leq 2M\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}

and whose derivative cost is the same as that of
f
. The constant
2
above is consumed by
\lesssim_{Y}
. This observation can be used to any
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
with
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}<\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, yielding

\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{Y}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}

which is the wanted
(
3.0.16
). Taking into account that
\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}=\bar{\Psi}_{q}+\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
, compare also Remark
3.15
, we have the remaining wanted (
3.0.15
).
This ends the proof of Lemma
3.2
.

3.3.
Proof of Lemma
3.3
and its Corollaries

3.3.1.
Initial remarks

Proof of Lemma
3.3
uses in many places reasonings very similar to proof of Lemma
3.2
, which we invoke without repeating fully. In particular, we will use

Remark 3.17

(Remark on commutativity)
.

As observed in the proof of Lemma
3.2
, even though the space vs advective derivatives did not commute, since the estimates for each type of derivative were consistent regardless of the order of derivation, the resulting inequalities were as if the derivatives commuted.

There is, however, one important difference between the currently wanted estimates for
\Psi_{q+1}
and the already proven in Lemma
3.2
estimates for
\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
. Namely, the estimates on
\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
follow from an inductive procedure, so in essence from estimates on
\bar{\Psi}_{q}
. Therefore we were careful concerning derivative loss, since otherwise induction over
q\in\mathbb{N}
fails (looses eventually all derivatives). However, the estimates for
\Psi_{q+1}
involve essentially the estimate for
\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
and no induction is used. (So ’loss of derivative’
Y\to Y-N_{*}-1
is not related to inductive step
\Psi_{q}\to\Psi_{q+1}
, but to a ’same step comparison’ between
\Psi_{q+1}
and
\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
).

3.3.2.
Estimates on flowmaps

Before proceeding to the estimates for
\psi_{q+1}
, let us obtain estimates for the explicit flow
\Phi_{q}
and for the difference between the exact and the explicit flows, i.e.
\bar{\Phi}_{q}-\Phi_{q}=\sum_{\iota}(\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}-\Phi_{q,\iota})\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}
.
In view of the formula directly preceding (
3.1.13
)

D_{q}\Phi_{q,\iota}=\frac{1}{N_{*}!}((t_{q,\iota}-t)^{N_{*}}D_{q}^{N_{*}}u_{q})=\frac{1}{\mu^{N_{*}}_{q+1}}D_{q}^{N_{*}}\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\frac{1}{{N_{*}}!}(\iota-\mu_{q+1}t)^{N_{*}}=:G.

Importantly, the velocity used above is
u_{q}=\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\Psi}_{q}
, whose estimates we know. In particular, in view of (
3.0.15
), for any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y-N_{*}

\displaystyle|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q}G|_{\operatorname{supp}\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\mu^{N_{*}}_{q+1}}\frac{\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q}}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{N_{*}+|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+1}

\displaystyle=(\frac{\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}}{\mu_{q+1}})^{N_{*}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}

(3.3.1)

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}}_{q+1}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}},

with the last uses the
\gamma
-gap in
\mu_{q+1}>\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}
.
So
\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}-\Phi_{q,\iota}
solves

D_{q}(\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}-\Phi_{q,\iota})=g

on the support of
\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}
with zero initial datum.
The solution of
\partial_{t}f+u\cdot\nabla f=g
with zero initial datum enjoys

\|f(\cdot,t)\|_{0}\leq\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\|g(\cdot,\tau)\|_{0}d\tau,

and, up to its Gronwall time,

|\nabla^{n}f(t)|\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\big(|g(\cdot,\tau)|_{n}+(t-\tau)|u|_{n}|g(\cdot,\tau)|_{1}\big)d\tau\quad\text{ for }n\geq 1

see for instance
[
GiriRadu
]
, Proposition B.1. Consequently, using (
3.3.1
) and (
3.0.15
) we have for any
n=0,\dots,Y-N_{*}

\displaystyle|\nabla^{n}(\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}-\Phi_{q,\iota})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\big(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}}_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{n}+(t-\tau)\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{n}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}}_{q+1}\lambda_{q}\big)d\tau

\displaystyle\leq(\mu^{-1}_{q}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\mu^{-2}_{q}\delta_{q}\lambda_{q})\lambda_{q}^{n}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}}_{q+1}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}}_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{n},

where for the last inequality we used lossy inequality
(\mu^{-1}_{q}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\mu^{-2}_{q}\delta_{q}\lambda_{q})\leq 1
for the scales. This means that we have the following counterpart of the estimate
(
3.2.21
)

|\nabla^{i}(\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}-\Phi_{q,\iota})|_{supp\;\tilde{\eta}_{q,\iota}},\quad|\nabla^{i}(\bar{\Phi}_{q,\iota}-\Phi_{q,\iota})|\lesssim\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}}_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{i}\leq\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{i-1}\qquad i=0,\dots,L-N_{*}

(3.3.2)

Reasoning now analogously to section
3.2.4
and observing that the cost of derivatives of the forcing term
G
is commensurate, we have for
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=0,\dots,Y-N_{*}

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}-\Phi_{q})|\lesssim{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}-1}\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}{\lambda}^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}.

(3.3.3)

Triangle inequality and (
3.2.28
), (
3.3.3
) yield for
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=1,\dots,Y-N_{*}

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\Phi_{q}|\lesssim\frac{1}{{\lambda}_{q}}{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}.

(3.3.4)

3.3.3.
Estimates for
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}\psi_{q+1}

Recall that by definition (
2.3.10
)

\psi_{q+1}(x,t)=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\det\nabla\Phi_{q})H_{12}\big(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big).

The term
H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)
above behaves as the term
H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)
appearing in
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}(x,t)
, already analysed. Indeed,
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t)
and
\Phi_{q}(x,t)
have identical estimates for any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y-N_{*}
(see, respectively, (
3.2.28
) and (
3.3.4
)) and the involved cutoff functions are the same. Consequently, along the proof of estimates for
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}(x,t)
, see sections
3.2.5
,
3.2.6
, we have

\left\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}H_{12}\big(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t\big)\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{Y}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}\quad\text{ for any }|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y-N_{*}.

(3.3.5)

The estimate (
3.3.4
), taking into account Remark
3.17
and quadraticity of
\det
, gives

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}(\det\nabla\Phi_{q})|\lesssim{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}}\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{t}}\quad\text{ for any }|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq Y-N_{*}-1.

(3.3.6)

In order to provide the wanted estimate for
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}\psi_{q+1}
, we need estimates for
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}(\det\nabla\Phi_{q})
, i.e. ’shift the flow’ in the estimate (
3.3.6
). Recall that
D_{q+1}=(D_{q}+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla)
. We have full knowledge of estimates of
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
, so repeating the reasoning of section
3.2.6
, in particular its second part, now for function
\det\nabla\Phi_{q}
, we obtain for any
|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq Y-N_{*}-1
.

|D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}(\det\nabla\Phi_{q})|\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{t}}.

(3.3.7)

Putting together estimates (
3.3.7
) and (
3.3.5
) for the two ingredients of
\psi_{q+1}
we reach via the product rule/Remark
3.17
the wanted (
3.0.18
) for
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
derivatives, and consequently (
3.0.17
) for
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
derivatives, with the upper bound on number of derivatives dictated by the bound in (
3.3.6
).

Let us note in passing, for further reference, that analogously to the way we obtained (
3.3.7
) from (
3.3.6
), we see that (
3.3.6
) implies for any
|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq Y-N_{*}-1
(and for each component
\nabla\Phi^{1}_{q}
,
\nabla\Phi^{2}_{q}
, which we do not distinguish below)

|D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}(\nabla\Phi_{q})|\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{t}}

(3.3.8)

and that

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla(\bar{\Phi}_{q}-\Phi_{q})|\lesssim{\lambda}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}\mu_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}{\lambda}^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}

given by (
3.3.3
) implies

|D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}(\bar{\nabla}\Phi_{q}-\nabla\Phi_{q})|\lesssim\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{t}}.

(3.3.9)

3.3.4.
Estimates for
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}(\bar{\psi}_{q+1}-\psi_{q+1})

In view of definitions (
2.3.13
), (
2.3.14
), and (
2.3.9
), (
2.3.10
), we have

\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}-\Psi_{q+1}=\bar{\psi}_{q+1}-\psi_{q+1}
and

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}_{q+1}-\psi_{q+1}

(3.3.10)

\displaystyle=(1-\det\nabla\Phi_{q})\bar{\psi}_{q+1}

\displaystyle=I

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\det\nabla\Phi_{q})(H_{12}\left(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)-H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)\right)

\displaystyle=:II

The term I in (
3.3.10
) involves
1-\det\nabla\Phi_{q}=\det\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q}-\det\nabla\Phi_{q}

valid in view of the incompressibility of the flow; therefore quadracity of
\det
gives
1-\det\nabla\Phi_{q}\sim(\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q}-\nabla\Phi_{q})(\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q}+\nabla\Phi_{q})
. Or, performing the computations, we have

\displaystyle 1-\det\nabla\Phi_{q}=

\displaystyle\det\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q}-\det\nabla\Phi_{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\bar{\Phi}^{1}_{q}\partial_{x_{2}}\bar{\Phi}^{2}_{q}-\partial_{x_{1}}\bar{\Phi}^{2}_{q}\partial_{x_{2}}\bar{\Phi}^{1}_{q}-(\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi^{1}_{q}\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi^{2}_{q}-\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi^{2}_{q}\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi^{1}_{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{x_{1}}\bar{\Phi}^{1}_{q}-\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi^{1}_{q})\partial_{x_{2}}\bar{\Phi}^{2}_{q}+\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi^{1}_{q}(\partial_{x_{2}}\bar{\Phi}^{2}_{q}-\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi^{2}_{q})

\displaystyle+(\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi^{2}_{q}-\partial_{x_{1}}\bar{\Phi}^{2}_{q})\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi^{1}_{q}+\partial_{x_{1}}\bar{\Phi}^{1}_{q}(\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi^{1}_{q}-\partial_{x_{2}}\bar{\Phi}^{1}_{q})

Therefore, invoking (
3.3.8
) and product rule/Remark
3.17
we have for any
|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq Y-N_{*}-1

\|D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}(\det\nabla\bar{\Phi}_{q}-\det\nabla\Phi_{q})\|_{\infty}\lesssim\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{t}}.

(3.3.11)

This, with the estimate

\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}

given by (
3.0.16
)
yields for
I
of (
3.3.10
), again via product rule

\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}I\|_{\infty}\lesssim\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}

(3.3.12)

for any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y-N_{*}-1
.

The term
II
of r.h.s. of (
3.3.10
) involves difference
H_{12}(\xi,\tau)-H_{12}(\xi^{\prime},\tau)
. In view of the definition of
H_{12}
, see
(
2.3.7
), (
2.3.8
), we have

H_{12}(\xi,\tau)-H_{12}(\xi^{\prime},\tau)=\eta_{1}(\tau)\left(\sin(2\pi\xi_{1})-\sin(2\pi\xi^{\prime}_{1})\right)+\eta_{2}(\tau)\left(\sin(2\pi\xi_{2})-\sin(2\pi\xi^{\prime}_{2})\right)

since the two summands above have identical structure, we suppress
i=1,2
and write, with
f(\cdot)=\sin(2\pi\cdot)
,

H_{12}(\xi,\tau)-H_{12}(\xi^{\prime},\tau)\sim\eta(\tau)\left(f(\xi_{1})-f(\xi^{\prime}_{1})\right)

so

\displaystyle d(x,t)

\displaystyle=H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)-H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t))

\displaystyle\sim\eta(\mu_{q+1}t)f(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t))-f(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t))

The estimates of
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
of the above quantity follow now the already known path. It may be sufficient to realise that the derivatives of outer functions
\sin(\pi\cdot)
,
\cos(\pi\cdot)
play no role in estimates with restricted number of derivatives (
\leq Y
). More precisely, all the simplifying observations (i)-(iv) of section
3.2.5
hold also currently. Following the lines of section
3.2.5
we use (
3.2.34
) with
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}(x,t)
replaced with our
d(x,t)
, and
\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}\|_{\infty}
replaced with
\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}}(\Phi_{q}-\bar{\Phi}_{q})\|_{\infty}
, whose estimates we already know thanks to (
3.3.3
). This means that as we obtained (
3.2.35
) we have now

|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)-H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)\right)|\lesssim_{Y}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\mu^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}_{q+1}\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}_{q+1}

so

\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}|D_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)-H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)\right)|\lesssim_{Y}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\mu^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}_{q+1}\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}_{q+1}

(3.3.13)

Observe that (
3.3.13
) is consistent with (
3.2.35
), i.e. it is the smallness
\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}
(following from the proximity between exact and approximate flows
\bar{\Phi}_{q}
,
\Phi_{q}
) times r.h.s. of (
3.2.35
).

The shift to estimates for fast derivatives
D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
is now identical to section
3.2.6
. Now, instead of starting with (
3.2.35
) we start with (
3.3.13
) and obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}

\displaystyle|D_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)-H_{12}(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{\Phi}_{q}(x,t),\mu_{q+1}t)\right)|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{Y}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}.

This estimate, together with (
3.3.7
) yields

\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}II\|_{\infty}\lesssim\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}.

(3.3.14)

The two estimates (
3.3.12
), (
3.3.14
) in (
3.3.10
) give the wanted (
3.0.19
) for
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
derivatives. (As before, for any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y-N_{*}-1
, since the known derivatives of approximate flow
\Phi_{q}
are restricted to
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y-N_{*}
, and zeroth-order formula (
3.3.10
) involves first derivative
\Phi_{q}
.)

3.3.5.
Estimates for
O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
derivatives

The only difference between

O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
derivatives and
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
derivatives lies in the flow involved, since in view of their definition (
2.3.15
)

D_{q+1}=(D_{q}+\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla)\quad\text{ whereas }\quad O_{q+1}=(D_{q}+\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1}\cdot\nabla).

(3.3.15)

At this stage of the proof we have full information on estimates for
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}\psi_{q+1}
, namely

\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}\psi_{q+1}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}\qquad\text{for any }|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y-N_{*}-1.

This estimate is consistent with the estimate (
3.2.37
) for
\psi_{q+1}
used in section
3.2.6
to deal with
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
. Therefore, following the lines of the proof from section
3.2.6
onwards, we have the inequalities (
3.0.17
) (
3.0.18
) (
3.0.19
) for
O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
. Concerning the admissible highest order of derivatives, observe that the highest possible purely space derivative is of order
Y-N_{*}-1
, because for pure space derivatives
O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}=D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
, and the highest possible mixed derivative (i.e. with at least one advective derivative) is also of order
Y-N_{*}-1
, because as before there is no loss of total number of derivatives in this case, as one sees from (
3.3.15
).

3.3.6.
Estimates for
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
derivatives

In view of (
3.3.15
)

(D_{q+1}-O_{q+1})f=(\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}-\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1})\cdot\nabla f.

(3.3.16)

Let us make the following observations:

(i) The cost of derivative of
(\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}-\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1})
is

\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}
for an advective derivative (no matter if it is
O_{q+1}
or
D_{q+1}
derivative) and
\lambda_{q+1}
for space derivative, and the amplitude of
(\nabla^{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{q+1}-\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1})
is
\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}
, all thanks to (
3.0.19
). This means that
D_{q+1}-O_{q+1}
applied to any
f
with amplitude
M
and

\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}
cost for an advective derivative and
\lambda_{q+1}
cost for space derivative, results in a function with amplitude
\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}M\lambda_{q+1}
and

\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}
cost for an advective derivative and
\lambda_{q+1}
cost for space derivative.

(ii) Functions
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
,
\psi_{q+1}
(or any of their derivatives) satisfy the conditions for
f
of (i), thanks to (
3.0.18
), with amplitude
M=\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}
(and appropriately for derivatives).

(iii) It holds (for
D=D_{q+1}
,
O=O_{q+1}
)

(D^{m}-O^{m})=(D-O)D^{m-1}+O(D-O)D^{m-2}+O^{2}(D-O)D^{m-3}+\dots+O^{m-1}(D-O)

(3.3.17)

(iv) The observations (i)-(iii) imply in particular that
(D_{q+1}^{m}-O_{q+1}^{m})\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
or
(D_{q+1}^{m}-O_{q+1}^{m})\psi_{q+1}
is a function with amplitude
m\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sim\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\delta_{q+1}
,
with
\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}
cost of an advective derivative and
\lambda_{q+1}
cost for space derivative. (The combinatorial factor
m
is negligible, since in the estimates we have
\lesssim_{Y}
, with
Y
being the highest possible derivative order.)

(v) Analogously to (iv), using (ii) for derivatives, we have that

\displaystyle(D_{q+1}^{m}-O_{q+1}^{m})D^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1}\bar{\psi}_{q+1},

\displaystyle(D_{q+1}^{m}-O_{q+1}^{m})D^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1}\psi_{q+1},

\displaystyle(D_{q+1}^{m}-O_{q+1}^{m})O^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1}\bar{\psi}_{q+1},

\displaystyle(D_{q+1}^{m}-O_{q+1}^{m})O^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1}\psi_{q+1}

are functions with amplitude
\sim\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\delta_{q+1}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}}
and the costs of derivatives as before.

(vi) Furthermore, any
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
consists of blocks of advective derivatives and of space derivatives, for instance

\displaystyle D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}

\displaystyle=D^{n_{1}}_{q+1}\nabla^{m_{1}}..D^{n_{k}}_{q+1}-O^{n_{1}}_{q+1}\nabla^{m_{1}}..O^{n_{k}}_{q+1}

(3.3.18)

\displaystyle=D^{n_{1}}_{q+1}\nabla^{m_{1}}..(D^{n_{k}}_{q+1}-O^{n_{k}}_{q+1})+\dots+(D^{n_{1}}_{q+1}-O^{n_{1}}_{q+1})\nabla^{m_{1}}..O^{n_{k}}_{q+1}.

Using observation (v) in (
3.3.18
) gives that

(D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1})\bar{\psi}_{q+1},\quad(D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1})\psi_{q+1}

are functions with amplitude
\sim\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\delta_{q+1}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}
and their costs of derivatives as before. This justifies the wanted estimate (
3.0.20
).

Using again (v) and reasoning along (vi) shows that, denoting by
\Theta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
either
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
or
O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
, the symbol

(D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}}_{q+1})\Theta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}}_{q+1}(D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{3}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{3}}_{q+1})\dots\Theta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}}_{q+1}

(3.3.19)

acting on either
\bar{\psi}_{q+1}
or on
\psi_{q+1}
yield functions with

\text{amplitude }\quad\sim\lambda^{-\gamma N_{*}+1}_{q+1}\delta_{q+1}(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}

and the costs of derivatives as before, i.e.
\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}
is cost of an advective derivative and
\lambda_{q+1}
is cost for a space derivative. Of course, the only requirement for the symbol of type (
3.3.19
) is that it has at least one block of
(D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}}_{q+1})
, its outermost and the innermost part may be any of
(D^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1})
,
\Theta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
. Observe also that we do not claim further gain from more than one block of type
(D^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1})
appearing, so a brutal estimate replacing all but one blocks
(D^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{q+1})
with the respective sum suffices.

The upper bound of number of admissible derivatives is dictated by what is available from (
3.0.17
) and (
3.0.18
), which is
Y-N_{*}-1
(and there is no further loss of derivatives, as before in the case of mixed derivatives; indeed otherwise, i.e. in case
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
are only pure space derivatives,
(D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}-O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1})=0
.) We have reached (
3.0.22
).

Replacing observation (ii) with:

(ii’) Any derivative of
\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
or of
\Psi_{q+1}
satisfies the conditions for
f
of (i), thanks to (
3.0.17
) with (lossy) amplitude
M=1
for (zeroth-order)
\bar{\Psi}_{q+1}
or
\Psi_{q+1}
and appropriately for derivatives.

We obtain (
3.0.21
), (
3.0.23
) along the preceding lines.

In fact, as already seen from the original observation (ii), for any smooth function
f
such that

\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}f\|_{\infty}+\|O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}f\|_{\infty}\lesssim M(\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lambda_{q+1}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}\qquad\text{for any }|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq Y-N_{*}-n_{0}.

(3.3.20)

we have the same conclusions, thus Corollary
3.5
holds, under the weaker assumption that we have control on both
D
and
O
derivatives, i.e. taking (
3.3.20
) in place of the ’either-or’ of (
3.0.24
), (
3.0.25
).

In order to upgrade from assuming (
3.3.20
) to using merely ’either-or’ of Corollary
3.5
, we proceed as follows. Assume we know only the behavior of
D
-derivatives, which means that we assume only (
3.0.24
) (the other case is symmetric). But this assumption already contains knowledge of pure space derivatives, so we can bound

(D_{q+1}-O_{q+1})f
in view of the identity (
3.3.16
). Analogously we can bound
(D_{q+1}-O_{q+1})^{m}f
up to maximal order allowed by the assumption. But (with
D=D_{q+1}
,
O=O_{q+1}
)

D^{2}-O^{2}=(D-O)D+\underbrace{O(D-O)}_{=D(D-O)-(D-O)^{2}}

so we have also appropriate bound on
D^{2}-O^{2}
, consequently on
O^{2}
. Repeating this reasoning inductively for higher-order terms we see that one recovers the lacking estimate (
3.0.25
)
on
O^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{q+1}
. From this point onwards we have all the assumptions of Corollary
3.5
.

4.
Decomposition lemmas

As we see in Section
2.4
, the vector fields at step
q
generate oscillatory functions documented by polynomial-derivative space
\mathcal{P}_{q}
and
\mathcal{O}_{q}
at step
q
. In this section, we quantitatively decompose the elements in
\mathcal{O}_{q+1}
into products of elements in
\mathcal{P}_{q}
and known oscillatory profiles.

4.1.
Statements of the decomposition lemmas

From (
3.0.6
) and (
3.0.7
), we have

\displaystyle D_{q,t}\Phi_{q}(x,t)=

\displaystyle\,\omega_{q,N}(x,t)\phi_{N}(\mu_{q+1}t)+\sum_{m=1}^{N}\omega_{q,m}(x,t)z_{m}(\mu_{q+1}t).

(4.1.1)

For any
\eta\in\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}\eta\subset\operatorname{supp}\eta_{1}\text{ or }\operatorname{supp}\eta\subset\operatorname{supp}\eta_{2},

(4.1.2)

we have

\displaystyle\eta(\mu_{q+1}t)D_{q,t}\Phi_{q}(x,t)=

\displaystyle\,\omega_{q,N}(x,t)\phi_{N}(\mu_{q+1}t)\eta(\mu_{q+1}t).

(4.1.3)

Now we can state the decomposition lemmas.

Lemma 4.1
.

For any
\alpha\in\mathcal{I}
with
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq 2NQ
, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Psi_{q+1}\Bigg)=g_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{*}}\ddot{\chi}_{k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\ddot{\eta}_{k}(\mu_{q+1}t)g_{k}

(4.1.4)

for some
k_{*}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\ddot{\chi}_{k}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
\ddot{\eta}_{k}:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
g_{k}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1])
with

\displaystyle k_{*}\leq

\displaystyle\,2N(3N^{2})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|},

(4.1.5)

\displaystyle\max_{k\geq 1}\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\ddot{\chi}_{k}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3},

(4.1.6)

\displaystyle\max_{k\geq 1}\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\ddot{\eta}_{k}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1.\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}.

(4.1.7)

For any
k
,
(\ddot{\chi}_{k},\ddot{\eta}_{k})
satisfies the generalized shear condition. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle g_{k}

\displaystyle\,\in\mathcal{P}_{q}(3N(|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+1)),

(4.1.8)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim r_{0}(|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}),

(4.1.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{k}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim r_{1}(|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}),

(4.1.10)

with some
\{r_{0}(i,j)\}_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}
,
\{r_{1}(i,j)\}_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle r_{1}(0,0)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{2(1+\gamma_{R})}},\quad r_{0}(0,0)=\frac{\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q}}\frac{\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}},

(4.1.11)

\displaystyle r_{1}(i+1,j)\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}r_{1}(i,j),\quad r_{0}(i+1,j)\leq\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}r_{0}(i,j),

(4.1.12)

\displaystyle r_{1}(i,j+1)\leq

\displaystyle\,\max\bigg\{\frac{\mu_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}},\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\bigg\}r_{1}(i,j)+\max\bigg\{\frac{\mathring{\mu}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}},\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\bigg\}r_{0}(i,j),

(4.1.13)

\displaystyle r_{0}(i,j+1)\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\mu}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}r_{0}(i,j).

(4.1.14)

Lemma 4.2
.

For any
\chi:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, any
\eta:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and any
\rho:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq p_{0},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq p_{0}

for some
p_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
. Define

\displaystyle g(x,t)=h(x,t)\chi\big(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q}(x,t)\big)\eta(\mu_{q+1}t)\rho(x,t).

(4.1.15)

Suppose
(\chi,\eta)
satisfies generalized shear condition and
h\in\mathcal{P}_{q}(Q)
. Then we have the following decomposition for any
\alpha\in\mathcal{I}
with
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq 3Q

\displaystyle\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}g=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{k=1}^{\Gamma^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}}h_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\chi_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}(\mu_{q+1}t)

with

\displaystyle h_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}

\displaystyle\,\in\mathcal{P}_{q}\big(Q+2(N+2)|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\big),

(4.1.16)

\displaystyle\sum_{k,\boldsymbol{\omega}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}\bigg(\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}

(4.1.17)

and

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}

\displaystyle\,\leq(3N^{2})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|},

(4.1.18)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq p_{0}-|\boldsymbol{\omega}|,

(4.1.19)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1.\quad p\leq p_{0}-|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{t}.

(4.1.20)

Moreover,
(\chi_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})},\eta_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})})
satisfies the generalized shear condition for any
k
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}
. Furthermore, if we assume
\chi
is constant, then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k,\boldsymbol{\omega}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}\bigg(\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}.

(4.1.21)

The proofs of the above two lemmas are postponed to the end of this section.

Corollary 4.3
.

For any
g\in\mathcal{O}_{q+1}(2NQ)
, we have the following decomposition

\displaystyle g=g_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{*}}\ddot{\chi}_{k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\ddot{\eta}_{k}(\mu_{q+1}t)g_{k}

(4.1.22)

with

\displaystyle g_{k}\in\mathcal{P}_{q}(9N^{2}Q),\quad\sum_{k=0}^{k_{*}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{k}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q+1}

(4.1.23)

and

\displaystyle k_{*}

\displaystyle\,\leq(10N)^{8NQ},

(4.1.24)

\displaystyle\max_{k}\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\ddot{\chi}_{k}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\leq 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3},

(4.1.25)

\displaystyle\max_{k}\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\ddot{\eta}_{k}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\leq 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}.

(4.1.26)

Moreover,
(\ddot{\chi}_{k},\ddot{\eta}_{k})
satisfies the generalized shear condition for any
k
.

Proof.

If
g
is a constant, all statements are trivial. Assume
d_{*}\leq 2NQ
. For any
g\in\mathcal{O}_{q+1}(d_{*})
that are not constant, we consider the representation of
g
that attains
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q+1}
, i.e.

\displaystyle g=\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}a_{k}\prod_{i=1}^{I_{k}}\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,i}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Psi_{q+1}\Bigg),\quad\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}|a_{k}|={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q+1}.

(4.1.27)

Without loss of generality, we also assume
k_{0}\geq 1
,
a_{k}\neq 0
and
I_{k}\geq 1
for any
k
.

For
1\leq d\leq d_{*}
, we define

\displaystyle\Lambda(d):=

\displaystyle\,\bigg\{1\leq k\leq k_{0}\,\bigg|\,\sum_{i}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,i}|=d\bigg\}.

(4.1.28)

From elementary combinatorics, we have the following estimate on the cardinality of
\Lambda(d)

\displaystyle|\Lambda(d)|\leq 6^{d}.

(4.1.29)

For a fixed
d
, we apply Lemma
4.1
to those
k\in\Lambda(d)
.

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{I_{k}}\mathring{D}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,i}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Psi_{q+1}\Bigg)=

\displaystyle\,\prod_{i=1}^{I_{k}}\Bigg(g_{k,i,0}+\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k,i}}\ddot{\chi}_{k,i,j}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\ddot{\eta}_{k,i,j}(\mu_{q+1}t)g_{k,i,j}\Bigg)

(4.1.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,g_{k,0}+\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\ddot{\chi}_{k,j}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\ddot{\eta}_{k,j}(\mu_{q+1}t)g_{k,j}

(4.1.31)

From (
4.1.30
) to (
4.1.31
), we just expand the product in
i
, then we rewrite and renumber the product in the form of (
4.1.31
). From (
4.1.5
), we have the following estimate for
k\in\Lambda(d)

\displaystyle 1+J_{k}\leq\prod_{i=1}^{I_{k}}(1+J_{k,i})\leq(3N^{2})^{\sum_{i=1}^{I_{k}}(|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,i}|+1)}\leq(3N^{2})^{2d}.

(4.1.32)

From Lemma
4.1
, we also have

\displaystyle\max_{j}\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\ddot{\chi}_{k,i,j}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3},

(4.1.33)

\displaystyle\max_{j}\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\ddot{\eta}_{k,i,j}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}

(4.1.34)

and

\displaystyle g_{k,i,j}\in

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{P}_{q}\big(3N(|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,i}|+1)\big),

(4.1.35)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{J_{k,i}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{k,i,j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\gamma_{R}}\bigg(\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,i}|_{x}-2}\bigg(\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,i}|_{t}}.

(4.1.36)

Note that
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,i}\in\mathcal{I}(2,0)\cup\mathcal{I}(1,N_{*})
from (
2.4.4
). Using
N\gamma_{I}>2
from (
2.1.6
) and (
2.1.14
), we absorb the constants and deduce

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{J_{k,i}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{k,i,j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\frac{\gamma_{R}}{2}},\quad\prod_{i=1}^{I_{k}}\sum_{j=0}^{J_{k,i}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{k,i,j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\frac{I_{k}\gamma_{R}}{2}}.

(4.1.37)

Then from the product rules, (
4.1.33
) and (
4.1.34
), we have

\displaystyle\max_{j}\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\ddot{\chi}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3},

\displaystyle\max_{j}\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\ddot{\eta}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}.

From Remark
2.18
and (
4.1.37
), we have for
k\in\Lambda(d)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{J_{k}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{k,j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\lesssim\prod_{i=1}^{I_{k}}\sum_{j=0}^{J_{k,i}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{k,i,j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\frac{I_{k}\gamma_{R}}{2}}.

(4.1.38)

Now we sum up (
4.1.31
) in
k
. With slight abuse of notations, we renumber all terms in
k
and
j
in a single index, again denoted by
1\leq k\leq k_{*}
, to arrive at the form (
4.1.22
). From (
4.1.29
) and (
4.1.32
), the cardinality satisfies the estimate which verifies (
4.1.24
),

\displaystyle k_{*}\leq\sum_{d=1}^{d_{*}}\sum_{k\in\Lambda(d)}(3N^{2})^{2d}\leq\sum_{d=1}^{d_{*}}(9N)^{4d}\leq(10N)^{4d_{*}}.

Also, (
4.1.38
) yields the following estimate verifying (
4.1.23
),

\displaystyle\sum_{k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{k}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\lesssim|a_{0}|+\sum_{d=1}^{d_{*}}\sum_{k\in\Lambda(d)}|a_{k}|\sum_{j=0}^{J_{k}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{k,j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\lesssim{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q+1}.

(4.1.39)

From (
4.1.35
) and Remark
2.18
, we have

\displaystyle g_{k}\in\mathcal{P}_{q}\bigg(3N\bigg(I_{k}+\sup_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{I_{k}}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,i}|\bigg)\bigg).

(4.1.40)

Since
g\in\mathcal{O}_{q+1}(2NQ)
, we have
I_{k}\leq NQ
. Then (
4.1.23
) follows from (
4.1.39
) and (
4.1.40
).
∎

4.2.
Proof of Lemma
4.1
and Lemma
4.2

In the rest of this section, we prove Lemma
4.1
and Lemma
4.2
. The proofs are similar inductive arguments.

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

All the indices
m,m_{1},m_{2}
are summed from
1
to
N
. When
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Psi_{q+1}=\frac{\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q}}\frac{\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}}{\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\Bigg)+\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\psi_{q+1}

and

\displaystyle\psi_{q+1}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\det\nabla\Phi_{q}\Big(\eta_{1}(\mu_{q+1}t)\Pi_{1}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})+\eta_{2}(\mu_{q+1}t)\Pi_{2}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\sum_{m}\varpi_{q,m}\zeta_{m}(\mu_{q+1}t)\Big(\eta_{1}(\mu_{q+1}t)\Pi_{1}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})+\eta_{2}(\mu_{q+1}t)\Pi_{2}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\sum_{m}\varpi_{q,m}\Big([\eta_{1}\zeta_{m}](\mu_{q+1}t)\Pi_{1}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})+[\eta_{2}\zeta_{m}](\mu_{q+1}t)\Pi_{2}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\Big).

(4.2.1)

Then we renumber and write the terms in (
4.2.1
) in the form of (
4.1.4
), giving

\displaystyle\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Psi_{q+1}=

\displaystyle\,g_{0,0}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}g_{0,k}\chi_{0,k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{0,k}(\mu_{q+1}t),

\displaystyle g_{0,0}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q}}\frac{\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}}{\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\bar{\Psi}_{q}\bigg),

(4.2.2)

\displaystyle g_{0,m}=g_{0,N+m}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{2(1+\gamma_{R})}}\varpi_{q,m},\quad\text{for any }1\leq m\leq N,

(4.2.3)

with
k_{0}=2N
and
\chi_{0,k},\eta_{0,k}
given by (
4.2.1
). From Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{k\geq 1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{0,k}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\leq\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{1+\gamma_{R}}}\Bigg)^{2}.

From (
4.2.2
), we also have

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{0,0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\leq\frac{\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q}}\frac{\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}.

This verifies the statements when
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=0
. In particular, (
4.1.6
) follows from (
2.3.8
). (
4.1.7
) follows from Lemma
2.3
and (
3.0.10
). (
4.1.8
-
4.1.11
) follow from (
3.0.8
), (
3.0.11
) and Remark
2.18
.

Let
n:=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|
. Assume that for some
k_{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Psi_{q+1}\Bigg)=g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\mu_{q+1}t),

(4.2.4)

And we assume (
4.1.5
-
4.1.10
) for
\boldsymbol{\beta}
. From such inductive assumptions, our goal is to derive the information on

\displaystyle\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Psi_{q+1}\Bigg)=g_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},0}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n+1}}g_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},k}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},k}(\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.5)

for some
k_{n+1}\in\mathbb{N}
.

We consider the two cases:
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=1\boldsymbol{\beta}
or
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=t\boldsymbol{\beta}
. The case of
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=2\boldsymbol{\beta}
is completely symmetric to
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=1\boldsymbol{\beta}
.

Case 4.2.1
.

We consider the case
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=1\boldsymbol{\beta}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\partial}_{q+1,1}\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\frac{1}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\partial_{1}\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}.

(4.2.6)

Recalling the notion of renormalized derivatives in Definition
2.6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Psi_{q+1}\Bigg)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\partial_{1}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\mu_{q+1}t)+\frac{1}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\partial_{1}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\mu_{q+1}t)\mathring{\partial}_{q,1}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}+\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\mathring{\partial}_{q,1}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}\Big[\mathrm{Id}+\varepsilon_{q}\sum_{m}B_{q,m}\phi_{m}(\mu_{q+1}t)\Big]_{i1}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\mu_{q+1}t)g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\mu_{q+1}t)\mathring{\partial}_{q,1}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}+\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\mathring{\partial}_{q,1}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}

(4.2.7)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\varepsilon_{q}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}\sum_{m}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})[\phi_{m}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}](\mu_{q+1}t)[B_{q,m}]_{i1}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}

(4.2.8)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}\partial_{\xi_{1}}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\mu_{q+1}t)g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}.

(4.2.9)

Here, we use (
3.0.1
) for computing
\nabla\Phi_{q}
.

Now we renumber the contributions from (
4.2.7
-
4.2.9
), in the form of (
4.2.5
). Then

\displaystyle k_{n+1}\leq(N+2)k_{n},

(4.2.10)

which verifies (
4.1.5
) for
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
. We derive the following information for any
p\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\max_{k\geq 1}\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},k}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\max_{k\geq 1,m}\big\{\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}\|_{\infty},\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}(\phi_{m}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k})\|_{\infty}\big\},

(4.2.11)

\displaystyle\max_{k\geq 1}\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},k}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\max_{k\geq 1}\big\{\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}\|_{\infty},\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p+1}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}\|_{\infty}\big\},

(4.2.12)

which verifies (
4.1.6
), (
4.1.7
) for
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
.

We also have

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathring{\partial}_{q,1}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q},

(4.2.13)

\displaystyle\max_{k\geq 1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},k}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\leq

\displaystyle\,\max_{k\geq 1,m}\bigg\{\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathring{\partial}_{q,1}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q},\frac{\varepsilon_{q}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B_{q,m}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q},\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\bigg\},

(4.2.14)

verifying (

), (

) and (

) for

) holds for
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
. Here, we use Remark

, (

) and (

Case 4.2.2
.

We consider the case
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=t\boldsymbol{\beta}
, then

\begin{split}\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=&\,\mathring{O}_{q+1,t}\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}^{-1}O_{q+1,t}\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\\
=&\,\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}^{-1}D_{q,t}\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}^{-1}\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}.\end{split}

(4.2.15)

Then we can compute

\displaystyle\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1}^{2}}{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+2\gamma_{R}}}\Psi_{q+1}\Bigg)=

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}^{-1}D_{q,t}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.16)

\displaystyle+

(4.2.17)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}^{-1}D_{q,t}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}+\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}^{-1}\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1}\cdot\nabla g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}

\displaystyle=

(4.2.24)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}+J_{4}+J_{5}+J_{6}+J_{7}.

We also compute

\begin{split}\nabla\psi_{q+1}=&\,\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\sum_{m}\nabla\varpi_{q,m}\zeta_{m}(\mu_{q+1}t)\Big(\eta_{1}(\mu_{q+1}t)\Pi_{1}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})+\eta_{2}(\mu_{q+1}t)\Pi_{2}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\Big)\\
