+&\,\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\eta_{1}(\mu_{q+1}t)\nabla_{\xi}\Pi_{1}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\\
+&\,\text{symmetric terms concering }\eta_{2},\Pi_{2}\end{split}

(4.2.25)

The contributions from (

), (

(4.2.26)

\displaystyle J_{3}=

(4.2.27)

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\mu}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\mathring{D}_{q,t}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}.

(4.2.28)

Using (
4.1.1
), the contribution from (

\displaystyle J_{2}=\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}\omega_{q,N}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\mu_{q+1}t)\phi_{N}(\mu_{q+1}t)g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}.

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}\lambda_{q+1}}\sum_{m}[\eta_{1}\zeta_{m}](\mu_{q+1}t)\Pi_{1}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{\perp}\varpi_{q,m}\cdot\mathring{\nabla}_{q}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}

(4.2.30)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\varepsilon_{q}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{m}[\eta_{1}\varphi_{m}](\mu_{q+1}t)\nabla_{\xi}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\cdot\big(\operatorname{adj}E_{q,m}^{T}\mathring{\nabla}_{q}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}\big)

(4.2.31)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\eta_{1}(\mu_{q+1}t)\nabla_{\xi}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\cdot\mathring{\nabla}_{q}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},0}

(4.2.32)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\text{symmetric terms concering }\eta_{2},\Pi_{2}

(4.2.33)

Using (
4.2.25
), the contribution from (
4.2.22
) is

\displaystyle J_{4}=

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1}\cdot\nabla g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}(\mu_{q+1}t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}\lambda_{q+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}\sum_{m}[\eta_{1}\zeta_{m}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}](\mu_{q+1}t)[\Pi_{1}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{\perp}\varpi_{q,m}\cdot\mathring{\nabla}_{q}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}

(4.2.34)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{n}}[\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}](\mu_{q+1}t)[\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\mathring{\partial}_{q,1}g_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

We prove this by induction. For
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=0
, the decomposition is (
4.1.15
) itself.

. Assume we have, for some

\displaystyle\mathring{O}_{q+1}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\Big(h\chi(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta(\mu_{q+1}t)\rho\Big)=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{k=1}^{\Gamma(n,|\boldsymbol{\omega}|)}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}(\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}(\mu_{q+1}t).

-

) for

-

for some

(4.2.72)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{\nabla}_{q}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\cdot\sum_{k}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big[\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\nabla_{\xi}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.73)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{k}\mathring{\nabla}_{q}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\cdot\big[\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\nabla_{\xi}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.74)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\varepsilon_{q}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{\partial}_{q,j}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{m,k}[E_{q,m}]_{ji}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big[\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.75)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\varepsilon_{q}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{m,k}[E_{q,m}]_{ji}\mathring{\partial}_{q,j}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big[\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\partial_{\xi_{i}}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.76)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\text{symmetric terms concering }\eta_{2},\Pi_{2}

(4.2.77)

and

\displaystyle J_{2}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{m,k}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{\perp}\varpi_{q,m}\cdot\big[\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\Pi_{1}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\zeta_{m}\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.78)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\varepsilon_{q}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},k}\mathring{\partial}_{q,j}^{\perp}\varpi_{q,m_{1}}[B_{q,m_{2}}]_{ij}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big[\Pi_{1}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\zeta_{m_{1}}\phi_{m_{2}}\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.79)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{k}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big[\nabla_{\xi}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.80)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\varepsilon_{q}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{m,k}[B_{q,m}]_{ij}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big[\partial_{\xi_{i}}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\partial_{\xi_{j}}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\phi_{m}\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.81)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\varepsilon_{q}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{m,k}[E_{q,m}]_{ij}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big[\partial_{\xi_{i}}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\partial_{\xi_{j}}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\varphi_{m}\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.82)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\varepsilon_{q}^{2}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\leq n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},k}[B_{q,m_{2}}E_{q,m_{1}}]_{ij}h_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big[\partial_{\xi_{i}}\chi_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\partial_{\xi_{j}}^{\perp}\Pi_{1}\big](\lambda_{q+1}\Phi_{q})\big[\varphi_{m_{1}}\phi_{m_{2}}\eta_{1}\eta_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}^{(\boldsymbol{\omega})}\big](\mu_{q+1}t)

(4.2.83)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\text{symmetric terms concerning }\eta_{2},\Pi_{2}.

(4.2.84)

For the contributions from (
4.2.66
-
4.2.84
), we have

\displaystyle r(n+1,|\boldsymbol{\omega}|)\lesssim\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}r(n,|\boldsymbol{\omega}|),+\frac{\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}+\mathring{\mu}_{q}}{\mathring{\mu}_{q+1}}r(n,|\boldsymbol{\omega}|-1).

(4.2.85)

Also, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma(n+1,|\boldsymbol{\omega}|)\leq 3N^{2}\Gamma(n,|\boldsymbol{\omega}|)+4N\Gamma(n,|\boldsymbol{\omega}|-1)

(4.2.86)

From (
4.2.63
), (
4.2.64
), (
4.2.85
), (
4.2.86
), we deduce (
4.1.18
) and (
4.1.17
). (
4.1.16
), (
4.1.19
) and (
4.1.20
) are proved in the same way as Lemma
4.1
.

When
\chi
is a constant function, we do not get fast spatial scale
\lambda_{q+1}
. Therefore, (
4.1.21
) is a direct consequence of this observation.
∎

5.
Energy-type estimates

As before, we will write
D_{u}=\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla
. By

D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
(
D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
etc) we denote generalised derivative, it has
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}
-order of space derivatives and it has
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}
-many advective derivatives
D_{u}
, all arranged according to
\boldsymbol{\beta}
.

Consider

\begin{split}D_{u}\rho-\kappa\Delta\rho=&\operatorname{div}f,\\
\rho(\cdot,0)=&\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{split}

(5.0.1)

In this section we provide energy-type estimates for
D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho
.

5.1.
Scales assumption and how to apply them

We make the following ’scales assumption’ on the drift
u
:

Assumption 5.1
.

There are three real numbers
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mathcal{T}
larger than
1
, such that for any
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq 2U
, where
U
is a fixed natural number, it holds

\left(\frac{\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{2}\leq\mathcal{A}\mathcal{S}\mathcal{S}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}

(5.1.1)

One may think of
\mathcal{A}\mathcal{S}
above as ’amplitude’,
\mathcal{S}
as ’cost of space derivative’, and
\mathcal{T}
as ’cost of advective derivative’.

The general results of this section, i.e. Propositions
5.4
,
5.15
will specified in section
5.4
to the setting of the rest of this paper. More precisely, in the rest of the paper (other chapters), we invoke the energy-type estimates for
u:=u_{q}
,
\kappa:=\kappa_{q}
and with:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle=C_{U}

(5.1.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle=\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\kappa^{-1}_{q}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle=(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{2}

5.1.1.
Consistency of our scales assumptions

The introduced scales assumption are consistent with the rest of the paper, in view of

Lemma 5.2
.

The velocities
u_{q}
,
v_{q}
of Section
3
enjoy Assumption
5.1
with the choice (
5.1.2
), with
U=\lfloor\frac{Y-N_{*}-1}{2}\rfloor
.

Proof.

In view of (
5.1.2
), (
5.1.1
) is

\left(\frac{\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{2}\leq C_{U}\left(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\kappa^{-1}_{q}\right)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+1}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{2|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}

(5.1.3)

Estimates of Section
3
yield the upper bound

\left(\frac{\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{2}\lesssim(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}_{q}\kappa^{-1}_{q})^{2}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{2|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}

(5.1.4)

which says that with choices (
5.1.2
) in order to have (
5.1.1
) or equivalently (
5.1.3
), it suffices to have

(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}}_{q}\kappa^{-1}_{q})^{2}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{2|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\lesssim\left(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\kappa^{-1}_{q}\right)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+1}(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{2|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}

(5.1.5)

which is, taking into account definitions of
\delta_{q}
and
\kappa_{q}

\lambda^{-\beta}_{q}\lambda_{q}\lambda^{\theta}_{q}\geq(\lambda^{-\beta}_{q}\lambda^{n}_{q}\lambda^{\theta}_{q})^{\frac{2}{n+1}}

this last inequality holds iff
\theta\geq\beta+1
.
This, in view of (
2.1.4
) which defines
\theta=\frac{2b}{b+1}(1+\beta)
, is always satisfied.
∎

Remark 5.3

(Informal version of Assumption
5.1
with the choice (
5.1.2
))
.

Observe that the assumption (
5.1.1
) with the choice (
5.1.2
) can be informally stated
as

\left(\frac{\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{2}\leq\left(\frac{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+1}\|\nabla u\|^{2|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}_{L^{\infty}},

(5.1.6)

because the equivalent form (
5.1.3
) of (
5.1.1
) has the right hand side of (
5.1.6
), if one
imagines that the estimate for
\|\nabla u_{q}\|_{L^{\infty}}
is saturated, i.e. that
\|\nabla u_{q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\sim\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}
.

5.2.
Notations of this section

To state our estimates and to proceed efficiently with the proof, introduce the following shorthand notations, for:

(i)dissipation

\mathcal{D}_{\rho}:=\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

(5.2.1)

(ii) ’initial datum’:

ID_{n}:=\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

(5.2.2)

(iii) ’initial datum and forcing’:

\displaystyle IDF_{n}=\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

(5.2.3)

(iv) ’Leray-Hopf quantity of function
h
’

LH_{h}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}):=\sup_{t\leq T}\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}h(t)\|^{2}_{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla h(t)\|^{2}_{2}dt

(5.2.4)

(v) ’maximum of Leray-Hopf quantities with a certain order of derivatives’

LH_{h}(l_{x},l_{t}):=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}=l_{x},\\
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=l_{t}\end{subarray}}LH_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta})

(5.2.5)

(vi) renormalised version of Leray-Hopf quantities

{\mathring{LH}_{h}}(l_{x},l_{t}):=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}=l_{x},\\
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=l_{t}\end{subarray}}{\mathring{LH}_{h}}(\boldsymbol{\beta})=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}=l_{x},\\
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=l_{t}\end{subarray}}\left(\sup_{t\leq T}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}h(t)\|^{2}_{2}+\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}_{q}h(t)\|^{2}_{2}dt\right).

(5.2.6)

5.3.
General energy-type estimates

The main result of this section, which requires of scales merely Assumption
5.1
, reads

Proposition 5.4
.

Take smooth, divergence free
u
, smooth forcing
f
and datum
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}
. If the scales Assumption
5.1
holds, then the smooth solution of (
5.0.1
) satisfies:

(I) For
n\geq 1
,
n\leq 2U

\sup_{t\leq T}\|\nabla^{n}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\lesssim_{n}\mathcal{A}^{n}\mathcal{S}^{n}\mathcal{D}_{\rho}+IDF_{n}=:RHS(n)

(5.3.1)

(II) Using
RHS(n)
defined in (
5.3.1
), we have for the case
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}>0
: For any
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq U

\displaystyle LH_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}

\displaystyle\kappa^{2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}RHS(|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t})

(5.3.2)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\kappa^{2i}(\kappa^{2}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{S}^{2})^{j-i}\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}RHS(l_{x})

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\kappa^{2j}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-j-1)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\sum_{j=0}^{n-2}\kappa^{2j}(\kappa^{2}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{S}^{2})^{n-j-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-n,\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}),

where we employ convention that sums over empty sets (of indices) are vanishing.

In fact, the above Proposition will be proven as a special case of even more comprehensive, technical Proposition
5.15
, where the quantities with which we estimate
LH_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
are more flexible, e.g. they are allowed to have less space derivatives, at the cost of more advective derivatives. These proofs are provided in Section
5.6
.

The generality of Propostions
5.4
,
5.15
makes them useful for diverse choices of scales. For the purpose of particular scales used in this paper, we specify Propostion
5.4
in the following two sections.

5.4.
First specification of the general energy-type estimates

In this paper, scales do not only satisfy Assumption
5.1
, but they are also chosen according to (
5.1.2
). This means in particular that
\mathcal{A}
can be consumed by
\lesssim
(since it is a constant) and that
\kappa^{2}=\mathcal{T}\mathcal{S}^{-2}
. It turns out that these two properties suffice to extract the estimates that we need, even without resorting to the specific choice (
5.1.2
) of scales. More precisely, in addition to Assumption
5.1
, we take

Assumption 5.5
.

The scales satisfy
\mathcal{A}=C
,
\kappa^{2}=\mathcal{T}\mathcal{S}^{-2}

Using Assumptions
5.1
,
5.5

and definition of
RHS(n)
, we see that for the second line of r.h.s. of (
5.3.2
) one has

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\kappa^{2i}(\kappa^{2}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{S}^{2})^{j-i}\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}RHS(l_{x})

(5.4.1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\mathcal{S}^{-2i}\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\left(\mathcal{S}^{l_{x}}\mathcal{D}_{\rho}+IDF_{l_{x}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathcal{D}_{\rho}+\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\sum_{i=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}-2i-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}IDF_{l_{x}}.

Putting this estimate together with an analogous estimate for the first line of r.h.s. of (
5.3.2
), we see that the both the first and the second line of r.h.s. of (
5.3.2
) are estimated as follows

\text{two first r.h.s. lines of \eqref{e:ener_gen_mixed}}\lesssim\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathcal{D}_{\rho}+\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-l}IDF_{l}.

(5.4.2)

Concerning the ’forcing terms’ of r.h.s. of (
5.3.2
), we first observe that for
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=1
the last line of r.h.s. of (
5.3.2
) vanishes, so the only forcing term is
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=1
-case of the middle line of r.h.s. of (
5.3.2
), being here simply
LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},0)
(consistently with the initialization of the induction in section
5.6.5
). For
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}>1
, via
\kappa^{2}=\mathcal{T}\mathcal{S}^{-2}
, we have

\displaystyle\text{third r.h.s\ line of \eqref{e:ener_gen_mixed}}=\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{S}^{-2})^{j}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-j-1)

(5.4.3)

and we have

fourth r.h.s line of (
5.3.2
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\sum_{j=0}^{n-2}\mathcal{S}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-n\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t})

(5.4.4)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\mathcal{S}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{S}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}),

where the inequalities are valid, respectively, by lossy extension of the range of sums, and then the fact the the summands do not depend on
n
any more. Observe that now the last line of (
5.4.4
) contains, as its
l_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j
,
l_{t}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)
-term, the estimate (
5.4.3
). Thus we have

third and fourth r.h.s line of (
5.3.2
)

(5.4.5)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{S}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}),

Putting together (
5.4.2
), (
5.4.5
) for (
5.3.2
), using the definition (
5.2.3
) of the quantity
IDF_{n}
as well as providing basic energy estimate, we have

Corollary 5.6
.

Take smooth, divergence free
u
, smooth forcing
f
and datum
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}
. Then the smooth solution of (
5.0.1
) satisfies the base energy identity

\mathcal{D}_{\rho}:=\sup_{t\leq T}\|\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq\|\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds.

(5.4.6)

Furthermore, if the scales Assumptions
5.1
and
5.5
hold, then we have:

(I. Case of pure space derivatives) For
n\geq 1
,
n\leq 2U

\sup_{t\leq T}\|\nabla^{n}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\lesssim\mathcal{S}^{n}\mathcal{D}_{\rho}+\left(\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)

(5.4.7)

(II. Case of mixed derivatives) For the case
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}>0
: For any
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq U

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}LH_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{D}_{\rho}+\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-l}\left(\|\nabla^{l}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{l}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)

(5.4.8)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}),

Several remarks related to Corollary
5.6
are in order

Remark 5.7

(Consistency with
u_{q}
,
v_{q}
and generality of advective derivatives)
.

Lemma
5.2
shows that
the assumptions of Corollary
5.6
hold for solutions of (
5.0.1
) in case of
D_{u}
there being either
D
-derivative (i.e.
u:=u_{q}
) or
O
-derivative (i.e.
u:=v_{q}
), with
U=\lfloor\frac{Y-N_{*}-1}{2}\rfloor
. Analogously, taking into account results of Section
3
Corollary
5.6
holds for any combination of
O^{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}}
and
D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}}
present in the quantity
LH_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
, provided the same combination appears in
LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
.

Remark 5.8

(Non-divergence form forcing)
.

In case (
5.0.1
) is replaced with

\begin{split}D_{u}\rho-\kappa\Delta\rho=&F,\\
\rho(\cdot,0)=&\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{split}

(5.4.9)

we will content ourselves with possibly the estimates obtained as follows. The pure space estimate with
n=0
uses Poincare inequality to estimate
\rho
with its gradient, and similarly the pure space estimate with
n>0
uses one integration by parts

\int\nabla^{n}F\nabla^{n}\rho dx=-\int\nabla^{n-1}F\nabla^{n+1}\rho dx

Consequently, for non-divergence forcing, Corollary
5.6
holds with the following changes

(i) Instead of
LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
we have
LH_{F}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
,

(ii) instead of

\left(\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)

in right-hand sides, we have

\left(\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{0\wedge(n-1)}F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\right)

5.5.
Further specification of the general energy-type estimates

In order to make our estimates even more convenient and tailored to our needs, we split them into two cases: of nonzero initial datum and zero forcing, and of zero initial datum and nonzero forcing. Furthermore, we renormalise certain quantities. To this end, recall definition (
5.2.5
) of quantity
LH_{h}(l_{x},l_{t})
and definition (
5.2.6
) of its renormalisation, i.e.
\mathring{L}H_{h}(l_{x},l_{t})
.

5.5.1.
Setup

Consider

\begin{split}D_{q}\rho-\kappa\Delta\rho=&\,F=\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-1}\operatorname{div}f,\\
\rho(\cdot,0)=&\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{split}

(5.5.1)

Remark 5.9
.

In view of Remark
5.7
, if we replace
D_{q}
by
O_{q}
in (
5.5.1
), or in definition of the Leray-Hopf quantities used below, the results of this section hold true.

5.5.2.
Scales and renormalised scales

We will use the scales

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle=\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\kappa^{-1}_{q}

(5.5.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle=(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q})^{2}

as in (
5.1.2
) and additionally their renormalised counterparts:

\displaystyle\mathcal{\mathring{S}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{S}}{\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q}}

(5.5.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{\mathring{T}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{T}}{\mathring{\mu}^{2}_{q}}

One can informally identify these scales as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{\|\nabla u_{q}\|_{\infty}}{\kappa_{q}},\qquad\mathcal{\mathring{S}}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{\|\nabla u_{q}\|_{\infty}}{\kappa_{q}\mathring{\lambda}^{2}_{q}},

(5.5.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle\sim\|\nabla u_{q}\|^{2}_{\infty},\qquad\mathcal{\mathring{T}}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{\|\nabla u_{q}\|^{2}_{\infty}}{\mathring{\mu}^{2}_{q}},

see also Remark
5.3
.

5.5.3.
Result for initial datum and no forcing

Using Corollary
5.6
, taking into account Remark
5.7
, as well as notation as in Section
5.2
,
we obtain directly the case with no forcing

Lemma 5.10
.

Consider (
5.5.1
) with
f=0
. For any
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq\lfloor\frac{Y-N_{*}-1}{2}\rfloor
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|>0

we have

\mathcal{S}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}{{LH}_{\rho}}(|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}\kappa\iint|\nabla\rho|^{2}dxdt+\sum_{p=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-p}\|\nabla^{p}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.5.5)

Reformulated in renormalized quantities, we have

\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}{\mathring{LH}_{\rho}}(|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\iint|\mathring{\nabla}\rho|^{2}dxdt+\sum_{p=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-p}\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{p}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.5.6)

Remark 5.11

(Case of pure space derivatives)
.

In both estimates, in the case
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=0
, the sum
\sum_{p=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}
may be replaced with its last summand (so that after multiplying with the space scale present as the first r.h.s. term, the initial datum is bare
\|\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}^{2}
, or respectively
\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}^{2}
). For instance (
5.5.5
) for
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=0
reads

\mathcal{S}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}{{LH}_{\rho}}(|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}\kappa\iint|\nabla\rho|^{2}dxdt+\|\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.5.7)

5.5.4.
Results with forcing and zero initial datum (non-renormalised)

Let us now specify Corollary
5.6
to the case with no initial datum, but forced. Our forcing that fits Corollary
5.6
equals
\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-1}f
, see (
5.5.1
), or in non-divergence case simply
F
(then we take into account Remark
5.8
). In the former case, observe that the quantities appearing in the estimates are quadratic, thus
{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}^{-2}
appears accordingly on right-hand sides. Taking also into account Remark
5.8
, Corollary
5.6
yields

Lemma 5.12
.

Consider (
5.5.1
) with
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}=0
. For any
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq\lfloor\frac{Y-N_{*}-1}{2}\rfloor
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|>0
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}{{LH}_{\rho}}(|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t})

(5.5.8)

\displaystyle+\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\kappa^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-l}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{l}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}{{LH}_{\rho}}(|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{F}(l_{x},l_{t}),

(5.5.9)

\displaystyle+\kappa^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-l}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{l-1}F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds.

As in the previous lemma, in case of pure space derivatives the first lines of r.h.s.’s vanish, and the remaining sum (over
l
’s) may be replaced by its last summand.

5.5.5.
Results with forcing and zero initial datum (renormalised and simplified)

Finally, let us renormalise the quantities in Lemma
5.12
and simplify the formulas.

Consider first the l.h.s’s in Lemma
5.12
.
Since the order of
\boldsymbol{\beta}
agrees with the powers of scales appearing there, the l.h.s’s in Lemma
5.12
are equal to their renormalised counterparts, i.e.

\mathcal{T}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}{LH}_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})=\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathring{LH}_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta}).

(5.5.10)

(i. Case of forcing in divergence form.) Let us now consider the r.h.s. of (
5.5.8
).
Concerning the first term of the r.h.s. of (
5.5.8
), we have

\displaystyle\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t})

(5.5.11)

\displaystyle=\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\underbrace{\mathcal{S}^{2}\mathcal{T}^{-1}}_{:=\kappa^{-2}}\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}-2}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\kappa^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{l}_{x}=1,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j+1,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-\tilde{l}_{x}-1}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{f}(\tilde{l}_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle=\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\kappa^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{l}_{x}=1,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j+1,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-\tilde{l}_{x}-1}\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-l_{t}}\mathring{LH}_{f}(\tilde{l}_{x},l_{t}).

Concerning the second term of the r.h.s. of (
5.5.8
), we have

\displaystyle\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\kappa^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-l}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{l}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle=\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\kappa^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-l}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{l}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

(5.5.12)

\displaystyle=\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-l}\underbrace{\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{l}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds}_{\leq\mathring{LH}_{f}(l-1,0)}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\sum_{\tilde{l}=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-\tilde{l}-1}\mathring{LH}_{f}(\tilde{l},0)

where the under-braced inequality holds since
\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{l}=\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{l-1}\mathring{\nabla}_{q}
.

Observe that if in the last line of (
5.5.11
) we extend the lower bound of the sum from
\tilde{l}_{x}=1
down to
\tilde{l}_{x}=0
, we accommodate as the
l_{t}=0
,
j=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1
term of the sum the entire (
5.5.12
). Thus, putting together (
5.5.10
), (
5.5.11
), (
5.5.12
) into (
5.5.8
), we have

\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathring{LH}_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\kappa^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{l}_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j+1,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-\tilde{l}_{x}-1}\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-l_{t}}\mathring{LH}_{f}(\tilde{l}_{x},l_{t}).

(5.5.13)

(ii. Case of forcing in non-divergence form.)
Analogously we deal with the (
5.5.9
). More precisely, we have for the first r.h.s. term of (
5.5.9
)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{F}(l_{x},l_{t})

(5.5.14)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathcal{S}^{2}\mathcal{T}^{-1}}_{:=\kappa^{-2}}\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{-l_{x}-2}\mathcal{T}^{-l_{t}}LH_{F}(l_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle=\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\kappa^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-l_{x}-2}\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-l_{t}}LH_{F}(l_{x},l_{t})

and for the second r.h.s. term of (
5.5.9
)

\displaystyle\kappa^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-l}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{l-1}F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle=\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-l}\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{l-1}F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle=\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\sum_{\tilde{l}=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-\tilde{l}-1}\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{\tilde{l}}F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

Splitting in the preceding identity the sum over
\tilde{l}
into the summand for
\tilde{l}=0
and the rest, we have

\displaystyle\kappa^{-1}

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{S}^{-l}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{l-1}F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

(5.5.15)

\displaystyle=\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\sum_{\tilde{l}=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-\tilde{l}-1}\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{\tilde{l}}F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds+\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-1}\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{0}F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\sum_{\tilde{l}=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-\tilde{l}-1}\mathring{LH}_{F}(\tilde{l}-1,0)+\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-1}\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{0}F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle=\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\sum_{l=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-2}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-l-2}\mathring{LH}_{F}(l,0)+\kappa^{-1}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds.

Observe that the first term of the last line of (
5.5.15
) is the
l_{t}=0
,
j=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1
term of the sum in the last line of (
5.5.14
). Taking this into account and
putting together (
5.5.10
), (
5.5.14
), (
5.5.15
) into (
5.5.9
), one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathring{LH}_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\kappa^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-l_{x}-2}\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-l_{t}}LH_{F}(l_{x},l_{t})

(5.5.16)

\displaystyle+\kappa^{-1}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds.

The formulas (
5.5.13
), (
5.5.16
) are our key estimates. In order to shorten the sums appearing on their r.h.s.’s, let us use the notation
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]:=|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}
, see section
1.5.1
, and rewrite the sum in (
5.5.13
) according to

\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{l}_{x}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2j+1,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-(j+1)\end{subarray}}(\cdot)\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{l}_{x}+2l_{t}\leq|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1,\\
l_{t}=0,\dots,|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}(\cdot)\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}\leq|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1,\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}\leq|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}(\cdot)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]-1,\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}\leq|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}(\cdot),

where the penultimate sum means that we sum over all
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}
such that
0\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}\leq|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1
and
0\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}\leq|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1
, and analogously in the last sum. Similar rewriting of the sum of (
5.5.16
) differs only in the range being now
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]-2
.
Observe in both cases the ranges are admissible as long as
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|>0
, where in the case of pure space derivatives, as long as
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|>0
, we have only the range
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]-1
or, respectively,
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]-2
, present. With these remarks, formulas (
5.5.13
), (
5.5.16
) yield

Lemma 5.13
.

Consider (
5.5.1
) with
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}=0
. For any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}
such that
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq\lfloor\frac{Y-N_{*}-1}{2}\rfloor
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|>0
we have:

In case of the forcing term of (
5.5.1
) being
\lambda_{q}^{-1}\operatorname{div}f
:

\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathring{LH}_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\kappa^{-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]-1,\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}\leq|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-(|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+1)}\cdot\mathring{LH}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}).

(5.5.17)

In case of the forcing term of (
5.5.1
) being
F
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mathring{LH}_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}&\,\kappa^{-1}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|F(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\\
&+\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\kappa^{-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]-2,\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}\leq|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}\mathcal{\mathring{T}}^{-|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}}\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{-(|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+2)}\cdot\mathring{LH}_{F}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}).\end{split}

(5.5.18)

In case of pure space derivatives, the ranges of r.h.s. sums are, respectively,
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]-1
,
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]-2
.

Corollary 5.14
.

Suppose for some
n_{0}\leq 12Q^{3}
and some
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq 3Q
, satisfies the following estimates

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\int|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}g(t)|^{2}dx+\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\iint|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\mathring{\nabla}_{q}g|^{2}dxdt\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q}^{-4[\boldsymbol{\omega}]b\gamma},\quad\forall\,[\boldsymbol{\omega}]\leq n_{0},

(5.5.19)

\displaystyle h\in\mathcal{P}_{q}(n_{1}),\quad{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}.

(5.5.20)

The force
f
is given by

\displaystyle f:=h\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}g,

(5.5.21)

Then we have

\begin{split}\|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}\|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}_{q}\rho\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lambda_{q}^{-2([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+[\boldsymbol{\alpha}])b\gamma},\quad\forall\,[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq n_{0}-3Q.\end{split}

(5.5.22)

Proof.

Note that we have

\displaystyle|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f|^{2}\sim|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}h\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}g|^{2}+|h\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}g|^{2}

and from Corollary
3.7

\displaystyle\|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}h\|_{\infty}\lesssim\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\lambda_{q}^{-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\gamma_{R}},\quad|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq Y-n_{1}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mathring{LH}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\lesssim\kappa^{2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{4}\cdot\lambda_{q}^{-4([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+[\boldsymbol{\alpha}])b\gamma},\quad[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]+[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq n_{0}

Here, we require
\gamma_{R}\geq 2b\gamma
. Then from Lemma
5.13
, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{LH}_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}\kappa^{-2}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-4}\sup_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]-1}\mathring{LH}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}\lambda_{q}^{-4([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+[\boldsymbol{\alpha}])b\gamma}.

Here, we use the relation

\displaystyle\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{\mathring{T}}\lesssim\mathcal{\mathring{S}}^{2},

which follows from the definition of
\mathcal{\mathring{S}}
and
\mathcal{\mathring{T}}
, and the parameter relations in Section
2.1
.
∎

5.6.
Proof of Proposition
5.4

5.6.1.
Notational remarks

We will use at times slightly informal language, to avoid too cumbersome notation. In particular

(i. Concerning derivatives) Instead of an arbitrary partial space derivative of order
k
, we simply write
\nabla^{k}
; consequently ’
\cdot
’ between high-order derivatives needs to be interpreted appropriately.

(ii. Concerning combinatorial coefficients)
We will write
a\sim_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\sum_{i}b_{i}
if
a=\sum_{i}c_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(i)b_{i}
, where
c_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(i)
are coefficients uniformly bounded in terms of
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|
(or
a\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}\sum_{i}b_{i}
in case of estimates).
Since the coefficient
c_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(i)
will originate from combinatorial coefficients of a product rule, we will refer to
c_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(i)
’s as to ’combinatorial-type coefficients’.

5.6.2.
Case of pure space derivatives

The estimate (
5.3.1
) for pure space derivatives can be readily obtained along the lines of
[
burczak2023anomalous
]
. Indeed, inspecting the proof of Lemma 4.1
[
burczak2023anomalous
]
(see the last inequality in the proof) and taking into account Corollary 4.2
[
burczak2023anomalous
]
(which includes forcing), together with Young inequality to estimate the forcing term, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq T}\|\nabla^{n}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq

(5.6.1)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C_{n}\left(\left(\frac{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{n}+\left(\frac{\|\nabla^{n}u\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\kappa}\right)^{\frac{2n}{n+1}}\right)\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle+\kappa^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds.

(The forcing term was left un-estimated in
[
burczak2023anomalous
]
, since it was subsequently treated in ’time-averaging’ section by further integration by parts. Now we don’t need it, since our homogenisation Ansatz is higher order.)

Using in (
5.6.1
) the scales relationship (
5.1.1
) we arrive at

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq T}\|\nabla^{n}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n+1}\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{n}\rho_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2C_{n}\mathcal{A}^{n}\mathcal{S}^{n}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds+\kappa^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla^{n}f(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

which is (
5.3.1
), taking into account the shorthands (
5.2.1
), (
5.2.3
).

5.6.3.
Formula for replication in case of advective derivatives

Consider the case of a single advective derivative, i.e.
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|-1
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=1
. If the advective derivative is the innermost one, we have from the equation (
5.0.1
)

\nabla^{k}D\rho=\kappa\nabla^{k}\Delta\rho+\nabla^{k}\operatorname{div}f.

(5.6.2)

If the advective derivative is the outermost one, then

\displaystyle D\nabla^{k}\rho

\displaystyle=\nabla^{k}D\rho+[D,\nabla^{k}]\rho=\nabla^{k}D\rho+[u\cdot\nabla,\nabla^{k}]\rho

(5.6.3)

\displaystyle=\nabla^{k}D\rho+\sum_{i=1}^{k}c_{k,i}\nabla^{i}u\cdot\nabla^{k-i}\nabla\rho,

where
c_{k,i}
are combinatorial coefficients.
Acting with
\nabla^{l}
on (
5.6.3
) we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{l}D\nabla^{k}\rho

\displaystyle=\nabla^{k+l}D\rho+\sum_{j=0}^{l}\tilde{c}_{l,j}\sum_{i=1}^{k}c_{k,i}\nabla^{i+j}u\cdot\nabla^{k-i+l-j}\nabla\rho

(5.6.4)

\displaystyle=\kappa\nabla^{k+l}\Delta\rho+\nabla^{k+l}\operatorname{div}f+\sum_{i=1}^{k+l}c_{k,i,l}\nabla^{i}u\cdot\nabla^{k+l-i}\nabla\rho,

where for the last identity we used
(
5.6.2
).

Together, we see from (
5.6.2
) and (
5.6.4
) with
k+l=\boldsymbol{\beta}
that in the case
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|-1
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=1
it holds

\displaystyle D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho

\displaystyle\sim_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\kappa\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\Delta\rho+\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\operatorname{div}f+\sum_{i=1}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\nabla^{i}u\cdot\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}-i}\nabla\rho

(5.6.5)

\displaystyle=\kappa\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\Delta\rho+\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\operatorname{div}f+\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}-1}\nabla^{j}\nabla u\cdot\nabla^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}-j}\rho

Importantly, we see that we do not have advective derivatives on r.h.s. of (
5.6.5
), so we can use the case of pure space derivatives to estimate
D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho
for any
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|-1
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=1
. Observe also that we ’paid’ for this single advective derivative with two space derivatives: the highest space derivative on r.h.s. is of order
\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2
(the dissipative term).

In (
5.6.5
) we see also our ’informal notation’ of Section
5.6.1
(ii) is useful: comparing (
5.6.2
) and (
5.6.3
) we see that certain orders of taking advective and space derivatives produce less terms than other ones, but they are all captured by (
5.6.5
).

Next, consider the case of two advective derivatives and the rest being space derivatives, i.e.
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|-2
,
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}=2
. In other words, we consider now
\nabla^{m}D\nabla^{l}D\nabla^{k}\rho
. Using (
5.6.4
) we see that

\displaystyle D\nabla^{l}D\nabla^{k}\rho

\displaystyle=\kappa D\nabla^{k+l}\Delta\rho+D\nabla^{k+l}\operatorname{div}f+D\sum_{i=1}^{k+l}c_{k,i,l}\nabla^{i}u\cdot\nabla^{k+l-i}\nabla\rho

(5.6.6)

\displaystyle\sim_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\kappa D\nabla^{k+l+2}\rho+D\nabla^{k+l}\operatorname{div}f+\sum_{i=1}^{k+l}D\nabla^{i}u\cdot\nabla^{k+l-i}\nabla\rho+\sum_{i=1}^{k+l}\nabla^{i}u\cdot D\nabla^{k+l+1-i}\rho.

Acting on (
5.6.6
) with
\nabla^{m}
we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{m}D\nabla^{l}D\nabla^{k}\rho

\displaystyle\sim_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\kappa\nabla^{m}D\nabla^{k+l+2}\rho+\nabla^{m}D\nabla^{k+l}\operatorname{div}f+

(5.6.7)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{m}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k+l}D\nabla^{i}u\cdot\nabla^{k+l-i}\nabla\rho+\sum_{i=1}^{k+l}\nabla^{i}u\cdot D\nabla^{k+l-i}\nabla\rho\right)

so, analogously to (
5.6.5
), we have now (via
k+l+m=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}
)

D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho\sim_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\kappa\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2,\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=1\end{subarray}}D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho+\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=1\end{subarray}}D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\operatorname{div}f+\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\omega}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}>0\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}+|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{t}=1\end{subarray}}D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla u\cdot D^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho.

(5.6.8)

We can obtain now recursively the general case. Indeed, analyzing structure of (
5.6.5
), (
5.6.8
) (where
1=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1
) we see that in the general case it holds

D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho\sim_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\kappa\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2,\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho+\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\operatorname{div}f+\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\omega}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{x}>0\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}+|\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{t}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla u\cdot D^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho.

(5.6.9)

We want to estimate the Leray-Hopf quantity

LH_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta}):=\sup_{t\leq T}\|D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho(t)\|^{2}_{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|D^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\nabla\rho(t)\|^{2}_{2}dt,

compare its definition (
5.2.4
). So via (
5.6.9
) for
\rho
and for
\nabla\rho
we have

\displaystyle LH_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}\sum_{\sup\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}+2,\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}LH_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})+\sum_{\sup\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})

(5.6.10)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sup\,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}:\begin{subarray}{c}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|_{x}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x},|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|_{x}>0\\
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|_{t}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}-1\end{subarray}}\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla u\|^{2}_{\infty}LH_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}),

importantly, as already observed in the special case of two advective derivatives, also in the general formula (
5.6.9
) there is one advective derivative less on r.h.s. compared to l.h.s. (and two space derivatives more). The formula (
5.6.10
) applied recursively, allows to decrease number of advective derivatives from
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}
down to
0
, at the cost of
2|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{t}
more space derivatives. Instead of going all the way back, we will use (
5.6.10
) within induction step.

5.6.4.
h
-depth formula

Since
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq U
, we can replace
\lesssim_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|}
of (
5.6.10
) with
\lesssim_{U}
, for which we will write simply
\lesssim
(
U
is pre-fixed). This and our considerations thus far
allow to rewrite (
5.6.10
), using the quantity (
5.2.5
),
as

\displaystyle LH_{\rho}(m,n)\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}LH_{\rho}(m+2,n-1)+LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m,n-1)

(5.6.11)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{\tilde{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-1\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\rho}(l_{x},l_{t}),

where to estimate
\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla u\|^{2}_{\infty}
we used the assumption (
5.1.1
) that gives

\|D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\nabla u\|^{2}_{\infty}\leq\kappa^{2}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}+2}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}=:\mathcal{\tilde{A}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}

where by definition
\mathcal{\tilde{A}}=\kappa^{2}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{S}^{2}
.
The inequality (
5.6.11
) allows to conjecture

Proposition 5.15
.

Within assumptions of Proposition
5.4
one has for an arbitrary
h=1,\dots,n

LH_{\rho}(m,n)\leq RLH_{\rho}(m,n,h)+RLH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m,n,h),

(5.6.12)

where

\displaystyle RLH_{\rho}(m,n,h)

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2h}LH_{\rho}(m+2h,n-h)+\sum_{i=0}^{h-1}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{h-i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-h,l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\rho}(l_{x},l_{t})

(5.6.13)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{h}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{j-i}\mathcal{T}^{n-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\left(RHS(l_{x})\right),

with
RHS(l_{x})=\mathcal{A}^{l_{x}}\mathcal{S}^{l_{x}}\mathcal{D}_{\rho}+IDF_{l_{x}}
as defined in (
5.3.1
),
and

\displaystyle RLH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m,n,h)=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{h}\kappa^{2(j-1)}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m+2(j-1),n-j)

(5.6.14)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{h}\sum_{i=0}^{j-2}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{j-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-j,\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}),

where we use the convention that sum over empty set is vanishing, so for
j=1
the second line of (
5.6.14
) equals
0
.

Even though the formulas in Proposition
5.15
seem cumbersome, their structure should be clear: using the replication formula (
5.6.11
) recursively, one lowers the required-on-
\rho
advective derivatives at the cost of twice as many space derivatives, paying attention to have
\kappa
,
\mathcal{\tilde{A}}
,
\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}
,
\mathcal{T}
in appropriate powers. In particular, the first line of (
5.6.13
) can be expanded further using the replication formula (
5.6.11
), while the second line of (
5.6.13
) is the ’initial term’; it contains: (i) the case of
l_{t}=0
instead of
l_{t}>0
in the first line of (
5.6.13
), which is the second line of (
5.6.13
) for
j=h
, (ii) the previous scales of initial data, i.e. the second line of (
5.6.13
) for
j<h
. (Of course we can write both terms: involving
l_{t}=0
and involving
l_{t}>0
together, but it is useful for application of Proposition
5.15
to separate them.)
Similarly, the forcing-related term (
5.6.14
) appears, with the difference compared (
5.6.13
) being that forcing acts via ’each scale’: we can’t lower the needed advective derivatives from
n
down to
n-h
, but we have the full sum
\sum_{j=1}^{h}
.

5.6.5.
Proof of Proposition
5.15

We prove now (
5.6.12
) inductively over
h
. For
h=1
we have it directly from (
5.6.11
), splitting there

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-1\end{subarray}}\quad\text{ into }\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-1,\\
l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-1,\\
l_{t}=0\end{subarray}}

which holds, because it is exactly (
5.6.11
).

Assume now that (
5.6.12
) holds for any
h\leq h_{0}-1
; this is our inductive assumption and we want to prove that (
5.6.12
) holds for
h_{0}
. This is a straightforward computation which involves merely shifts in summing indices, but we provide it for completeness.
The inductive assumption is

\displaystyle LH_{\rho}(m,n)

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2(h_{0}-1)}LH_{\rho}(m+2(h_{0}-1),n-(h_{0}-1))

\displaystyle=:I

(5.6.15)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=0}^{h_{0}-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-(h_{0}-1),\\
l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{(h_{0}-1)-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\rho}(l_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle=:II

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{h_{0}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{j-i}\mathcal{T}^{n-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}RHS(l_{x})

\displaystyle=:III

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{h_{0}-1}\kappa^{2(j-1)}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m+2(j-1),n-j)

\displaystyle=:IV

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{h_{0}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{j-2}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{j-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-j,\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle=:V,

Using (
5.6.11
) with
m:=m+2(h_{0}-1),n:=n-h_{0}+1
we have

\displaystyle I\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa^{2h_{0}}LH_{\rho}(m+2h_{0},n-h_{0})

\displaystyle=:I^{1}_{\rho}

(5.6.16)

\displaystyle+\kappa^{2(h_{0}-1)}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m+2h_{0}-2,n-h_{0})

\displaystyle=:I_{\operatorname{div}f}

\displaystyle+\kappa^{2(h_{0}-1)}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2h_{0}-2,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-h_{0}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\rho}(l_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle=:I^{2}_{\rho},

Using in
II
(
5.6.11
) with
m:=l_{x},n:=l_{t}
we have

\displaystyle II\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0,\dots,(h_{0}-1)-1,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-(h_{0}-1),l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{(h_{0}-1)-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}\kappa^{2}LH_{\rho}(l_{x}+2,l_{t}-1)

\displaystyle=:II^{1}_{\rho}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0,\dots,(h_{0}-1)-1,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-(h_{0}-1),l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{(h_{0}-1)-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}-1)

\displaystyle=:II_{\operatorname{div}f}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0,\dots,(h_{0}-1)-1,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-(h_{0}-1),l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{(h_{0}-1)-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{k}_{x}+\tilde{l}_{x}=l_{x},\tilde{l}_{x}>0\\
\tilde{k}_{t}+\tilde{l}_{t}=l_{t}-1\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{\tilde{k}_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{\tilde{k}_{t}}LH_{\rho}(\tilde{l}_{x},\tilde{l}_{t})

\displaystyle=:II^{2}_{\rho}

By shifting indices of
II^{1}_{\rho}
as follows:
i+1
to
i
,
l_{x}+2
to
l_{x}
and
l_{t}-1
to
l_{t}
(the latter allowed since
II^{1}_{\rho}
involves sums only over
l_{t}>0
) we have

\displaystyle II^{1}_{\rho}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,h_{0}-1,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-h_{0}\end{subarray}}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{h_{0}-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\rho}(l_{x},l_{t})

(5.6.17)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,h_{0}-1,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-h_{0},l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{h_{0}-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\rho}(l_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,h_{0}-1,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{h_{0}-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{n-h_{0}}\underbrace{LH_{\rho}(l_{x},0)}_{\lesssim RHS(l_{x})}

In the double sum of
II^{2}_{\rho}
we compute
\tilde{k}_{x}+\tilde{l}_{x}=l_{x}=m+2i-k_{x}
, so
(\tilde{k}_{x}+k_{x})+\tilde{l}_{x}=m+2i
and analogously
(\tilde{k}_{t}+k_{t})+\tilde{l}_{t}=n-h_{0}
. Therefore

II^{2}_{\rho}\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0,\dots,h_{0}-2,\\
(\tilde{k}_{x}+k_{x})+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\\
(\tilde{k}_{t}+k_{t})+l_{t}=n-h_{0}\end{subarray}}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{h_{0}-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}+\tilde{k}_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}+\tilde{k}_{t}}LH_{\rho}(\tilde{l}_{x},\tilde{l}_{t}),

(5.6.18)

which is already contained in the r.h.s. of (
5.6.17
).

Together, definitions in (
5.6.15
), (
5.6.16
) (splitting
I_{\rho}^{2}
into
l_{t}=0
and
l_{t}>0
part) and inequalities (
5.6.17
), (
5.6.18
) give

\displaystyle I^{1}_{\rho}+I^{2}_{\rho}+II^{1}_{\rho}+II^{2}_{\rho}+III

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2h_{0}}LH_{\rho}(m+2h_{0},n-h_{0})

(5.6.19)

\displaystyle+\kappa^{2(h_{0}-1)}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2(h_{0}-1),l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-h_{0},l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\rho}(l_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle+\kappa^{2(h_{0}-1)}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2(h_{0}-1),l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{n-h_{0}}RHS(l_{x})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,h_{0}-1,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-h_{0},l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{h_{0}-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\rho}(l_{x},l_{t})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,h_{0}-1,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{h_{0}-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{n-h_{0}}RHS(l_{x})

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{h_{0}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{j-i}\mathcal{T}^{n-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}RHS(l_{x}).

Compare r.h.s. of (
5.6.19
) with
RLH_{\rho}(m,n,h_{0})
as defined by (
5.6.13
). We see that the first r.h.s. term of (
5.6.19
) equals the first term of
RLH_{\rho}(m,n,h_{0})
. The two terms of r.h.s. of (
5.6.19
) that are sums involving
l_{t}>0
are estimated by the former of these two terms (the latter is
i=h_{0}-1
summand in the former), which equals the second term of
RLH_{\rho}(m,n,h_{0})
. The ’initial terms’, i.e. the remaining three terms of r.h.s. of (
5.6.19
) that have
RHS(l_{x})
sum up to (a constant times) the last term of
RLH_{\rho}(m,n,h_{0})
. So
altogether we see that the terms not involving forcing, which estimate
LH_{\rho}(m,n)
are controlled by
RLH_{\rho}(m,n,h_{0})
.

We are left with estimating the ’forcing terms’, namely

\displaystyle I_{\operatorname{div}f}+II_{\operatorname{div}f}+IV+V

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2(h_{0}-1)}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m+2(h_{0}-1),n-h_{0})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0,\dots,h_{0}-2,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-(h_{0}-1),l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{(h_{0}-1)-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}-1)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{h_{0}-1}\kappa^{2(j-1)}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m+2(j-1),n-j)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{h_{0}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{j-2}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{j-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-j,\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}),

whose first and third r.h.s. term summed are equal to the first term of
RLH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m,n,h_{0})
as defined in (
5.6.14
); shifting indices as before in the second term we can estimate it with

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0,\dots,h_{0}-2,\\
k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-h_{0},l_{t}>0\end{subarray}}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{(h_{0}-1)-i}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}),

which is
j=h_{0}
summand of the second term of
RLH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m,n,h_{0})
, whereas the remaining to be estimated term is the
\sum_{j<h_{0}}
part of the second term of
RLH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m,n,h_{0})
.

Altogether, we have verified

LH_{\rho}(m,n)\leq RLH_{\rho}(m,n,h_{0})+RLH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m,n,h_{0})

which is what we wanted for our inductive step. Consequently Proposition
5.15
is proven.

5.6.6.
Concluding remarks for proof of Proposition
5.4

Take Proposition
5.15
with
h=n
, which gives (
l_{t}>0
is now empty condition), since
LH_{\rho}(m+2n,0)\lesssim RHS(m+2n)

\displaystyle LH_{\rho}(m,n)

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2n}RHS(m+2n)+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{j-i}\mathcal{T}^{n-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,l_{x}>0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}RHS(l_{x})

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\kappa^{2(j-1)}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(m+2(j-1),n-j)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{i=0}^{j-2}\kappa^{2i}\mathcal{\tilde{A}}^{j-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{x}+l_{x}=m+2i,\\
k_{t}+l_{t}=n-j,\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{T}^{k_{t}}LH_{\operatorname{div}f}(l_{x},l_{t}).

Via definitions of
\mathcal{\tilde{A}}
and of
LH_{\rho}(m,n)
as in Section
5.6.4
, it gives (
5.3.2
).

Let us remark that the upper bound
|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq U
is a careless estimate of the amount of derivatives needed: in the worst case, when estimating
U
-many advective derivatives, we need
2U
-many space derivatives in formula (
5.3.2
), available via Assumption
5.1
. Of course, all our estimates have a huge constant in
\lesssim
, which is however merely
U
-dependent.

6.
Homogenization in inertial range

In this section, we prove the homogenization estimates from step
q
to step
q+1
.

6.1.
Characterization of initial datum

We introduce a few notions to characterize initial datum. We need two types of notions, to measure the initial datum at
L^{2}
level, and to control the derivatives of initial datum up to order
14Q^{3}
.

At
L^{2}
-level, we define energy dissipation as follows.

Definition 6.1

(Energy dissipation)
.

For a divergence-free vector field
u\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1])
and
\kappa>0
, we use
|\cdot|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}
to denote energy dissipation for initial datum
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}:=\bigg(\kappa\iint|\nabla\rho|^{2}dxdt\bigg)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\quad\text{with}\quad\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\rho+u\cdot\nabla\rho-\kappa\Delta\rho=&\,0,\\
\quad\rho(\cdot,0)=&\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{aligned}\right.

(6.1.1)

Then we have the following trivial estimate

\displaystyle|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}\leq\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.1.2)

To control the (macroscopic) derivatives efficiently, we introduce
composed initial datum
.

Definition 6.2

(Composed initial datum)
.

Given some
n_{*}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, define

\begin{split}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(q,n_{*})}:=\inf\bigg\{&\,R>0\,\bigg|\,\rho=\sum_{0\leq k\leq n_{*}}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}^{(k)};\\
&\,\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{p}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}^{(k)}\|_{2}<\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2(k+p)b\gamma}R,\,\forall\,1\leq p\leq 14Q^{3}-k,\,k\leq n_{*}\bigg\}.\end{split}

(6.1.3)

Naturally,
\mathfrak{S}(q,n_{*})
is the space of functions for which
|\cdot|_{\mathfrak{S}(q,n_{*})}
is finite.
Here, we call
\{\rho_{\mathrm{in}}^{(k)}\}_{k}

atoms
of the composed initial datum
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}
. For brevity, we also introduce shorthand notations

\begin{split}\mathfrak{S}(q):=\mathfrak{S}\big(q,Q^{3}\big),\quad\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q):=\mathfrak{S}\big(q,2Q^{3}\big).\end{split}

(6.1.4)

Remark 6.3
.

For any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
only containing space derivatives, we have

\displaystyle|\mathring{\partial}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(q,n+[\boldsymbol{\alpha}])}\leq

\displaystyle\,|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(q,n)}.

(6.1.5)

In composed initial datum, the bounds are weighted by certain scale in
k
. Below we also introduce an unweighted version.

Definition 6.4

(Composed initial datum without weight)
.

For
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q)
, we define

\begin{split}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q)}:=\inf\Big\{R>0\,\big|\,\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{p}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}<\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2bp\gamma}R,\,\forall\,p\leq 12Q^{3}\Big\}.\end{split}

(6.1.6)

Comparing
\mathfrak{P}
to
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
, we have the following immediate consequence

\displaystyle|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q_{1})}\lesssim|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q_{2})}\lesssim|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q_{2})},\quad\forall\,q_{2}\leq q_{1},\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}).

(6.1.7)

Every element of
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q)
is a sum of atoms for which we have derivative estimates of different order. In the following definition and remark, we introduce an operator
\mathbb{S}
to classify these atoms into two classes: one with derivative estimates of higher order, denoted by
\mathfrak{S}(q)
, and the other with derivative estimates of possibly lower order, denoted by
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q)
.

Definition 6.5
.

We define a projection
\mathbb{S}:\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q)\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}(q)
with

\displaystyle|\mathbb{S}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(q)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}-\mathbb{S}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q)},

(6.1.8)

\displaystyle|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}-\mathbb{S}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2Q^{3}b\gamma}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q)}.

(6.1.9)

Remark 6.6
.

This projection is designed to do some bookkeeping and one has multiple choices for defining it. In all instances where we shall use
\mathbb{S}
, it acts on elements of form

\displaystyle f=\mathring{\partial}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}^{(k_{0})}\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q)

(6.1.10)

for some function
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathfrak{S}(q)
and its atoms
\{\rho_{\mathrm{in}}^{(k)}\}_{k}
. We choose
\mathbb{S}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}f:=\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathring{\partial}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}^{(k_{0})},\quad\text{if }[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]+k_{0}\leq Q^{3},\\
0,\quad\text{if }[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]+k_{0}>Q^{3}.\end{aligned}\right.\in\mathfrak{S}(q).

Then we have

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\nabla}_{q}^{p}(f-\mathbb{S}f)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2(Q^{3}+p)b\gamma}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(q)}.

(6.1.11)

When
\mathbb{S}
acts on linear combinations of terms with form (
6.1.10
), we extend the definition of
\mathbb{S}
linearly. This verifies (
6.1.9
).

For functions defined in
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]
, we introduce a technical condition for its behavior at time
t=0
, which essentially says the transport derivatives associated to
u_{q}
can be conveniently expressed by space derivatives.

Definition 6.7

(Transmission condition)
.

For functions
\rho_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we say the collection
\{\rho_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq n_{*}}
satisfies transmission condition at step
q
if for any
p\leq 10Q

\displaystyle\mathring{D}_{q,t}^{p}\rho_{n}(x,0)=\sum_{a=0}^{n_{*}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|_{x}=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{Q^{3}+Q^{2}+2p}c_{n,a}^{(p,\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\mathring{\partial}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho_{a}(x,0),

(6.1.12)

for a set of constants
c_{n,a}^{(p,\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
satisfies

\displaystyle|c_{n,a}^{(p,\boldsymbol{\alpha})}|\lesssim 1.

(6.1.13)

In
\lesssim
is an absolute constant.

6.2.
Main results of this section

In our inductive scheme from
u_{q}
to
u_{q+1}
, we need to solve the operator
D_{q,t}-\kappa\Delta
multiple times (
2Q^{2}
times) with rather general forces. We also need to propagate a large collection of estimates. For this purpose, we introduce the following notions for estimates of chain type.

Definition 6.8

(Chain estimate I)
.

Fix
\kappa>0
and initial datum
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Consider

\displaystyle\{s_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}\subset

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{R}_{\geq 2},

(6.2.1)

\displaystyle\{g_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}\subset

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{P}_{q}(2NQ),

(6.2.2)

\displaystyle\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}\subset

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{I}_{*},\quad[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}]\leq 3Q

(6.2.3)

with

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\lesssim\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\lambda_{q}^{-s_{n}b\gamma},\quad\forall\,n.

(6.2.4)

Define the functions
\{\rho_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}
to be the solutions of

\begin{split}D_{q,t}\rho_{0}-\kappa\Delta\rho_{0}=&\,0,\\
\rho_{0}(\cdot,0)=&\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}},\\
\ldots&\,\\
D_{q,t}\rho_{n+1}-\kappa\Delta\rho_{n+1}=&\,\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-1}\operatorname{div}(g_{n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\rho_{n}),\\
\rho_{n+1}(\cdot,0)=&\,0,\\
\ldots&\,\end{split}

(6.2.5)

Then we define the notion
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\cdot]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa}
via

\begin{split}{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa}:=\inf\bigg\{R>0\,\bigg|&\,\|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{q}^{-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}R,\quad\forall\,\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*},[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn,\\
&\,\text{for any }n,\{s_{n}\}_{n},\{g_{n}\}_{n},\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}\}_{n},\{\rho_{n}\}_{n}\text{ satisfying }\eqref{e:2:resolve}-\eqref{e:resolveRho}\bigg\}\end{split}

We remind that, according to Section
1.5.5
,
\|\cdot\|
denotes
L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}
-norm and
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
denotes
L_{x,t}^{\infty}
norms.

To propagate
\alpha_{0}
-Hölder estimates, we also define the notion
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\cdot]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u_{q},\kappa}
via

\begin{split}{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u_{q},\kappa}:=\inf\bigg\{&\,R>0\,\bigg|\,\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\leq\lambda_{q}^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}R,\\
&\,\text{for any }n,\{s_{n}\}_{n},\{g_{n}\}_{n},\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}\}_{n},\{\rho_{n}\}_{n}\text{ satisfying }\eqref{e:2:resolve}-\eqref{e:resolveRho}\bigg\}\end{split}

We extend above definitions to
v_{q}
, denoted by
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\cdot]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{v_{q},\kappa}
and
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\cdot]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{v_{q},\kappa}
.

Now we can state the main results of this section.

Theorem 6.9
.

Fix some
q_{*}\leq q
. Suppose that, for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa_{q}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q}^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{q})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.2.6)

then

\displaystyle\left||\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{q})}-|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q+1},\kappa_{q+1})}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q+1}^{-\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{q})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.2.7)

Theorem 6.10
.

Fix some
q_{*}\leq q
. Suppose that, for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa_{q}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q}^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{q})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.2.8)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u_{q},\kappa_{q}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\left(1-\frac{4}{q}\right)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{q})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.2.9)

then for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q+1},\kappa_{q+1}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q+1},\kappa_{q+1})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.2.10)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u_{q+1},\kappa_{q+1}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\left(1-\frac{4}{q+1}\right)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q+1},\kappa_{q+1})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.2.11)

Remark 6.11
.

From the definitions of
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\cdot]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{\cdot,\cdot}
and
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\cdot]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{\cdot,\cdot}
, we see that the estimates in Theorem
6.10
and Theorem
6.9
are linear in
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}
.

The proofs of above theorems are postponed to Section
6.11
. To validate the estimates (
6.2.10
-
6.2.11
) for
D_{q+1,t}-\kappa_{q+1}\Delta
, we need two ingredients. One ingredient is unconditional energy estimates in Section
6.4
