. The other ingredient is homogenization which deduces estimates on
D_{q+1,t}-\kappa_{q+1}\Delta
from (
6.2.8
) on
D_{q,t}-\kappa_{q}\Delta
. This homogenization scheme is implemented in Section
6.5
, Section
6.6
, Section
6.8
, Section
6.10
and Section
6.11
.

For technical convenience, we also introduce the following notion for chain estimates.

Definition 6.12

(Chain estimate II)
.

Fix
\kappa>0
,
0\leq n_{*}\leq 2Q^{2}
and
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1])
. Consider
\{s_{n}\}
,
\{g_{n}\}
and
\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}\}
satisfying (
6.2.1
)-(
6.2.4
). Define the functions
\{\rho_{n}\}_{1\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}-n_{*}}
to be the solutions of

\begin{split}D_{q,t}\rho_{1}-\kappa\Delta\rho_{1}=&\,\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-1}\operatorname{div}(g_{0}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}}f),\\
\rho_{0}(\cdot,0)=&\,0,\\
\ldots&\,\\
D_{q,t}\rho_{n+1}-\kappa\Delta\rho_{n+1}=&\,\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-1}\operatorname{div}(g_{n}\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\rho_{n}),\\
\rho_{n+1}(\cdot,0)=&\,0,\\
\ldots&\,\end{split}

(6.2.12)

Then we define the notion
{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\cdot\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{u_{q},\kappa,n_{*}}
via

\begin{split}{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle f\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{u_{q},\kappa,n_{*}}:=\inf\bigg\{&\,R>0\,\bigg|\,\|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{q}^{-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}R,\quad\forall\,\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*},[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn_{*},\\
&\,\|\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{q}^{-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}R,\quad\forall\,\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*},[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Q(n+n_{*}),\\
&\,\text{for any }n,\{s_{n}\}_{n},\{g_{n}\}_{n},\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}\}_{n},\{\rho_{n}\}_{n}\text{ satisfying }\eqref{e:2:resolve}-\eqref{e:5:resolve},\eqref{e:FResolveRho}\bigg\}\end{split}

Similarly, we also define the alternative for propagating Hölder estimates

\begin{split}{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle f\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u_{q},\kappa,n_{*}}:=\inf\bigg\{&\,R>0\,\bigg|\,\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\leq\lambda_{q}^{-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}R,\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\leq\lambda_{q}^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}R,\\
&\,\text{for any }n,\{s_{n}\}_{n},\{g_{n}\}_{n},\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}\}_{n},\{\rho_{n}\}_{n}\text{ satisfying }\eqref{e:2:resolve}-\eqref{e:5:resolve},\eqref{e:FResolveRho}\bigg\}\end{split}

We also extend above definitions to
v_{q}
, denoted by
{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\cdot\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{v_{q},\kappa,n_{*}}
and
{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\cdot\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{v_{q},\kappa,n_{*}}
.

We have following immediate consequences for the notions above.

Lemma 6.13
.

For any
n_{1}\leq n_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa},

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle f\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{u_{q},\kappa,n_{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\,{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle f\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{u_{q},\kappa,n_{1}}.

This lemma is a direct consequence of the definitions.

Lemma 6.14
.

For
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we define
\{\rho_{n}\}_{n}
as in (
6.2.5
). Then, for any
0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\rho_{n}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{u_{q},\kappa,n}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q}^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa}.

Proof.

Comparing Definition
6.12
and Definition
6.8
concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.15
.

Suppose
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and
g\in\mathcal{P}_{q}(2NQ)
satisfies

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q}\leq\kappa\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{2}\lambda_{q}^{-s_{*}b\gamma}

for some
s_{*}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 2}
. Define

\begin{split}D_{q,t}\rho_{*}-\kappa\Delta\rho_{*}=&\,\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-1}\operatorname{div}\big(g\mathring{D}_{q}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}}\rho_{F}\big),\\
\rho_{F}(\cdot,0)=&\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\end{split}

(6.2.13)

for some
\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\omega}]\leq 3Q
, then we have

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\rho_{*}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{u_{q},\kappa,n_{0}+1}\leq

\displaystyle\,{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\rho_{F}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{u_{q},\kappa,n_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{-(s_{*}-1)b\gamma}+{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa}.

(6.2.14)

The proof of this lemma is immediate and analogous to Lemma
6.14
.

Remark 6.16
.

Analogous results to Lemma
6.13
, Lemma
6.14
and Lemma
6.15
hold for Hölder estimates
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u_{q},\kappa}
and
{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle f\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u_{q},\kappa,n_{*}}
and the
v_{q}
alternatives.

6.3.
Notations of this section

In this section, we introduce the following simplified notations.

Definition 6.17

(Parameters)
.

Define

\displaystyle\lambda:=\lambda_{q+1},\quad\delta:=\delta_{q+1},\quad\mu:=\mu_{q+1},\quad\bar{\lambda}:=\lambda_{q},\quad\bar{\delta}:=\delta_{q},\quad\bar{\mu}:=\mu_{q},

(6.3.1)

\displaystyle\mathring{\lambda}:=\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1},\quad\mathring{\mu}:=\mathring{\mu}_{q+1},\quad\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}:=\mathring{\lambda}_{q},\quad\mathring{\bar{\mu}}:=\mathring{\mu}_{q},

(6.3.2)

\displaystyle\bar{\kappa}:=\kappa\bigg(1+\frac{\delta}{\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}}\bigg),\quad\varepsilon:=\frac{\bar{\delta}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\bar{\lambda}^{1+2b\gamma_{R}}}{\mu}.

(6.3.3)

Definition 6.18

(Coefficient fields)
.

Recall the velocity fields
u_{q}
and
v_{q}
, the flow map
\bar{\Phi}_{q}
and
\Phi_{q}
constructed in Section
2.3
and Lemma
3.1
. Define the following functions,

\displaystyle\,\bar{u}:=u_{q},\quad u:=u_{q+1},\quad v:=v_{q+1},\quad\Phi:=\Phi_{q},\quad\phi_{*}:=\phi_{N},\quad\omega_{m}:=\omega_{q,m},

\displaystyle\,B_{m}:=B_{q,m},\quad E_{m}:=E_{q,m},\quad S_{m}:=S_{q,m},\quad\Omega_{m}:=\Omega_{q,m},\quad\omega_{*}:=\lambda^{3}\omega_{q,N}.

We introduce the following shorthand notations for operators, quantities and spaces,

\displaystyle O:=O_{q+1},\quad\mathring{O}:=\mathring{O}_{q+1},\quad\bar{D}:=

\displaystyle\,D_{q},\quad\mathring{\bar{D}}:=\mathring{D}_{q},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}:=\mathcal{P}_{q},\quad\mathcal{P}:=\mathcal{P}_{q+1},\quad{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}:=

\displaystyle\,{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q},\quad{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{+1}:={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q+1}.

We also define
Z,\Omega,\Xi:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}
via

\displaystyle\Omega(x,t,\tau):=

\displaystyle\,\omega_{*}(x,t)\phi_{*}(\tau),

(6.3.4)

\displaystyle Z(x,t,\tau):=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{m}\omega_{m}(x,t)z_{m}(\tau),

(6.3.5)

\displaystyle\Xi(x,t,\tau):=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{m}S_{m}(x,t)\vartheta_{m}(\tau).

(6.3.6)

In the whole Section
6
,
\sum_{m}
always means
\sum_{m=1}^{N}
for the integer
N
defined in Section
2.1
.

Consider the following equation for
\varrho:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}O_{q+1,t}\varrho-\kappa_{q+1}\Delta\varrho=&\,0,\\
\varrho(\cdot,0)=&\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{split}

(6.3.7)

From above definitions, we can rewrite (
6.3.7
) in the following form

\begin{split}\bar{D}_{t}\varrho-\operatorname{div}(A\nabla\varrho)=&0,\\
\varrho(\cdot,0)=&\rho_{\text{in}},\end{split}

(6.3.8)

with
A:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}
given by

\displaystyle A(x,t):=

\displaystyle\,\kappa\mathrm{Id}+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi(x,t)H(\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi^{T}(x,t)

(6.3.9)

with
H
an anti-symmetric matrix

\displaystyle H_{12}(\xi,\tau)=

\displaystyle\,\eta_{1}(\tau)\sin(2\pi\xi_{1})+\eta_{2}(\tau)\sin(2\pi\xi_{2})=-H_{21}(\xi,\tau),

(6.3.10)

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}(\xi)=

\displaystyle\,\sin(2\pi\xi_{1}),\quad\Pi_{2}(\xi)=\sin(2\pi\xi_{2}).

(6.3.11)

Remark 6.19
.

From above definitions, we have

\displaystyle\gamma>0,\quad\varepsilon<1,\quad\lambda_{q}<\lambda,\quad\delta_{q}>\delta.

(6.3.12)

From (
2.1.10
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\geq\max\Bigg\{\frac{\mu}{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}},\frac{\varepsilon\kappa\lambda^{2}}{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}},\frac{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}{\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}},\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2}}{\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda}\Bigg\}.

(6.3.13)

From (
2.1.6
) and (
2.1.9
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{2b\gamma+\gamma_{S}}\leq 1.

(6.3.14)

From (
2.1.7
) and (
2.1.12
), we also have

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mu}\leq\lambda^{-\gamma}.

(6.3.15)

Remark 6.20
.

Recalling Lemma
3.1
, above definitions lead to

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{t}\Phi=

\displaystyle\,Z(x,t,\mu t)+\lambda^{-3}\Omega(x,t,\mu t),

(6.3.16)

\displaystyle\Xi(x,t,\mu t)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\kappa}{\varepsilon}\big(\nabla\Phi\nabla\Phi^{T}-(\det\nabla\Phi)^{2}\mathrm{Id}\big)=\sum_{m=1}^{N}S_{m}(x,t)\vartheta_{m}(\mu t),

(6.3.17)

\displaystyle\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi\det\nabla\Phi=

\displaystyle\,\mathrm{Id}+\varepsilon\sum_{m=1}^{N}E_{m}\varphi_{m}(\mu t),\quad\nabla\Phi=\mathrm{Id}+\varepsilon\sum_{m=1}^{N}B_{m}\phi_{m}(\mu t),

(6.3.18)

\displaystyle(\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi H(\xi,\tau)

\displaystyle\,\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi^{T})_{ij}=H_{ij}(\xi,\tau)+\varepsilon\sum_{m=1}^{N}\sigma_{m}(\tau)\Omega_{m,ij}H_{12}(\xi,\tau).

(6.3.19)

From Lemma
3.1
, we have, for any
(x,t)\in\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}Z(x,t,\cdot)\cap\operatorname{supp}\eta_{1}=\operatorname{supp}Z(x,t,\cdot)\cap\operatorname{supp}\eta_{2}=\varnothing.

(6.3.20)

Recalling (
3.0.3
), we have, from (
6.3.9
) and (
6.3.17
),

\displaystyle A(x,t)=\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi\bigg(\kappa\mathrm{Id}+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}H(\lambda\Phi,\mu t)\bigg)\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi^{T}+\kappa\big(\mathrm{Id}-\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi^{T}\big).

(6.3.21)

We also have

\displaystyle\Phi(x,0)=x.

(6.3.22)

Remark 6.21

(Estimates on velocity)
.

Under Definition
6.17
and Definition
6.18
, we deduce from Lemma
3.1

\displaystyle\omega_{N},\omega_{*}\in(\mathcal{\bar{P}}(N))^{2},\quad B_{m},E_{m},S_{m},\Omega_{m}\in

\displaystyle\,(\mathcal{\bar{P}}(N))^{2\times 2}.

(6.3.23)

Moreover, for any
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
with
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq 8Q^{3}
and
p
with
p\leq 8Q^{3}
,

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}B_{m}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}E_{m}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}S_{m}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\Omega_{m}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\omega_{m}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\omega_{*}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,

(6.3.24)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\phi_{m}\|_{\infty},\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\phi_{*}\|_{\infty},\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\varphi_{m}\|_{\infty},\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}z_{m}\|_{\infty},\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\sigma_{m}\|_{\infty},\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\vartheta_{m}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1.

(6.3.25)

6.4.
Energy and stability estimates for chains

In this section, we prove unconditional energy estimates for equations like (
6.2.5
). We also prove stability estimates between
D_{q+1,t}-\kappa_{q+1}\Delta
and
O_{q+1,t}-\kappa_{q+1}\Delta
.

Assumption 6.22

(Step
q+1
with
\mathcal{O}
coefficient fields)
.

For initial datum
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, suppose that
\{g_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{q+1}(2NQ)
and
\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}\subset\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}]\leq 3Q
satisfying

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q+1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\kappa\mathring{\lambda}^{2}\lambda^{-s_{n}b\gamma}

(6.4.1)

for some
\{s_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}\subset\mathbb{R}_{\geq 2}
. Define the functions
\{\varrho_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}
to be the solutions to

\displaystyle O_{t}\varrho_{0}-\kappa\Delta\varrho_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.4.2)

\displaystyle\varrho_{0}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.4.3)

\displaystyle\ldots,

\displaystyle O_{t}\varrho_{n+1}-\kappa\Delta\varrho_{n+1}=

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\operatorname{div}}(g_{n}\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\varrho_{n}),

(6.4.4)

\displaystyle\varrho_{n+1}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,0.

(6.4.5)

For
\{\varrho_{n}\}
defined in Assumption
6.22
, we have the following energy estimates.

Proposition 6.23
.

Taking Assumption
6.22
, we have that, for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 12Q^{3}-3Qn
,

\begin{split}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{n}\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\varrho_{n}\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda^{-\big(2[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}s_{i}\big)b\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).\end{split}

(6.4.6)

From interpolation, we also have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{v,\kappa}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}^{1-2\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right),

(6.4.7)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{v,\kappa}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}^{(1-2\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).

(6.4.8)

Moreover, for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{n}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\mathring{\lambda}^{1-2\gamma}\lambda^{-\big(2[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}s_{i}\big)b\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).

(6.4.9)

Remark 6.24
.

The above result is presented for
\mathbb{T}^{2}
. For higher space dimensions, the additional power
\mathring{\lambda}^{1-2\gamma}
in (
6.4.8
) and (
6.4.9
) changes.

Proof.

From the smallness of
\gamma
given by (
2.1.6
), we apply energy estimates in Lemma
5.10
to deduce that, for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 12Q^{3}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{0}\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\varrho_{0}\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda^{-2[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).

From (
6.4.1
) and Corollary
5.14
, we have
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 12Q^{3}-3Q
,

\begin{split}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{1}\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\varrho_{1}\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda^{-(2[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+2[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}]-2+s_{0})b\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).\end{split}

Inductively, we have for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 12Q^{3}-3Qn
,

\begin{split}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{n}\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\varrho_{n}\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda^{-\big(2[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}+2[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{i}]-2)\big)b\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).\end{split}

To deduce (
6.4.7
) and (
6.4.9
), we apply the interpolation inequality in Lemma
9.2
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{p}\varrho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla^{k+p}\varrho_{n}\|^{s}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla^{p}\varrho_{n}\|^{1-s}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}},

(6.4.10)

with
s=Q^{-3}
,
k=Q^{3}
and
p\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn
. From (
6.4.6
) and (
6.4.10
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\nabla}^{p}\varrho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}^{1-2\gamma}\lambda^{-\big(2p+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}s_{i}\big)b\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).

(6.4.11)

When
\boldsymbol{\beta}
contains time derivatives, the following
L_{x,t}^{\infty}
estimate follows from the equation (
6.4.2
) or (
6.4.4
), commutator equalities and (
6.4.11
),

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}^{1-2\gamma}\lambda^{-\big(2[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}s_{i}\big)b\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).

(6.4.12)

To deduce (
6.4.8
), we apply the interpolation inequality (
9.2.4
) in Lemma
9.2
.

∎

Assumption 6.25

(Step
q+1
with
\mathcal{P}
coefficient fields)
.

Fix some initial datum
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Assume
\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}\subset\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}]\leq 3Q
and
\{h_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}\subset\mathcal{P}_{q+1}(2NQ)
satisfying

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q+1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\kappa\mathring{\lambda}^{2}\lambda^{-s_{n}b\gamma}

(6.4.13)

for some
\{s_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}\subset\mathbb{R}_{\geq 2}
Define the functions
\{\ddot{\varrho}_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}}
to be the solutions to

\displaystyle D_{t}\ddot{\varrho}_{0}-\kappa\Delta\ddot{\varrho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,\quad\ddot{\varrho}_{0}(\cdot,0)=\rho_{\mathrm{in}},

\displaystyle\ldots,

\displaystyle D_{t}\ddot{\varrho}_{n+1}-\kappa\Delta\ddot{\varrho}_{n+1}=

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\operatorname{div}}(h_{n}\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\ddot{\varrho}_{n}),\quad\ddot{\varrho}_{n+1}(\cdot,0)=0.

We also have the following energy estimates for
\{\ddot{\varrho}_{n}\}
defined in Assumption
6.25
. The proof is the same as the proof of Proposition
6.23
. We omit it here.

Proposition 6.26
.

Taking Assumption
6.25
, we have that, for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
,
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 12Q^{3}-3Qn
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\ddot{\varrho}_{n}\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\ddot{\varrho}_{n}\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\big(2[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}s_{i}\big)b\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).

(6.4.14)

From interpolation, we also have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u,\kappa}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}^{1-2\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right),

(6.4.15)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u,\kappa}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}^{(1-2\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).

(6.4.16)

Moreover, for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\ddot{\varrho}_{n}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\mathring{\lambda}^{1-2\gamma}\lambda^{-\big(2[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}s_{i}\big)b\gamma}\left(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}\right).

(6.4.17)

Remark 6.27
.

Compare (
6.4.14
) and the estimate in (
6.2.10
) following Definition
6.8
. We know that, for
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]>Q
and
n>Q
, the estimates needed in (
6.2.10
) are covered by Proposition
6.26
. Here, we use the fact
Q\gamma>3
from Section
2.1
.

Next, we prove the stability between the chain estimates for
D_{q+1,t}-\kappa_{q+1}\Delta
and
O_{q+1,t}-\kappa_{q+1}\Delta
.

Proposition 6.28
.

Suppose that, for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{v,\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.4.18)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{v,\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\left(1-\frac{4}{q}+\lambda^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}\right)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.4.19)

Then for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have the stability estimate for dissipation

\displaystyle\big||\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}-|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}\big|\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-2}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)

(6.4.20)

and the stability estimates for chains

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u,\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.4.21)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u,\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\left(1-\frac{4}{q+1}\right)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.4.22)

Proof.

Take any
\{g_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathcal{O}_{q+1}(2NQ)
,
\{h_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathcal{P}_{q+1}(2NQ)
and
\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}]\leq 3Q
satisfying

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q+1}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{q+1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\kappa\mathring{\lambda}^{2}\lambda^{-s_{n}b\gamma}.

(6.4.23)

Suppose that, for any
n\geq 0
,
g_{n}
and
h_{n}
are congruent. Define
\{\varrho_{n}\}_{n}
and
\{\ddot{\varrho}_{n}\}_{n}
in Assumption
6.22
and Assumption
6.25
. To simplify the notation, we introduce

\displaystyle\Box:=\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

Step 6.4.1

(Estimates on derivatives of high order)
.

We first prove

\displaystyle\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\ddot{\varrho}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.4.24)

\displaystyle\|\ddot{\varrho}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)

(6.4.25)

for
n\geq Q
or
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\geq Q
. Both follow from (
6.4.17
) in Proposition
6.26
and the sufficient gain in exponent
Q\gamma>3
.

Step 6.4.2

(Estimates on derivatives of relatively low order)
.

To prove (
6.4.24
) and (
6.4.25
) at low orders, we first make the following claim.

Claim 6.4.1
.

For any
0\leq n\leq Q
and any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn
, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\varrho_{n}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n})\|+&\,\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}(\varrho_{n}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n})\|_{2}\\
\lesssim&\,\lambda^{-\alpha-2-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box.\end{split}

(6.4.26)

Using Claim
6.4.1
and standard embedding estimates, we have for any
0\leq n\leq Q
and any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn-2
,

\begin{split}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\varrho_{n}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n})\|_{\infty}\lesssim&\,\lambda^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-\alpha-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box,\\
\|\varrho_{n}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim&\,\lambda^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box.\end{split}

(6.4.27)

Now we use (
6.4.27
) and (
6.4.18
)-(
6.4.19
) to conclude (
6.4.24
)-(
6.4.25
) for
0\leq n\leq Q
and
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q
, which yields (
6.4.21
) and (
6.4.22
). Here, we use the factor
\lambda^{-\gamma}
in (
6.4.27
) to absorb the constants in
\lesssim
.

Proof of (
6.4.20
) and Claim
6.4.1
.

From (
6.4.18
), we have for any
0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}
,
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn

\displaystyle\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{n}\|\lesssim\lambda^{-\alpha-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box.

(6.4.28)

Subsequently by Lemma
3.3
, Lemma
3.4
and Lemma
3.5
, we have that, for any
0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}((u-v)\varrho_{n})\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-3-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box,

(6.4.29)

\displaystyle\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}((u-v)\varrho_{n})\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-3-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box.

(6.4.30)

Observe that
(\varrho_{0}-\ddot{\varrho}_{0})(\cdot,0)=0
and

\displaystyle D_{t}(\varrho_{0}-\ddot{\varrho}_{0})-\kappa\Delta(\varrho_{0}-\ddot{\varrho}_{0})=(D_{t}-O_{t})\varrho_{0}=\operatorname{div}((u-v)\varrho_{0}).

(6.4.31)

Applying Lemma
5.13
, we deduce, for
0\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}
,

\begin{split}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\varrho_{0}-\ddot{\varrho}_{0})\|+&\,\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}(\varrho_{0}-\ddot{\varrho}_{0})\|_{2}\\
\lesssim&\,\kappa^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{-\alpha-3-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\Box.\end{split}

(6.4.32)

Then from the definition of dissipation in (
6.1.1
) and using negative power of
\lambda
to absorb the constants, we have

\begin{split}\big||\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}-|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\kappa)}\big|\leq\lambda^{-\alpha-2}\Box.\end{split}

(6.4.33)

Now we proceed with the induction. Assume following estimates for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn
,

\begin{split}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\varrho_{n}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n})\|+&\,\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}(\varrho_{n}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n})\|_{2}\\
\lesssim&\,\lambda^{-\alpha-2-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box.\end{split}

(6.4.34)

It is not difficult to see
\varrho_{n+1}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n+1}
satisfies

\begin{split}D_{t}(\varrho_{n+1}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n+1})-&\,\kappa\Delta(\varrho_{n+1}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n+1})\\
=&\,\mathring{\operatorname{div}}\big(\mathring{\lambda}(u-v)\varrho_{n+1}\big)+\mathring{\operatorname{div}}\big((g_{n}-h_{n})\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\varrho_{n}\big)\\
+&\,\mathring{\operatorname{div}}\big(h_{n}(\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}-\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}})\varrho_{n}\big)+\mathring{\operatorname{div}}\big(h_{n}\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}(\varrho_{n}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n})\big).\end{split}

(6.4.35)

Now we have for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Q(n+1)
,

\displaystyle\kappa^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big((u-v)\varrho_{n+1}\big)\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-2-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box,

(6.4.36)

\displaystyle\kappa^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}^{-1}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big((g_{n}-h_{n})\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\varrho_{n}\big)\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-2-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box,

(6.4.37)

\displaystyle\kappa^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}^{-1}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big(h_{n}(\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}-\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}})\varrho_{n}\big)\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-2-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box,

(6.4.38)

\displaystyle\kappa^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}^{-1}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big(h_{n}\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}(\varrho_{n}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n})\big)\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-2-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box.

(6.4.39)

Here, (
6.4.36
) follows from (
6.4.30
). (
6.4.37
) follows from (
6.4.28
) and Corollary
3.8
. (
6.4.38
) follows from (
6.4.28
) and Lemma
3.5
. (
6.4.39
) follows from (
6.4.34
) and Corollary
3.6
.

Applying energy estimates in Lemma
5.13
with (
6.4.35
-
6.4.39
), we can deduce that, for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Q(n+1)
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\varrho_{n+1}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n+1})\|+

\displaystyle\,\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}(\varrho_{n+1}-\ddot{\varrho}_{n+1})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-2-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Box.

This finishes the proof of Claim
6.4.1
.
∎

∎

6.5.
Statement of expansion lemmas

In this section, we state two core expansion lemmas for homogenization. These lemmas aim to study a single equation in (
6.4.2
-
6.4.5
).

Lemma 6.29

(The expansion lemma I)
.

For
N,Q
chosen in Section
2.1
, consider the equation (
6.3.8
)-(
6.3.9
), or equivalently (
6.4.2
)-(
6.4.3
), under Definition
6.17
and Definition
6.18
. Then the solution
\varrho
admits the expansion

\begin{split}\varrho(x,t)=&\sum_{n=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\Big(\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)+\check{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\mu t)+\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,t)\Big)+\tilde{\rho}(x,t),\end{split}

(6.5.1)

with
\hat{\rho}_{n},\check{\rho}_{n},\bar{\rho}_{n},\tilde{\rho}
given by the following.

(C.1)

(Spatial correctors)

\hat{\rho}_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
has the form

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.5.2)

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\xi,\tau)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,t)

(6.5.3)

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n}\in

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{\bar{P}}(N(n-1)+2-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}])

(6.5.4)

with
\hat{h}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}_{\cdot}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

and
\hat{\eta}_{\cdot}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying the estimates for

\displaystyle\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\,\leq 2(3N)^{k},

(6.5.5)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{k}

(6.5.6)

\displaystyle\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1}^{2k+1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{k+1}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{k}

(6.5.7)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 11Q^{3},\quad\langle\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}\rangle_{\xi}=0,

(6.5.8)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}\|_{\infty}

(6.5.9)

Here the sum of differentiation index

in (

such that

. Moreover,

2.5
for any

,

and

.

(C.2)

(Temporal correctors)

\check{\rho}_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
has the form

\displaystyle\check{\rho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.5.10)

\displaystyle\check{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\tau)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(x,t)\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\tau)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,t)

(6.5.11)

\displaystyle\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n}\in

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{\bar{P}}(N(n-1)+3-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\big)

(6.5.12)

with
\check{h}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}_{\cdot}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

satisfy the estimates for
k\geq 0

\displaystyle\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\,\leq 8(3N)^{k},

(6.5.13)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mu}\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{k},

(6.5.14)

\displaystyle\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1}^{2k+2}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{k+1}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mu}\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{k},

(6.5.15)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 11Q^{3}-k,\quad\langle\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}\rangle_{\tau}=0.

(6.5.16)

(C.3)

(Residual correctors)

\bar{\rho}_{0},\{\bar{\rho}_{n}\}_{1\leq n\leq N}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
solve

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.5.17)

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{n}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)+1}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\Big(\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n-a}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}\Big)

(6.5.18)

\displaystyle\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n}\in

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{\bar{P}}(Nn+3-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}])

(6.5.19)

with

given by

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}=\bar{D}_{t}-\bar{\kappa}\Delta

(6.5.20)

and initial datum given by

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{0}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{S}\rho_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.5.21)

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,-\mathbb{S}[\check{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0,0)].

(6.5.22)

Here,
the vector-valued functions

\bar{h}^{(\cdot)}_{\cdot}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfy the estimates for

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\kappa\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\bigg)^{2}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{k+1}.

(6.5.23)

\displaystyle\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1}^{2k+3}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\kappa\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\bigg)^{2}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{k+1}.

(6.5.24)

(C.4)

(remainder)

\tilde{\rho}
solves the following equation

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{t}\tilde{\rho}-\operatorname{div}(A\nabla\tilde{\rho})=

\displaystyle\,\tilde{f}

(6.5.25)

with initial datum

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}(x,0)=

\displaystyle\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}(x)-\mathbb{S}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}(x)+\sum_{a=1}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\big(\mathbb{S}[\check{\rho}_{a}(\cdot,0,0)]-\check{\rho}_{a}(\cdot,0,0)\big).

(6.5.26)

Here,
\tilde{f}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{f}:=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}\Big(L_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{Q}+\check{\rho}_{Q}+\bar{\rho}_{Q})+L_{0}(\hat{\rho}_{Q-1}+\check{\rho}_{Q-1}+\bar{\rho}_{Q-1})

(6.5.27)

\displaystyle\,\quad+L_{-1}\hat{\rho}_{Q-2}\Big)(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)

(6.5.28)

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q+1}}\Big((L_{0}(\hat{\rho}_{Q}+\check{\rho}_{Q}+\bar{\rho}_{Q})+L_{-1}\hat{\rho}_{Q-1}\Big)(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)

(6.5.29)

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q+2}}\Big(L_{-1}\hat{\rho}_{Q}\Big)(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)

(6.5.30)

and differential operators
L_{1},L_{0},L_{-1}
acting on general functions
\rho
taking arguments
(x,t,\xi,\tau)
are given by

\displaystyle L_{1}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\partial_{\xi_{i}}H_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\partial_{\tau}\rho+\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}S_{m,ij}\vartheta_{m}\partial_{\xi_{i}\xi_{j}}\rho

(6.5.31)

\displaystyle L_{0}\rho=

\displaystyle\,\bar{D}_{t}\rho-\kappa\Delta_{x}\rho-\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\sum_{m}\sigma_{m}\partial_{x_{i}}\Omega_{m,ij}H_{12}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho-\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}\partial_{\xi_{i}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho

(6.5.32)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}\partial_{x_{j}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho-\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}\phi_{m}\partial_{x_{j}}B_{m,ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho-2\kappa\lambda\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{i}}\rho

(6.5.33)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,2\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}\phi_{m}B_{m,ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{j}}\rho

(6.5.34)

\displaystyle L_{-1}\rho=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\lambda}\phi_{*}\omega_{*}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho.

(6.5.35)

Remark 6.30
.

For spatial and temporal correctors, we may have none, one, or multiple correctors for fixed
n,a
and differentiation index
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
. For spatial correctors in (
6.5.3
), the cardinality of such correctors is denoted by
\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\in\mathbb{N}
. When
\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}=0
, this means we do not have correctors for such
n,a
and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
. The same also applies to temporal correctors.

Remark 6.31

(Stationarity of the correctors)
.

Note that the structural information of these correctors, such as
\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
,
\hat{h}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\hat{\eta}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
and
\hat{\chi}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
depend on the integer
n-a
, instead of
n
or
a
. For example, this means the structural information in first order corrector
\hat{\rho}_{1}
is shared in higher order correctors
\hat{\rho}_{n}
with
n\geq 2
. We refer this property as the
stationarity
of the correctors. We state the expansion lemma in a way that stationarity is claimed implicitly. However, this property does not come for free, and we shall prove it later.

We also have the following lemmas to estimate the initial datum of macroscopic states
\bar{\rho}_{n}
in Lemma
6.29
.

Corollary 6.32
.

Given the assumptions in Lemma
6.29
, then we have, for any
1\leq n\leq Q
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\check{\rho}_{n}(x,0,0)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{4n+1}\mathfrak{C}_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,0)\bigg)

(6.5.36)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,0)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{4n+1}\mathfrak{C}_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\,\mathbb{S}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,0)\bigg).

(6.5.37)

For
p\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{p}_{t}\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,0)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{4n+2p+1}\mathfrak{C}_{n,a}^{(p,\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,0)\bigg),

(6.5.38)

with the following estimates for the constant coefficients

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{C}_{n,a}^{(p,\boldsymbol{\alpha})}|\lesssim\lambda^{-(n-a)b\gamma}.

(6.5.39)

Proof.

This corollary is a direct consequence of (
6.5.18
). When
t\in[0,8\bar{\mu}^{-1}]
, the velocity field in
\bar{D}_{t}
is zero from (
2.3.18
), hence
\nabla
and
\bar{D}_{t}
commute at time
t=0
. For
n=0
, we can deduce that, from (
6.5.17
),

\displaystyle\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p}\bar{\rho}_{0}(x,0)=\bigg(\frac{\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}\bigg)^{p}\mathring{\bar{\Delta}}^{p}\bar{\rho}_{0}(x,0).

Using (
2.1.8
), we can deduce (
6.5.38
)-(
6.5.39
) for
n=0
.

For
n\geq 1
, we prove (
6.5.38
)-(
6.5.39
) by induction. Assume the desired estimates hold for
a\leq n
. We use (
6.5.11
) to derive the desired equality in (
6.5.38
) for
p=0
. We write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n+1}}\check{\rho}_{n+1}(x,0,0)=\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n+1-a)}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}_{n+1-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{n+1-a}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1-a}\bigg)(x,0)\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1-a}(0)\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}\bigg)(x,0).

(6.5.40)

Using the induction hypothesis for
a\leq n
, (
6.5.14
) and (
6.5.16
), this yields a representation of
\lambda^{-(n+1)}\check{\rho}_{n+1}(x,0,0)
analogous to (
6.5.38
), with constants satisfying (
6.5.39
). Here, we use Remark
2.16
to deduce that
\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1-a}
in (
6.5.40
) is constant in
x
when
t=0
. Then from (
6.5.22
) and Remark
6.6
, we deduce (
6.5.38
) and (
6.5.39
) for
n+1
and
p=0
.

When
p\geq 1
, we can deduce from (
6.5.18
)

\displaystyle\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p}\bar{\rho}_{n+1}(x,0)-\frac{\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p-1}\mathring{\bar{\Delta}}\bar{\rho}_{n+1}(x,0)=\frac{1}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)+3}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\Big(\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n-a+1}(x,0)\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p-1}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,0)\Big).

(6.5.41)

Here, note that
\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n+1-a}(x,t)
is constant when
t
is suitably small, from Remark
2.16
. Now we can deduce the representation in (
6.5.38
) for
\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p}\bar{\rho}_{n+1}(x,0)
by another induction on
p
. The estimates on constants (
6.5.39
) follow from induction assumptions (
6.3.14
) and (
6.5.23
).

From the parameters relations, including (
2.1.8
), (
6.5.24
) and induction assumptions, we can deduce (
6.5.39
) for
n+1
from the induction hypothesis.

∎

Lemma 6.33
.

Given the assumptions in Lemma
6.29
, we have that, for any
j\leq q
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\|\bar{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0)\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\|\check{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0,0)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\lambda^{-nb\gamma}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{-2b\gamma}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(q)},

(6.5.42)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}|\bar{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}|\check{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(j)}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\lambda^{-nb\gamma}\Bigg(\frac{\mathring{\lambda}_{j}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{q}}\Bigg)|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}.

(6.5.43)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\big|\mathbb{S}[\check{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0,0)]-\check{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0,0)\big|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\lambda^{-(Q+n)b\gamma}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(q+1)}.

(6.5.44)

Proof.

The
\check{\rho}_{n}
part of (
6.5.43
) follow from (
6.5.36
), Remark
6.3
. Then the
\bar{\rho}_{n}
part of (
6.5.43
) follows from Definition
6.5
and (
6.5.22
). (
6.5.44
) follows from (
6.1.9
) and Remark
6.3
.

∎

To study equations like (
6.4.4
)-(
6.4.5
), we also need to consider
O_{t}-\kappa\Delta
with forces. Here, we specify a typical force containing small scales
\lambda\Phi
and
\mu t
.

Assumption 6.34

(Small scale force)
.

Define a force
F:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
via

\displaystyle F(x,t)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,t)\sum_{l=1}^{J}h_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(x,t)\eta_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi(x,t))

The collection of functions
\{\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\}_{k}
satisfies transmission condition at step
q
. For some
\Upsilon>0
,
P\in\mathbb{N}
, the
vector-valued functions

h_{F}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfying

\displaystyle h_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\in[\mathcal{\bar{P}}(Q)]^{2},

(6.5.45)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1}^{Q}\sum_{l=1}^{J}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\kappa\lambda\mathring{\lambda}\Upsilon

(6.5.46)

and other scalar-valued functions satisfying

\displaystyle\|\partial^{p}_{\tau}\eta_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,1\quad p\leq P,

(6.5.47)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{p}_{\xi}\chi_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,1\quad p\leq P.

(6.5.48)

Moreover, we assume
\big(\chi_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\eta_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\big)
satisfies generalized shear condition for any
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
and
l
.

Lemma 6.35

(The expansion lemma II)
.

For
N,Q
chosen in Section
2.1
and any force
F
given by Assumption
6.34
, consider the equation

\begin{split}\bar{D}_{t}\varrho-\operatorname{div}(A\nabla\varrho)=&\,\mathring{\lambda}^{-1}\operatorname{div}F,\\
\varrho(\cdot,0)=&0\end{split}

(6.5.49)

with (
6.3.9
) in
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]
under Definition
6.17
and Definition
6.18
. Then the solution
\varrho
admits the following expansion

\begin{split}\varrho(x,t)=&\sum_{n=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\Big(\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)+\check{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\mu t)+\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,t)\Big)+\tilde{\rho}(x,t),\end{split}

(6.5.50)

with
\hat{\rho}_{n},\check{\rho}_{n},\bar{\rho}_{n},\tilde{\rho}
given by the following information.

(D.1)

(Spatial correctors)

\hat{\rho}_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
has the form

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.5.51)

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\xi,\tau)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,t)

(6.5.52)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-2+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,t)

(6.5.53)

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,n}\in

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{\bar{P}}(Q+N(n-1)+1)

(6.5.54)

with
\hat{h}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}_{\cdot}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
\hat{\chi}_{\cdot}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\hat{\eta}_{\cdot}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfy the estimates for

\displaystyle\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}

\displaystyle\,\leq J(3N)^{n-1}

(6.5.55)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\lambda^{2}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{n-2}\Upsilon,

(6.5.56)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1}^{2n-2+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\lambda^{2}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{n-2}\Upsilon,

(6.5.57)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq P,\quad\langle\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\rangle_{\xi}=0,

(6.5.58)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq P+1-n.

(6.5.59)

Here the sum of differentiation index
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
in (

) is among all indices in
\mathcal{I}_{*}
such that

(D.2)

(Temporal correctors)

has the form

\displaystyle\,0,

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{\bar{P}}(Q+N(n-1)+2)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{n-1}\Upsilon,

(6.5.65)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1}^{2n-1+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}\bigg)^{n-1}\Upsilon,

(6.5.69)

Remark 6.36
.

The
\hat{I}_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
,
\hat{h}_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\hat{\eta}_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\hat{\chi}_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\check{I}_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
,

,
\check{\eta}_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\bar{h}_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
in Lemma

and Lemma

are the same, i.e. they do not depend on

in Lemma

and the forcing terms in Lemma

. We shall prove this in Section

. However, the

in Lemma

and Lemma

are different, since they solve different equations, (

) respectively.

Corollary 6.37
.

Taking assumptions in Lemma
6.35
, suppose

\displaystyle\Upsilon=\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-2b\gamma}.

Then for any
0\leq n\leq Q
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\check{\rho}_{n}(x,0,0)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{4n+1}\mathfrak{C}_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,0)\bigg)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{4Q+1}\mathfrak{C}_{F,n,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,0)

(6.5.76)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,0)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{4n+1}\mathfrak{C}_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\,\mathbb{S}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,0)\bigg)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{4Q+1}\mathfrak{C}_{F,n,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\,\mathbb{S}\Big(\mathring{\bar{\partial}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,0)\Big).

(6.5.77)

For
p\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{p}_{t}\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,0)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{4n+2p+1}\mathfrak{C}_{n,a}^{(p,\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,0)\bigg)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}^{4Q+2p+1}\mathfrak{C}_{F,n,k}^{(p,\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,0),

(6.5.78)

with the following estimates for the constant coefficients

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{C}_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}|+|\mathfrak{C}_{n,a}^{(p,\boldsymbol{\alpha})}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(n-a)b\gamma},

(6.5.79)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{C}_{F,n,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}|+|\mathfrak{C}_{F,n,k}^{(p,\boldsymbol{\alpha})}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(n+2)b\gamma}.

(6.5.80)

Proof.

The proof is inductive and analogous to the proof of Corollary
6.32
. Similar to Corollary
6.32
,
\nabla
and
\bar{D}_{t}
commute at
t=0
. For
n=0
, (
6.5.76
-
6.5.77
) and (
6.5.79
-
6.5.80
) follow from (
6.5.60
) and (
6.5.71
). From (
6.5.68
) and (
6.5.71
), we have

\displaystyle\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p}\bar{\rho}_{0}(x,0)-\frac{\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p-1}\mathring{\bar{\Delta}}\bar{\rho}_{0}(x,0)=\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}\mathring{\lambda}}\sum_{k,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\Big(h_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(x,0)\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p-1}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,0)\Big)\langle\eta_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\chi_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}.

(6.5.81)

From (
2.1.8
), (
6.5.74
) and Assumption
6.34
, we can derive

\displaystyle\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}\mathring{\lambda}}\big|h_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(x,0)\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\bar{\kappa}\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{-2b\gamma}.

(6.5.82)

Note that
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
in (
6.5.81
) may contain both space derivatives and transport derivatives. To derive (
6.5.78
-
6.5.80
) for
n=0
, we do an induction on
p
and use transmission condition of
\{\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\}_{k}
in Assumption
6.34
to express transport derivative in space derivatives.

Next, assume the desired equalities and estimates for
a\leq n
. We use (
6.5.61
)-(
6.5.62
) to derive the desired equality in (
6.5.76
). We write

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n+1}}\check{\rho}_{n+1}(x,0,0)=&\,\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n+1-a)}\sum_{l}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{n+1-a}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1-a}\bigg)(x,0)\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1-a}(0)\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}\bigg)(x,0)\\
+&\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+1+Q}\sum_{l}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{n+1}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+1}\bigg)(x,0)\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+1}(0)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,0).\end{split}

(6.5.83)

Using the induction hypothesis, (
6.5.14
) and (
6.5.65
), we deduce (
6.5.76
). Here, we use Remark
2.16
. To express the transport derivatives
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
in (
6.5.83
) into space derivatives, we use transmission condition of
\{\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\}_{k}
in Assumption
6.34
and (
6.5.78
). (
6.5.77
) for
n+1
follows from (
6.5.72
) and (
6.5.76
).

From (
6.5.69
) and (
6.5.72
), we have

\begin{split}\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p}\bar{\rho}_{n+1}(x,0)-\frac{\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p-1}\mathring{\bar{\Delta}}\bar{\rho}_{n+1}(x,0)=&\,\frac{1}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)+3}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\Big(\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n-a+1}(x,0)\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p-1}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,0)\Big)\\
+&\,\frac{1}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+Q}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\Big(\bar{h}_{F,k,n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}(x,0)\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}^{p-1}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,0)\Big).\end{split}

(6.5.84)

From the parameter relations in (
2.1.8
), (
6.5.24
) and (
6.5.74
), we can derive

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}\,\big|\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n-a+1}(x,0)\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(n-a)b\gamma},

(6.5.85)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}\,\big|\bar{h}_{F,k,n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}(x,0)\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(n+2)b\gamma}.

(6.5.86)

Then (
6.5.78
) for
n+1
follows from an induction on
p
based on (
6.5.78
-
6.5.80
) and (
6.5.84
). Again, we use that
\nabla
and
\bar{D}_{t}
commute at
t=0
and
\bar{h}^{(\cdot)}_{\cdot}(\cdot,0)
are constant functions. We also use the transmission condition of
\{\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\}_{k}
in Assumption
6.34
.
∎

Lemma 6.38
.

Given the assumptions in Lemma
6.35
, for some
\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}>0
, suppose we have

\displaystyle|\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(\cdot,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}(q_{*})}+|\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(\cdot,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}(q+1)}\lesssim\lambda^{-(s-2)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},\quad\Upsilon=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-2b\gamma}.

\displaystyle\|\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(\cdot,0)\|_{2}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\gamma}|\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(\cdot,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\lesssim\lambda^{-(s-2)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},\quad\Upsilon=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-2b\gamma}.

Then we have for
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\bar{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0)\Big\|_{2}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}\Big|\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\bar{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0)\Big|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\lambda^{-(n+s)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.5.87)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}\Big|\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\check{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0,0)\Big|_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(j)}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\lambda^{-(n+s)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.5.88)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\big|\mathbb{S}[\check{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0,0)]-\check{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0,0)\big|_{\mathfrak{P}(q+1)}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\lambda^{-(Q+n+s)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.5.89)

Here,
\{\bar{\rho}_{a}\}_{a}
is given in (
6.5.68
) and (
6.5.69
) with initial conditions in (
6.5.71
)-(
6.5.72
).

Proof.

The proof is completely analogous to the proof of Lemma
6.33
and follows from Lemma
6.35
and Corollary
6.37
.
∎

6.6.
Proof of the expansion lemmas

In this section, we prove the expansion Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35
, also Remark
6.31
and Remark
6.36
, in three steps.

The first step is given in Section
6.6.1
, we show that it suffices to solve two asymptotic systems of differential equations, respectively for Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35
.

The asymptotic systems, obtained in Section
6.6.1
are solved by induction. The second step is to solve the initial steps of these asymptotic systems for Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35
. This is done separately in Section
6.6.2
and Section
6.6.3
.

The third step is the induction for solving the asymptotic systems for both expansion lemmas. Specifically, we solve for residual correctors, temporal correctors and spatial correctors, respectively in Section
6.6.4
, Section
6.6.5
and Section
6.6.6
. The proofs of Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35
are completely analogous, so we only present it for Lemma
6.29
and comment on their minor difference.

6.6.1.
Set up the asymptotic system for Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35

We write (
6.3.8
) in the following form

\displaystyle L\varrho=\bar{D}_{t}\varrho-\operatorname{div}(A\nabla\varrho)=0,

(6.6.1)

We make an
ansatz
that
\varrho
admits an expansion of the following form

\displaystyle\varrho(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\rho_{n}(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)+\tilde{\rho}(x,t).

(6.6.2)

for some function
\rho_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
.

Define
\hat{\rho}_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with arguments
(x,t,\xi,\tau)
and
\check{\rho}_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with arguments
(x,t,\tau)
via

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,t):=

\displaystyle\,\langle\rho_{n}(x,t,\cdot,\cdot)\rangle_{\xi,\tau},

(6.6.3)

\displaystyle\check{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\tau):=

\displaystyle\,\langle\rho_{n}(x,t,\cdot,\tau)\rangle_{\xi}-\langle\rho_{n}(x,t,\cdot,\cdot)\rangle_{\xi,\tau},

(6.6.4)

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\xi,\tau):=

\displaystyle\,\rho_{n}(x,t,\xi,\tau)-\langle\rho_{n}(x,t,\cdot,\tau)\rangle_{\xi}.

(6.6.5)

Then we have the natural decomposition for
\rho_{n}
with
n\geq 0

\displaystyle\rho_{n}(x,t,\xi,\tau)=\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\xi,\tau)+\check{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\tau)+\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,t)

(6.6.6)

and subsequently

\displaystyle\varrho(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\big(\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)+\check{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\mu t)+\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,t)\big)+\tilde{\rho}(x,t).

(6.6.7)

We have, for any
x,t
,

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\cdot,\tau)\rangle_{\xi}=0\text{ for any }\tau,\quad\langle\check{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\cdot)\rangle_{\tau}=0.

(6.6.8)

Lemma 6.39
.

Under Definition
6.17
and Definition
6.18
, for a general function
\varrho:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
given by

\displaystyle\varrho(x,t)=\rho(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t),

(6.6.9)

not necessarily solving (
6.3.8
), we have

\displaystyle L\varrho=\lambda^{2}L_{2}\rho+\lambda L_{1}\rho+L_{0}\rho+\lambda^{-1}L_{-1}\rho

(6.6.10)

with

\displaystyle L_{2}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\kappa\Delta_{\xi}\rho-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}(\det\nabla\Phi)^{2}\operatorname{div}_{\xi}H\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho+\frac{1}{\lambda}Z\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho,

\displaystyle L_{1}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\partial_{\xi_{i}}H_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\partial_{\tau}\rho+\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}S_{m,ij}\vartheta_{m}\partial_{\xi_{i}\xi_{j}}\rho:=L_{11}\rho+L_{12}\rho+L_{13}\rho

\displaystyle L_{0}\rho=

\displaystyle\,\bar{D}_{t}\rho-\kappa\Delta_{x}\rho-\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\sum_{m}\sigma_{m}\partial_{x_{i}}\Omega_{m,ij}H_{12}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho-\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}\partial_{\xi_{i}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}\partial_{x_{j}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho-\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}\phi_{m}\partial_{x_{j}}B_{m,ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho-2\kappa\lambda\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{i}}\rho

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,2\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}\phi_{m}B_{m,ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{j}}\rho:=\sum_{1\leq k\leq 7}L_{0k}\rho

\displaystyle L_{-1}\rho=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\lambda}\phi_{*}\omega_{*}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho.

Note that
L_{01}\rho=D_{t}\rho-\kappa\Delta_{x}\rho
, and
L_{0k},k\geq 2
is defined by the order of relevant terms.

Proof.

We omit the argument
(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)
for the function
\rho
in this proof. From (
6.6.9
) and (
6.3.9
), we compute the elliptic part

\begin{split}\operatorname{div}(A\nabla\varrho)=&\,\lambda^{2}\operatorname{div}_{\xi}(\nabla\Phi A\nabla\Phi^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\rho)+\lambda\operatorname{div}_{\xi}(\nabla\Phi A\nabla_{x}\rho)\\
&\,+\lambda\operatorname{div}_{x}(A\nabla\Phi^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\rho)+\operatorname{div}_{x}(A\nabla_{x}\rho).\end{split}

(6.6.11)

Using (
6.3.9
), we compute

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}_{\xi}(\nabla\Phi A\nabla\Phi^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\rho)=

\displaystyle\,\kappa(\nabla\Phi\nabla\Phi^{T})_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}\xi_{j}}\rho+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}(\det\nabla\Phi)^{2}\partial_{\xi_{i}}H_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{j}}\rho

(6.6.12)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\kappa\Delta_{\xi}\rho+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}(\det\nabla\Phi)^{2}\partial_{\xi_{i}}H_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{j}}\rho+\kappa(\nabla\Phi\nabla\Phi^{T}-\mathrm{Id})_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}\xi_{j}}\rho,

(6.6.13)

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}_{\xi}(\nabla\Phi A

\displaystyle\,\nabla_{x}\rho)+\operatorname{div}_{x}(A\nabla\Phi^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\rho)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(\nabla\Phi A+\nabla\Phi A^{T})_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{j}}\rho+\partial_{\xi_{i}}(\nabla\Phi A)_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\partial_{x_{j}}(A\nabla\Phi^{T})_{ji}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho

(6.6.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,2\kappa\nabla\Phi_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{j}}\rho+\kappa\partial_{x_{j}}\nabla\Phi_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\partial_{\xi_{i}}(\det\nabla\Phi H\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho

(6.6.15)

\displaystyle\,+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\partial_{x_{j}}(\det\nabla\Phi\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi H)_{ji}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho,

(6.6.16)

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}_{x}(A\nabla_{x}\rho)=\kappa\Delta_{x}\rho+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\partial_{x_{i}}(\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi H\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho.

(6.6.17)

For the transport term, we deduce from (
6.6.9
) and (
6.3.16
), that

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{t}\varrho=

\displaystyle\,\mu\partial_{\tau}\rho+\partial_{t}\rho+\lambda\partial_{t}\Phi_{i}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho+\bar{u}\cdot\nabla_{x}\rho+\lambda\bar{u}\cdot\nabla\Phi_{i}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mu\partial_{\tau}\rho+\bar{D}_{t}\rho+\lambda\bar{D}_{t}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mu\partial_{\tau}\rho+\bar{D}_{t}\rho+\lambda Z\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho+\lambda^{-2}\Omega\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho.

(6.6.18)

From (
6.6.1
), (

), (
6.6.11
-
6.6.18
), (
6.3.4
-
6.3.6
) and (

-
6.3.19
), we have (
6.6.10
) with

\displaystyle L_{2}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\kappa\Delta_{\xi}\rho-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}(\det\nabla\Phi)^{2}\operatorname{div}_{\xi}H\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho+\frac{1}{\lambda}Z\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho,

(6.6.19)

\displaystyle L_{1}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\partial_{\xi_{i}}H_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\partial_{\tau}\rho+\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\operatorname{div}_{\xi}(\Xi\nabla_{\xi}\rho),

(6.6.20)

\displaystyle L_{0}\rho=

\displaystyle\,\bar{D}_{t}\rho-\kappa\Delta_{x}\rho-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\partial_{x_{i}}(\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi H\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho-\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}\partial_{\xi_{i}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho

(6.6.21)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}\partial_{x_{j}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho-\kappa\lambda\partial_{x_{j}}\nabla\Phi_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho-2\kappa\lambda\nabla\Phi_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{j}}\rho,

(6.6.22)

\displaystyle L_{-1}\rho=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\lambda}\Omega\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho.

(6.6.23)

In particular, we remark that, in above computation, we decompose the last term in line (
6.6.15
) into two terms, with one being the first term in line (
6.6.20
), the other one being the last term in line (
6.6.21
). Then we use (
6.3.4
), (
6.3.18
) and (
6.3.19
) and above to conclude the proof.
∎

For the proof of Lemma

\begin{split}L\varrho=&\,\lambda^{2}L_{2}\rho_{0}+\lambda(L_{2}\rho_{1}+L_{1}\rho_{0})+(L_{2}\rho_{2}+L_{1}\rho_{1}+L_{0}\rho_{0})\\

Remark 6.40
.

For
\rho(x,t,\xi,\tau)=\hat{\rho}(x,t,\xi,\tau)+\check{\rho}(x,t,\tau)+\bar{\rho}(x,t)
with

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\rho}(x,t,\cdot,\tau)\rangle_{\xi}=0\text{ for any }x,t,\tau,\quad\langle\check{\rho}(x,t,\cdot)\rangle_{\tau}=0\text{ for any }x,t,

(6.6.44)

we have

\displaystyle\langle L_{2}\rho\rangle_{\xi}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.6.45)

\displaystyle L_{1}\rho=

\displaystyle\,L_{11}(\hat{\rho}+\check{\rho}+\bar{\rho})+L_{12}(\hat{\rho}+\check{\rho})+L_{13}\hat{\rho},

(6.6.46)

\displaystyle\langle L_{1}\rho\rangle_{\xi}=

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi}+L_{12}\check{\rho},

(6.6.47)

\displaystyle\langle L_{1}\rho\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}.

(6.6.48)

Note that
\langle H(\xi,\tau)\rangle_{\xi}=0
, then we also have

\displaystyle L_{0}\rho=

\displaystyle\,(L_{01}+L_{02}+L_{03})(\hat{\rho}+\check{\rho}+\bar{\rho})+L_{04}\hat{\rho}+L_{05}\hat{\rho}+L_{06}\hat{\rho}+L_{07}\hat{\rho}

(6.6.49)

\displaystyle\langle L_{0}\rho\rangle_{\xi}=

\displaystyle\,L_{01}\check{\rho}+L_{01}\bar{\rho}+\langle L_{02}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi}+\langle L_{03}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi}+\langle L_{04}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi},

(6.6.50)

\displaystyle\langle L_{0}\rho\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=

\displaystyle\,L_{01}\bar{\rho}+\langle L_{02}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle L_{03}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle L_{04}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}.

(6.6.51)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\langle L_{-1}\rho\rangle_{\xi}=0.

(6.6.52)

Remark 6.41

(Disjoint support of
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
)
.

When solving an differential equation in
\xi
of form

\displaystyle L_{2}\rho=f_{1}(x,t)g_{1}(\xi_{1})\eta_{1}(\tau)+f_{2}(x,t)g_{2}(\xi_{2})\eta_{2}(\tau),

(6.6.53)

with general smooth functions
f_{1},f_{2}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]
,
g_{1},g_{2}:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}
given by Lemma
2.3
. We use the fact that
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
has disjoint support. We claim that a solution to (
6.6.53
) equal to the solution of

\displaystyle-\kappa\Delta_{\xi}\rho_{*}=f_{1}(x,t)g_{1}(\xi_{1})\eta_{1}(\tau)+f_{2}(x,t)g_{2}(\xi_{2})\eta_{2}(\tau).

(6.6.54)

Indeed, the solution of (
6.6.54
) is given by

\displaystyle\rho_{*}=-f_{1}\eta_{1}\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{-2}g_{1}-f_{2}\eta_{2}\partial_{\xi_{2}}^{-2}g_{1}.

(6.6.55)

From Definition
6.18
, (
6.3.10
), (
6.3.11
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}H\cdot\nabla_{\xi}=

\displaystyle\,\eta_{2}\partial_{\xi_{2}}\Pi_{2}\partial_{\xi_{1}}-\eta_{1}\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\partial_{\xi_{2}},

(6.6.56)

\displaystyle Z(x,t,\tau)\eta_{1}=

\displaystyle\,Z(x,t,\tau)\eta_{2}=0

(6.6.57)

therefore we have
\operatorname{div}H\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho_{*}=Z\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho_{*}=0
. Thus the solution to (
6.6.53
) is given by
\rho=\rho_{*}
.

6.6.2.
Solve the asymptotic system: initial steps for Lemma
6.29

), (

) and (

Recalling Remark

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{1}(x,t,\xi,\tau)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mathring{\lambda}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\sum_{l=1}^{J}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}_{x}\Big(h_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\Big)\big(\eta_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\langle\eta_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\tau}\big)\langle\chi_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi}

\displaystyle\,+\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{1}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{1}\rangle_{\xi}

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mu}\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\tau}^{-1}\big(\eta_{1}^{2}-\langle\eta_{1}^{2}\rangle_{\tau}\big)\langle\Pi_{1}\partial_{\xi_{1}}\hat{\chi}_{1}^{(2,1)}\rangle_{\xi}\mathring{\bar{D}}_{22}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(6.6.92)

(6.6.93)

\displaystyle\,+\frac{\lambda}{\mu}\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mathring{\lambda}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\sum_{l=1}^{J}h_{F,k,j}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\partial_{\tau}^{-1}\big(\eta_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\langle\eta_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\tau}\big)\langle\chi_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{j}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}

6.6.4.
Induction assumptions and residual correctors

For the induction step, we solve (

For Lemma

, we impose the following inductive assumptions

\begin{split}\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\xi,\tau)=&\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,t)\\
+&\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-2+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{F,n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,t)\\
\hat{\rho}_{n+1}(x,t,\xi,\tau)=&\sum_{a=0}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)+1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a+1}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a+1}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a+1}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,t)\\
+&\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{F,n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+1}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+1}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+1}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,t)\\
\hat{\rho}_{n+2}(x,t,\xi,\tau)=&\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+3}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n+2}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+2}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+2}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+2}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}(x,t)\\
+&\,\sum_{a=1}^{n}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)+3}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a+2}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a+2}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a+2}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a+2}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,t)\\

+&\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+2+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n+2}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+2}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+2}(\tau)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+2}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(x,t)\\
:=&\hat{\rho}_{n+2,0}+\hat{\rho}_{n+2,M}+\hat{\rho}_{n+2,n+1}+\hat{\rho}_{n+2,F}\\
:=&\hat{\rho}_{n+2,0}+\hat{\rho}_{n+2,E}+\hat{\rho}_{n+2,F}:=\hat{\rho}_{n+2,R}+\hat{\rho}_{n+2,F}.\end{split}

(6.6.118)

and

\begin{split}\check{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\tau)=&\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(x,t)\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\tau)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,t)\\
