We first prove the stationarity property mentioned in Remark
6.31
, i.e.

Claim: We have, for
a\geq 1
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with
1\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2(n-a)+4
, that

\begin{split}\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta})}_{n+1,a}=&\,\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta})}_{n-a+1}.\end{split}

(6.6.130)

Proof of stationarity for
\bar{\rho}_{n+1}
, i.e. the claim above.

When
a\geq 1
, we have
n\geq 1
. From Remark
6.40
and taking
\langle\rangle_{\xi,\tau}
of (
6.6.28
), we deduce

\begin{split}\langle L_{11}(\hat{\rho}_{n+2,0}+\hat{\rho}_{n+2,M})\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2,n+1}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}&\,+\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2,F}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+L_{01}\bar{\rho}_{n+1}\\
+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n+1,R}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}&\,+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n+1,F}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=0.\end{split}

(6.6.131)

Using the coefficients
\hat{h}_{1}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}
,
\hat{\eta}_{1}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}
and
\hat{\chi}_{1}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}
computed in Section
6.6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2,n+1}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+L_{01}\bar{\rho}_{n+1}=\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{n+1}.

(6.6.132)

This is the same as deriving the equation for
\bar{\rho}_{0}
in Section
6.6.2
.

With the decomposition (
6.6.125
-
6.6.129
) and (
6.6.132
), to solve (
6.6.131
), it suffices to solve

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{n+1,R}+\langle L_{11}(\hat{\rho}_{n+2,0}+\hat{\rho}_{n+2,M})\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n+1,R}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.6.133)

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{n+1,F}+\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2,F}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n+1,F}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=

\displaystyle\,0.

(6.6.134)

On the other hand, consider the equations at the last induction step, i.e.

\displaystyle L_{2}\rho_{n+2}+L_{1}\rho_{n+1}+L_{0}\rho_{n}=

\displaystyle\,0,\quad\text{if }n=0,

(6.6.135)

\displaystyle L_{2}\rho_{n+2}+L_{1}\rho_{n+1}+L_{0}\rho_{n}+L_{-1}\rho_{n-1}=

\displaystyle\,0,\quad\text{if }n\geq 1.

(6.6.136)

Recalling (
6.6.52
), taking
\langle\rangle_{\xi,\tau}
of (
6.6.135
) or (
6.6.136
) gives

\displaystyle\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+L_{01}\bar{\rho}_{n}+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=

\displaystyle\,0.

(6.6.137)

Analogous to obtaining (
6.6.133
) and (
6.6.134
) from (
6.6.131
), we obtain, from (
6.6.137
),

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{n,R}+\langle L_{11}(\hat{\rho}_{n+1,0}+\hat{\rho}_{n+1,M})\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n,R}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.6.138)

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{n,F}+\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+1,F}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n,F}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=

\displaystyle\,0.

(6.6.139)

Then we have from (
6.6.138
) and (
6.6.133
),

\displaystyle-\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{n,R}=

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{11}(\hat{\rho}_{n+1,0}+\hat{\rho}_{n+1,M})\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n,R}\rangle_{\xi,\tau},

(6.6.140)

\displaystyle-\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{n+1,R}=

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2,M}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})(\hat{\rho}_{n+1,M}+\hat{\rho}_{n+1,n})\rangle_{\xi,\tau},

(6.6.141)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2,0}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n+1,0}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}.

(6.6.142)

The coefficients
\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta})}_{n+1,a}
with
1\leq a\leq n
are determined by the contributions from the line (
6.6.141
). Note that the coefficients of

,

and

∎

The terms in (

) contribute to those in line (
6.6.126
) for
a=0
. The terms in (
6.6.134
) contribute to those in line (
6.6.127
). Now we compute both. And we define

\displaystyle\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta})}_{n+1}:=\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta})}_{n+1,0}.

(6.6.143)

Next we compute the terms in (
6.6.142
). Then we have

\displaystyle\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2,0}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=-

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\lambda}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+3}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n+2}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\langle\eta_{1}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+2}\rangle_{\tau}\langle\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+2}\rangle_{\xi}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{2}\big(\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+2}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}\big)

(6.6.144)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\lambda}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+3}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n+2}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\langle\eta_{2}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+2}\rangle_{\tau}\langle\partial_{\xi_{2}}\Pi_{2}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+2}\rangle_{\xi}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{1}\big(\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+2}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}\big).

(6.6.145)

and

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2,F}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+2+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{F,n+2}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\text{ below}\bigg)

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\lambda}\sum\sum\sum\langle\eta_{1}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+2}\rangle_{\tau}\langle\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+2}\rangle_{\xi}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{2}\big(\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n+2}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\big)

(6.6.146)

\displaystyle+

For next term, we have

\displaystyle\langle L_{02}\hat{\rho}_{n+1,0}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}=

\displaystyle\,-\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\partial_{i}\Omega_{m,ij}H_{12}\sigma_{m}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1}\partial_{j}\big(\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}\big)

\displaystyle=-

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}}{\lambda}\sum\sum\sum\langle\sigma_{m}\eta_{1}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1}\rangle_{\tau}\langle\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\Big(\Omega_{m,ij}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{j}\big(\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}\big)\Big)

(6.6.148)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}}{\lambda}\sum\sum\sum\langle\sigma_{m}\eta_{2}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1}\rangle_{\tau}\langle\partial_{\xi_{2}}\Pi_{2}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\Big(\Omega_{m,ij}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{j}\big(\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n+1}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}\big)\Big)

and

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{02}\hat{\rho}_{n+1,F}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}

We also have

6.6.5.
Inductive step for temporal correctors

Next, we derive the structures of
\check{\rho}_{n+2}
.
We take the difference of
\langle\eqref{e:aspt:hrc4}\rangle_{\xi}
and
\langle\eqref{e:aspt:hrc4}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}
. Then we apply Remark
6.40
to deduce

\begin{split}L_{12}\check{\rho}_{n+2}=&\,-L_{01}\check{\rho}_{n+1}+\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+2}\rangle_{\xi}+\langle L_{02}\hat{\rho}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle L_{02}\hat{\rho}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi}\\
&\,+\langle L_{03}\hat{\rho}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle L_{03}\hat{\rho}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi}+\langle L_{04}\hat{\rho}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle L_{04}\hat{\rho}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi},\end{split}

(6.6.158)

from which we shall compute
\check{\rho}_{n+2}
.

We claim the stationary property holds for the coefficients in the following formula

\begin{split}\check{\rho}_{n+2}(x,t,\tau)=&\sum_{a=0}^{n+1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\beta}]=1,\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)+4}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}_{n+2,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta})}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}_{n+2,a}(x,t)\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}_{n+2,a}(\tau)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,t)\\
+&\,\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\beta}]=n_{0},\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+3+n_{0}}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}_{F,n+2}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta})}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}_{F,n+2}(x,t)\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}_{F,n+2}(\tau)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{\rho}_{F}(x,t):=\check{\rho}_{n+2,R}+\check{\rho}_{n+2,F}.\end{split}

(6.6.159)

Proof of stationarity for
\check{\rho}_{n+2}
, i.e. the claim above.

Indeed, we take the difference of
\langle\eqref{e:8:SolAspt}\rangle_{\xi}
and
\langle\eqref{e:8:SolAspt}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}
for
n=0
, or the difference of
\langle\eqref{e:9:SolAspt}\rangle_{\xi}
and
\langle\eqref{e:9:SolAspt}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}
for
n\geq 1
. Either of them gives

\displaystyle L_{12}\check{\rho}_{n+1}=

\displaystyle\,-L_{01}\check{\rho}_{n}+\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+1}\rangle_{\xi},

(6.6.160)

\displaystyle\,+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n}\rangle_{\xi}.

(6.6.161)

Subsequently, from the decomposition notation introduced in (
6.6.119
), we have

\displaystyle L_{12}\check{\rho}_{n+1,R}=

\displaystyle\,-L_{01}\check{\rho}_{n,R}+\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+1,R}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+1,R}\rangle_{\xi}

(6.6.162)

\displaystyle\,+\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n,R}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle(L_{02}+L_{03}+L_{04})\hat{\rho}_{n,R}\rangle_{\xi},

(6.6.163)

\displaystyle L_{12}\check{\rho}_{n+1,F}=

\displaystyle\,-L_{01}\check{\rho}_{n,F}+\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+1,F}\rangle_{\xi,\tau}-\langle L_{11}\hat{\rho}_{n+1,F}\rangle_{\xi}

(6.6.164)

(

&\,+N\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n+1}+\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta})}_{n+1}+\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n+1}+\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\gamma})}_{n+1}\end{split}

(6.6.192)

For index with form

and Case

and Case

obeys the estimate (

). Therefore, for
\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}
with

The goal is to compute the coefficients in

Similar to

6.7.
Technical preparation for remainders

One task we shall do later is to estimate the remainders in expansion lemmas in Section
6.5
. Specifically, we need to estimate
\tilde{\rho}
in Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35
. In this section, we prove some technical lemmas to prepare for this task.

Lemma 6.42
.

In the notions of Definition
6.18
and Definition
6.17
, consider
\rho:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
\chi:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
\eta:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
h\in\mathcal{\bar{P}}(10N^{2}Q)
. Suppose that, for
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
and
p\in\mathbb{N}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}],p\leq 11Q^{3}
, we have the following estimates

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rho\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma},

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi\|_{\infty}+

\displaystyle\,\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1.

Suppose
(\chi,\eta)
satisfies the generalized shear condition. For some
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
, define
\tilde{F}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
via

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(x,t)=h(x,t)\eta(\mu t)\chi(\lambda\Phi(x,t))\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho(x,t),

then we have the following estimates for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq 11Q^{3}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{F}\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]b\gamma}\bigg(\frac{\lambda}{\mathring{\lambda}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda}{\mathring{\mu}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}.

(6.7.1)

Proof of Lemma
6.42
.

We deduce from (
6.3.16
),

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{t}\big(\eta(\mu t)\chi(\lambda\Phi)\big)=

\displaystyle\,\mu\partial_{\tau}\eta(\mu t)\chi(\lambda\Phi)+\lambda\eta(\mu t)\bar{D}_{t}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\chi(\lambda\Phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mu\partial_{\tau}\eta(\mu t)\chi(\lambda\Phi)+\lambda^{-2}\eta(\mu t)\Omega(x,t,\mu t)\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\chi(\lambda\Phi)

(6.7.2)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\lambda\eta(\mu t)Z(x,t,\mu t)\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\chi(\lambda\Phi).

(6.7.3)

Since
(\chi,\eta)
satisfies the generalized shear condition, we can deduce from (
6.3.20
) that (
6.7.3
) is zero. Using (
6.3.4
), (
6.3.5
), (
6.3.18
), (
6.3.24
) and (
6.3.25
), we have

\displaystyle\big|\bar{D}_{t}\big(\eta(\mu t)\chi(\lambda\Phi)\big)\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mu,

(6.7.4)

\displaystyle\big|\nabla\big(\eta(\mu t)\chi(\lambda\Phi)\big)\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda.

(6.7.5)

Given the knowledge in (
6.7.4
) and (
6.7.5
), the following estimates follow from a direct induction argument similar to the proof of Lemma
3.2
,

\displaystyle\big|\bar{D}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big(\eta(\mu t)\chi(\lambda\Phi)\big)\big|\lesssim\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\mu^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}},\quad[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}.

Note that we have
O_{t}=\bar{D}_{t}+\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1}\cdot\nabla
, then we have

\displaystyle\big|O^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big(\eta(\mu t)\chi(\lambda\Phi)\big)\big|\lesssim\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}(\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}},\quad[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3},

(6.7.6)

and subsequently

\displaystyle\big\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big(\eta(\mu t)\chi(\lambda\Phi)\big)\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\lambda}{\mathring{\lambda}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda}{\mathring{\mu}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}},\quad[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}.

(6.7.7)

Indeed, to prove (
6.7.6
)-(
6.7.7
), note that we get additional terms contributed by
\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1}\cdot\nabla
. Since
\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q+1}\cdot\nabla
gives a factor
\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda
, we can inductively deduce (
6.7.6
)-(
6.7.7
), similar to the proof of Lemma
3.2
. Here, we also use
\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda>\mu
in the parameter relation of Remark
2.1
.

Similarly, one can show that, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
,

\displaystyle\|O^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho\|_{\infty}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]b\gamma}\lambda^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}(\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}},\quad[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq 11Q^{3}.

This leads to

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\rho\|_{\infty}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]b\gamma}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\lambda}{\mathring{\lambda}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda}{\mathring{\mu}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}},\quad[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq 11Q^{3}.

(6.7.8)

Applying Corollary
3.6
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{F}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]b\gamma}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\lambda}{\mathring{\lambda}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda}{\mathring{\mu}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}},\quad[\boldsymbol{\beta}]+[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq 11Q^{3}.

(6.7.9)

∎

In the next two lemmas, we estimate the force
\tilde{f}
for the remainder equations in Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35
.

Lemma 6.43
.

Given the assumptions in Lemma
6.29
, suppose that, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 5Q^{3}
,

\displaystyle{\lambda^{-a}}\|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{\rho}_{a}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha-(a+[\boldsymbol{\beta}])b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.7.10)

Then for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 4Q^{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{f}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\lambda^{-(Q\gamma-1)}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.7.11)

Proof.

Given all the information in
(C.1)
,
(C.2)
and
(C.3)
in Lemma

, we estimate
\tilde{f}
in (

) as follows. We expand
\tilde{f}

\displaystyle\tilde{f}=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}\Big(L_{11}(\hat{\rho}_{Q}+\check{\rho}_{Q}+\bar{\rho}_{Q})+L_{12}(\hat{\rho}_{Q}+\check{\rho}_{Q})+L_{13}\hat{\rho}_{Q}\Big)

(6.7.12)

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}\Big((L_{01}+L_{02}+L_{03})(\hat{\rho}_{Q-1}+\check{\rho}_{Q-1}+\bar{\rho}_{Q-1})+(L_{04}+L_{05}+L_{06}+L_{07})\hat{\rho}_{Q-1}\Big)

(6.7.13)

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q+1}}\Big((L_{01}+L_{02}+L_{03})(\hat{\rho}_{Q}+\check{\rho}_{Q}+\bar{\rho}_{Q})+(L_{04}+L_{05}+L_{06}+L_{07})\hat{\rho}_{Q}\Big)

(6.7.14)

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q+2}}\Big(\lambda^{2}L_{-1}\hat{\rho}_{Q-2}+\lambda L_{-1}\hat{\rho}_{Q-1}+L_{-1}\hat{\rho}_{Q}\Big)

(6.7.15)

Note that the argument on the right hand side of (

-

) is
(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t),\mu t)
.

We estimate
\tilde{f}
presented in (

-
6.7.15
). All terms are estimated in the same way. Here we take the term

as a typical example. First, we write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}L_{13}\hat{\rho}_{Q}=\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}S_{m,11}\vartheta_{m}\sum_{a=0}^{Q-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(Q-a)-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\eta}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\chi}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}.

From the estimate (
6.5.7
), we have

\displaystyle S_{m,11}\hat{h}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}

\displaystyle\,\in\mathcal{\bar{P}}((Q-a)N+m),

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{N}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(Q-a)-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\frac{\varepsilon\kappa\lambda}{\lambda^{Q-a}}

\displaystyle\,{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|S_{m,11}\hat{h}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\lambda^{-2(Q-a)\gamma}.

(6.7.16)

From (
6.3.25
), (
6.5.8
) and (
6.5.9
), we have, for
p\leq 8Q^{3}
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}(\vartheta_{m}\hat{\eta}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)})\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{p}_{\xi}\hat{\chi}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1.

We apply Lemma
6.42
to deduce that, for any
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q+3Q^{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{f}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sum_{a=0}^{Q-1}\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha-(a+[\boldsymbol{\beta}])b\gamma}\cdot\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\lambda^{-2(Q-a)\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.7.17)

The major gain comes from the factor

in (

). From direct computation and parameter relation in Section

, we can deduce (
6.7.12
).
∎

Lemma 6.44
.

Suppose
P\geq Q^{3}
. Given the assumptions in Lemma
6.35
, suppose we have, for some
s\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 5Q^{3}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\lambda^{-(s-1)b\gamma},

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{\rho}_{0}\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\lambda^{-(s-1)b\gamma},

\displaystyle{\lambda^{-a}}\|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{\rho}_{a}\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\lambda^{-(s-2+a)b\gamma},\quad a\geq 1.

Then for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 4Q^{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{f}\|\lesssim\lambda^{-([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+s-1)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(Q\gamma-1)}.

(6.7.18)

Proof.

As mentioned in
(D.4)
of Lemma
6.35
, the
\tilde{f}
is represented by (
6.7.12
-
6.7.15
), but the correctors
\hat{\rho}_{Q},\check{\rho}_{Q},\bar{\rho}_{Q},\hat{\rho}_{Q-1},\check{\rho}_{Q-1},\bar{\rho}_{Q-1},\hat{\rho}_{Q-2}
are given in
(D.1)
,
(D.2)
,
(D.3)
. This proof is analogous to the proof of Lemma

. Again, we take the term
L_{13}\hat{\rho}_{Q}
as a typical example. Consider

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}L_{13}\hat{\rho}_{Q}=

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}S_{m,11}\vartheta_{m}\sum_{a=0}^{Q-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(Q-a)-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\eta}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\chi}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}

(6.7.19)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}S_{m,11}\vartheta_{m}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{3Q-2}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,Q}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,Q}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}.

(6.7.20)

The analysis of (
6.7.19
) is the same as Lemma
6.43
. For (

), we have

\displaystyle S_{m,11}\hat{h}_{F,k,Q}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}

\displaystyle\,\in\mathcal{\bar{P}}\big(N(Q+1)+1\big),

(6.7.21)

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{N}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(Q-a)-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{Q-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\frac{\varepsilon\kappa\lambda}{\lambda^{Q}}

\displaystyle\,{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|S_{m,11}\hat{h}_{F,k,Q}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\lambda^{-2(Q-2)\gamma}.

), (

) and (

), we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}(\vartheta_{m}\hat{\eta}_{F,k,Q}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)})\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq P-Q,

(6.7.23)

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq P.

Then we apply Corollary

and Lemma

to deduce the following estimate for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}

Note that

is a large integer from Section

∎

6.8.
The first expansion at step
q+1

In this section, we rearrange the expansion formula in Lemma
6.29
to retrieve a simplified expansion. We also prove some related estimates for the simplified expansion.

Lemma 6.45
.

Suppose that for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.8.1)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa}}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle\,\left(1-\frac{4}{q}\right)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.2)

Now we fix some
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
in Assumption
6.22
, consider the solution
\varrho_{0}
in (
6.4.2
)-(
6.4.3
). Then
\varrho_{0}
admits the expansion

\displaystyle\varrho_{0}(x,t)=\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)+\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}+\tilde{\rho}_{0}

(6.8.3)

with

\displaystyle h^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{0,a}\in

\displaystyle\,\bar{\mathcal{P}}(Q),\qquad\sum_{a,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{0,a}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}.

(6.8.4)

The pair
\big(\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\big)
satisfies the generalized shear condition for all
a,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l
. The correctors
\chi_{0,a}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
\eta_{0,a}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfy
I_{0}\lesssim 1
and

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-Q,

(6.8.5)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}.

(6.8.6)

The collection
\{\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\}_{a}
satisfies the transmission condition, and for any
0\leq a\leq Q
,

\displaystyle\|\bar{\rho}_{0,a}(\cdot,0)\|_{2}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\bar{\rho}_{0,a}(\cdot,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-ab\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.7)

The following estimates hold,

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},a}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-ab\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.8.8)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},a}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-ab\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.8.9)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},0}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle\,\left(1-\frac{4}{q}\right)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.10)

Moreover, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with

, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{\rho}_{0}\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\tilde{\rho}_{0}\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda^{-3-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.11)

Lemma 6.46
.

In the setting of Lemma
6.45
, we have

\displaystyle h_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}=-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}},\quad\eta_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}=\eta_{2},\quad\chi_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}=-\partial_{\xi_{2}}^{-1}\Pi_{2},\quad\text{for }\boldsymbol{\alpha}=1,l=1.

(6.8.12)

\displaystyle h_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}=\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}},\quad\eta_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}=\eta_{1},\quad\chi_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}=-\partial_{\xi_{1}}^{-1}\Pi_{1},\quad\text{for }\boldsymbol{\alpha}=2,l=1.

(6.8.13)

The following estimate holds

\displaystyle\|h_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-b\gamma},\quad\text{for }a=0,(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)\notin\{(1,1),(2,1)\}.

(6.8.14)

Moreover,
\bar{\rho}_{0,0}
satisfies

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{t}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}-\bar{\kappa}\Delta\bar{\rho}_{0,0}=0,\quad\bar{\rho}_{0,0}(\cdot,0)=\rho_{\mathrm{in}}(\cdot).

(6.8.15)

Remark 6.47
.

Note that in (
6.8.2
) and (
6.8.10
), we have
\leq
instead of
\lesssim
. We need these two precise estimates for the global iterative scheme to converge. In contrast, the constants in the other estimates are not important, since we can absorb them later.

In the rest of this section, we prove two lemmas above. Specifically, (
6.8.3
)-(

) and Lemma

are proved in Section

. (

) and (

) are proved in Section

and Section

respectively.

6.8.1.

6.45
and Lemma

)-(

From Lemma

) to (

to be (we remind that below

for some integer

. It is clear from Lemma

that we can arrange this renumbering such that Lemma

holds. Here, we use the exact formula for the first two correctors in (

. The cardinality

\displaystyle\sum_{\frac{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]}{2}\leq i\leq Q-a}\hat{I}_{i}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}+\check{I}_{i}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\leq 11(3N)^{Q-a-1}.

(6.8.24)

Hence, we set

. In case that

is larger than the left-hand side of (

), we just set those correctors in additional slots of (

) to be zero.

With above representation of (
6.8.16
), we can write the expansion in Lemma

to be

\displaystyle\varrho_{0}(x,t)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(Q-a)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}h_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)+\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}+\tilde{\rho}

(6.8.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{Q}h_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)+\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}+\tilde{\rho}_{0}

(6.8.26)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=Q+1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(Q-a)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}h_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.8.27)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}\Big(h_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\mathbb{P}_{Q}h_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\Big)\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.8.28)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\tilde{\rho}.

(6.8.29)

Now, defining
h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}:=\mathbb{P}_{Q}h_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
, (
6.8.3
) follows from (
6.8.26
). (
6.8.4
) follows from (
6.3.14
), (
6.3.15
), (
6.5.4
), (
6.5.6
), (
6.5.12
) and (
6.5.14
). (
6.8.5
) follows from (
6.5.9
) and (
6.5.16
). (
6.8.6
) follows from (
6.5.8
) and (
6.8.21
). (
6.8.7
) follows from (
6.8.22
) and Lemma
6.33
.

6.8.2.
Proof of Lemma
6.45
: Estimating macroscopic states

From (
6.3.3
), (
6.3.14
), (
6.5.23
) and (
6.5.24
), we have
\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n}\in\mathcal{\bar{P}}(Nn+3)
and

\displaystyle\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1}^{2n+1}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\lambda^{-2nb\gamma}.

(6.8.30)

Recalling (
6.8.22
), (
6.5.18
) for
n\geq 1
becomes

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{0,n}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)+1}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n-a}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\bigg).

(6.8.31)

From Lemma
6.14
, (
6.5.17
), (
6.8.1
) and (
6.8.22
), we deduce

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},0}\leq

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.32)

Similarly, from the alternative of Lemma
6.15
for
{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\cdot\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\cdot,\cdot,\cdot}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}
, (
6.5.17
), (
6.8.2
) and (
6.8.22
), we deduce (
6.8.10
).

Next we proceed with an induction argument to prove (
6.8.8
). Suppose (
6.8.8
) holds for any
0\leq a\leq n
, i.e.

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},a}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-ab\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.33)

From (
6.8.1
) and (
6.8.7
), we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\bar{\rho}_{0,n+1}(\cdot,0)]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa}}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-(n+1)b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.34)

Then from (
6.8.30
), (
6.8.31
), (
6.8.33
), (
6.8.34
) and Lemma
6.15
, we can deduce

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{0,n+1}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n+1}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-(n+1)b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.35)

This concludes the proof of (
6.8.8
). The proof of (
6.8.9
) is completely analogous.

6.8.3.
Proof of Lemma
6.45
: Estimating the remainder

To estimate the remainder
\tilde{\rho}_{0}
, we first estimate terms in (
6.8.27
) and (
6.8.28
). From (
6.8.8
), we have that, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{\rho}_{0,n}\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\lambda^{-nb\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.36)

Using (
6.8.4
), (
6.8.5
), (
6.8.6
), (
6.8.36
) and applying Lemma
6.42
, we have the following estimate for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 5Q^{3}-3Q
,

\displaystyle\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\ref{e:42:reEXP1})\|\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+Q)b\gamma}\cdot\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\cdot\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)

and subsequently for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 4Q^{3}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\ref{e:42:reEXP1})\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}(\ref{e:42:reEXP1})\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda^{-3-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.37)

Estimating (
6.8.28
) is similar. Note that the gain in negative power of
\lambda
is from the lower bound on the differentiation order of
h_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\mathbb{P}_{Q}h_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
. Applying Corollary
3.6
and Lemma
6.42
, we have that, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 4Q^{3}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\ref{e:43:reEXP1})\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}(\ref{e:43:reEXP1})\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda^{-3-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.38)

For
\tilde{\rho}
in (
6.8.29
), we deduce, from Remark
6.3
and Lemma
6.33
, the following estimates on initial datum
\tilde{\rho}(\cdot,0)
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\bar{\nabla}}^{p}\tilde{\rho}(\cdot,0)\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-Q\gamma}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(q+1)},\quad p\leq 10Q^{3},

and from Lemma
6.43
, the following estimates on
\tilde{f}

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{f}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\lambda^{-(Q\gamma-1)}\bigg(

\displaystyle\,|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),\quad[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 4Q^{3}.

Then we apply the energy estimates Lemma
5.10
and Lemma
5.13
, in the style of Proposition
6.23
, to deduce the following estimates for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2Q+3Q^{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{\rho}\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\tilde{\rho}\|_{2}\lesssim\kappa^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda^{-(Q\gamma-1)}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.8.39)

Finally, (
6.8.27
-
6.8.29
) with
Q\gamma>3
and (
6.8.37
-
6.8.39
) together give (
6.8.11
).

6.9.
Dissipation estimates: Proof of Theorem
6.9

In this section, we prove the estimate on dissipation.

Proof of Theorem
6.9
.

In view of (
6.4.20
) in Proposition
6.28
, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\big||\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}-|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(\bar{u},\bar{\kappa})}\big|\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\alpha-2}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)

(6.9.1)

We work directly with the expansion formula (
6.8.3
) in Lemma
6.45
. We compute

\displaystyle\nabla\varrho_{0}=

\displaystyle\,\lambda\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\partial_{\xi_{j}}\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)\nabla\Phi_{j}

(6.9.2)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\nabla\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.9.3)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}\nabla h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.9.4)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\nabla\bar{\rho}_{0,a}+\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{0}.

(6.9.5)

The goal is to show the leading term of
\kappa\iint|\nabla\varrho_{0}|^{2}\,dxdt
is contributed by

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\mu t)\partial_{\xi_{j}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\lambda\Phi)\nabla\Phi_{j}+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{2}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\eta_{0,0}^{(2,1)}(\mu t)\partial_{\xi_{j}}\chi_{0,0}^{(2,1)}(\lambda\Phi)\nabla\Phi_{j}

(6.9.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\mu t)\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\lambda\Phi)\nabla\Phi_{2}+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{2}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\eta_{0,0}^{(2,1)}(\mu t)\partial_{\xi_{1}}\chi_{0,0}^{(2,1)}(\lambda\Phi)\nabla\Phi_{1}

(6.9.7)

and the rest are small error terms. We shall show all error terms in
\kappa\iint|\nabla\varrho_{0}|^{2}\,dxdt
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\lambda^{-b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)^{2}.

(6.9.8)

Here, we also remark that, using (
2.1.13
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\bar{\lambda}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{2b^{2}\gamma}.

(6.9.9)

Step 6.9.1

(Estimate the leading term (
6.9.6
))
.

Notice that
\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}
and
\eta_{0,0}^{(2,1)}
have disjoint support, and
\nabla\Phi=\mathrm{Id}+\varepsilon O(1)
given by (
3.0.1
). We have

\displaystyle\kappa\iint\eqref{e:18:homDissip}^{2}\,dxdt=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\iint|\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}|\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\mu t)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\lambda\Phi)|^{2}

(6.9.10)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\iint|\partial_{2}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}|\eta_{0,0}^{(2,1)}(\mu t)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi_{1}}\chi_{0,0}^{(2,1)}(\lambda\Phi)|^{2}

(6.9.11)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\iint|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{2}O(1).

(6.9.12)

Note that (
6.9.12
) can be bounded by (
6.9.8
) using (
6.3.3
),
\varepsilon^{2}<\lambda^{-b\gamma}
and integration by parts.

Then we estimate

\displaystyle\eqref{e:22:homDissip}-\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\iint|\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\iint|\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}|\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\mu t)|^{2}\Big(|\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\lambda\Phi)|^{2}-\langle|\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}|^{2}\rangle_{\xi}\Big)

(6.9.13)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\langle|\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}|^{2}\rangle_{\xi}\iint|\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}\Big(|\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\mu t)|^{2}-\langle|\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}|^{2}\rangle_{\tau}\Big).

(6.9.14)

Notice that there exists a potential
f:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with
\partial_{\xi_{1}}f=0
and
\|f\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi_{2}}f=|\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\xi)|^{2}-\langle|\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}|^{2}\rangle_{\xi},

hence

\displaystyle\partial_{2}\Big(\frac{1}{\lambda}f(\lambda\Phi)\Big)=\partial_{\xi_{2}}f(\lambda\Phi)\cdot\partial_{2}\Phi_{2}.

To estimate (
6.9.13
), we use again
\nabla\Phi=\mathrm{Id}+\varepsilon O(1)
given by (
3.0.1
),

\displaystyle\eqref{e:28:homDissip}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\iint|\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}|\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\mu t)|^{2}\partial_{2}\Big(\frac{1}{\lambda}f(\lambda\Phi)\Big)+\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\iint|\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}\cdot\varepsilon O(1)

(6.9.15)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\iint 2\partial_{21}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\mu t)|^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda}f(\lambda\Phi)+\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\iint|\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}\cdot\varepsilon O(1).

(6.9.16)

Using (
6.3.3
) and (
6.8.8
), the first term in (
6.9.16
) can be estimated by

\displaystyle\,\big|\text{the first term in }\eqref{e:38:homDissip}\big|\lesssim\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\lambda}\cdot\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\bar{\lambda}^{-2\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)^{2}.

(6.9.17)

Now we can use the parameter relations (
2.1.6
), (
2.1.11
) to deduce that (
6.9.17
) is bounded by (
6.9.8
).

To estimate (
6.9.14
), notice that there exists a potential
g:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with
\|g\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}g=|\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}(\tau)|^{2}-\langle|\eta_{0,0}^{(1,1)}|^{2}\rangle_{\tau},

then

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{t}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu}g(\mu t)\Big)=\partial_{\tau}g(\mu t).

Now we estimate (
6.9.14
) as

\displaystyle\eqref{e:30:homDissip}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\langle|\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}|^{2}\rangle_{\xi}\iint|\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}\bar{D}_{t}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu}g(\mu t)\Big)

(6.9.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\langle|\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}|^{2}\rangle_{\xi}\iint 2\bar{D}_{t}\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\cdot\frac{1}{\mu}g(\mu t)

(6.9.19)

\displaystyle\,+\frac{\delta}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\langle|\partial_{\xi_{2}}\chi_{0,0}^{(1,1)}|^{2}\rangle_{\xi}\int|\partial_{1}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}|^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\mu}g(\mu t)\bigg|_{t=0}^{t=1}.

(6.9.20)

Using (
6.3.3
) and (
6.8.8
), we estimate (
6.9.19
) and (
6.9.20
) as

\displaystyle\big|\eqref{e:46:homDissip}\big|\lesssim\frac{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}{\mu}\cdot\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\bar{\lambda}^{-2\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)^{2},

(6.9.21)

\displaystyle\big|\eqref{e:48:homDissip}\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\mu}\cdot\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\bar{\lambda}^{-2\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)^{2}.

(6.9.22)

From the parameter relations (
2.1.6
), (
2.1.7
) and (
2.1.12
), we deduce that (
6.9.21
) and (
6.9.22
) can be bounded by (
6.9.8
).

Combining above, we deduce that (
6.9.12
)-(
6.9.14
) can be bounded by (
6.9.8
).

Step 6.9.2

(Estimate other error terms)
.

Now we consider the contributions from (
6.9.2
). Other terms in (
6.9.3
)-(
6.9.5
) and cross terms can be dealt similarly.
Apart from (
6.9.6
), other contributions in (
6.9.2
) come from two type of terms:

\displaystyle\lambda\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,a}h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\partial_{\xi_{j}}\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)\nabla\Phi_{j},

\displaystyle\,\quad\text{for }a\geq 1,

(6.9.23)

\displaystyle\lambda\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0,0}h_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\partial_{\xi_{j}}\chi_{0,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)\nabla\Phi_{j},

\displaystyle\,\quad\text{for }(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)\notin\{(1,1),(2,1)\}.

(6.9.24)

For terms like (
6.9.23
), we use (
6.8.8
) and (
6.3.3
) to estimate

\displaystyle\kappa\iint\eqref{e:60:homDissip}^{2}\,dxdt\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-4b\gamma}\cdot\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\bar{\lambda}^{-2\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)^{2},

(6.9.25)

\displaystyle\kappa\iint\eqref{e:62:homDissip}^{2}\,dxdt\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-4b\gamma}\cdot\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\bar{\lambda}^{-2\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg)^{2}.

(6.9.26)

In the factor
\lambda^{-4b\gamma}
of (
6.9.25
), half of the power comes from
a\geq 1
and (
6.8.8
). The other half comes from
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]_{x}\geq 1
. For the factor
\lambda^{-4b\gamma}
of (
6.9.26
), half of the power comes from
(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)\notin\{(1,1),(2,1)\}
, Lemma
6.46
and (
6.8.8
). The other half comes from
[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]_{x}\geq 1
.

Combining above and (
6.9.9
), we deduce that all error terms in
\kappa\iint|\nabla\varrho_{0}|^{2}\,dxdt
can be bounded by (
6.9.8
).

∎

6.10.
Reformulate the expansion lemma II

In this section, we rearrange the expansion Lemma
6.35
and prove some related estimates.

Lemma 6.48
.

Given (
6.8.1
)-(
6.8.2
), taking Assumption
6.34
with some
P\geq Q^{3}
and

\displaystyle\Upsilon=\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\,\cdot\lambda^{-2b\gamma},

(6.10.1)

suppose that, for some
\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}>0
, we have

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-(s-2)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.10.2)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(s-2)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.10.3)

\displaystyle\|\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(\cdot,0)\|_{2}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\,|\bar{\rho}_{F,k}(\cdot,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\lesssim\lambda^{-(s-2)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.10.4)

Then the solution
\varrho
to the equation (
6.5.49
) admits the expansion

\displaystyle\varrho(x,t)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{K+Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{a}\sum_{l=1}^{\mathfrak{I}}\ddot{h}_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\ddot{\eta}_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\ddot{\chi}_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)+\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\ddot{\rho}_{a}+\tilde{\rho}_{F}

(6.10.5)

with
\mathfrak{I}\lesssim 1
,

\displaystyle\ddot{h}_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\in

\displaystyle\,\bar{\mathcal{P}}(2NQ),\qquad\sum_{k,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\ddot{h}_{k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}.

(6.10.6)

The pair
(\ddot{\chi}_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\ddot{\eta}_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)})
satisfies the generalized shear condition for all
a,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l
, and

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\ddot{\chi}_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq P,

(6.10.7)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\ddot{\eta}_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq P-Q.

(6.10.8)

Moreover, the collection
\{\ddot{\rho}_{a}\}_{a}
satisfies transmission condition, and for any
0\leq a\leq K+Q
, we have

\displaystyle\|\ddot{\rho}_{a}(\cdot,0)\|_{2}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\ddot{\rho}_{a}(\cdot,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(a+s)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.10.9)

and

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\ddot{\rho}_{a}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}+a+1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-(s-1)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.10.10)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\ddot{\rho}_{a}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}+a+1}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(s-1)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.10.11)

For
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2Q+3Q^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{\rho}_{F}\|+\kappa^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\tilde{\rho}_{F}\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-3-([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+s-1)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.10.12)

The proof of Lemma
6.48
is presented in the rest of this section and is similar to that of Lemma
6.45
in Section
6.8
. In Section
6.10.1
, we give an immediate reformulation of the expansion in Lemma
6.35
. In Section
6.10.2
, we prove (
6.10.5
)-(
6.10.9
). In Section
6.10.3
, we estimate the macroscopic states
\ddot{\rho}_{a}
, i.e. proving (
6.10.10
). In Section
6.10.4
, we estimate the remainder term
\tilde{\rho}_{F}
. i.e. proving (
6.10.12
).

6.10.1.
Proof of Lemma
6.48
: Reformulation

Recalling Remark
6.36
, there are shared coefficients and functions in Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35
, i.e.
\hat{h}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\hat{\eta}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\hat{\chi}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\check{h}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\check{\eta}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
and
\bar{h}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
. However, the macroscopic states
\bar{\rho}_{a},0\leq a\leq Q
in Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35
are different.

Collecting the expansion in Lemma
6.35
, we have

\displaystyle\varrho=\sum_{n=1}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\mu t)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\lambda\Phi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}

(6.10.13)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-2+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\mu t)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\lambda\Phi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}

(6.10.14)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\mu t)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}

(6.10.15)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-1+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\mu t)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\Bigg)

(6.10.16)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{n=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\bar{\rho}_{n}+\tilde{\rho}.

(6.10.17)

From (
6.5.54
), (
6.5.55
), (
6.5.63
) and (
6.5.64
), we have
\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n},\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}\lesssim 1
and

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n},\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\in

\displaystyle\,\bar{\mathcal{P}}(Q+NQ).

(6.10.18)

From (
6.10.2
), (
6.5.57
) and (
6.5.66
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l,\boldsymbol{\alpha},k}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,j,n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-2(n-1)\gamma},\quad n\geq 2,

(6.10.19)

\displaystyle\sum_{l,\boldsymbol{\alpha},k}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,j,n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mu}\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\lambda}\cdot\lambda^{-2n\gamma},\qquad n\geq 1.

(6.10.20)

From (
6.5.58
), (
6.5.59
) and (
6.5.67
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,j,n}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq P,

(6.10.21)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,j,n}\|_{\infty}+\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,j,n}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq P-Q.

(6.10.22)

6.10.2.
Proof of Lemma
6.48
: Rearrangement

For the terms in (
6.10.13
) and (
6.10.15
) involving
\bar{\rho}_{a}
, we define

\displaystyle\ddot{\rho}_{a}:=\frac{1}{\lambda^{a}}\bar{\rho}_{a}.

(6.10.23)

The rearrangement of (
6.10.13
) and (
6.10.15
) is completely analogous to the rearrangement for Lemma
6.29
in Section
6.8.1
, so we omit the details. This leads to

\displaystyle\eqref{e:24:reEXP2}+\eqref{e:28:reEXP2}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}\mathbb{P}h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.10.24)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}\Big(h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\mathbb{P}h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\Big)\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.10.25)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=Q+1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(Q-a)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{0}}h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi).

(6.10.26)

Here,
I_{0}
,
h_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
,
\eta_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
and
\chi_{0,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
are given in Lemma
6.45
.

Next, we rearrange the
F
-related terms as follows, i.e. the terms in (
6.10.14
) and (
6.10.16
) involving
\bar{\rho}_{F,k}
.

\displaystyle\,\eqref{e:26:reEXP2}+\eqref{e:30:reEXP2}

(6.10.27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{n=1}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-2+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\mu t)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\lambda\Phi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}

(6.10.28)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-1+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\mu t)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\Bigg)

(6.10.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-1+Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\sum_{n\geq\max\{1,\frac{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]+1-Q}{2}\}}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\Bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,n}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\mu t)

(6.10.30)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,k,n}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,n}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\mu t)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}(\lambda\Phi)\Bigg)

(6.10.31)

From (
6.10.28
-
6.10.29
) to (
6.10.30
-
6.10.31
), we set

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n},\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n},\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}=0,\quad\text{if }[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=2n-1+Q.

(6.10.32)

Then, we renumber the following correctors indexed by
n
and
l

\displaystyle\bigg\{\bigg\{\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n},\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n},\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\bigg\}_{1\leq l\leq\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,k,n}},\bigg\{\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\check{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n},\check{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n},\check{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}\bigg\}_{1\leq l\leq\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,k,n}}\bigg\}_{n}

with
\check{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k,n}=1
in a single index, denoted again by
l
, and write them in the following form

\displaystyle\big\{\ddot{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k},\ddot{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k},\ddot{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}\big\}_{1\leq l\leq\mathfrak{I}}.

Hence, the cardinality has the estimate

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}\leq\max_{k,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\sum_{n\geq\max\{1,\frac{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]+1-Q}{2}\}}^{Q}\Big(\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,k,n}+\check{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{F,k,n}\Big)

(6.10.33)

In case where
\leq
in (
6.10.33
) turns into a strict inequality, we set all correctors in additional slots to be zero. This leads to
\mathfrak{I}\lesssim 1
.

With the renumbering above, we deduce

\displaystyle\,\eqref{e:26:reEXP2}+\eqref{e:30:reEXP2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-1+Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{F,k}\sum_{l=1}^{\mathfrak{I}}\ddot{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}\ddot{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}(\mu t)\ddot{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.10.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{F,k}\sum_{l=1}^{\mathfrak{I}}\mathbb{P}_{Q}\ddot{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}\ddot{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}(\mu t)\ddot{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.10.35)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{F,k}\sum_{l=1}^{\mathfrak{I}}\Big(\ddot{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}-\mathbb{P}_{Q}\ddot{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}\Big)\ddot{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}(\mu t)\ddot{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.10.36)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=Q+1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-1+Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{F,k}\sum_{l=1}^{\mathfrak{I}}\ddot{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}\ddot{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}(\mu t)\ddot{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,k}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.10.37)

From (
6.10.19
) and (
6.10.20
), we have the following estimates

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n-1+Q}\sum_{l=1}^{\mathfrak{I}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\ddot{h}_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-\gamma}.

(6.10.38)

From (
6.5.58
), (
6.5.59
) and (
6.5.67
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\ddot{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,n}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq P,

(6.10.39)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\ddot{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{F,n}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq P-Q.

(6.10.40)

Now we return to (
6.10.13
-
6.10.17
). The terms in (
6.10.13
) and (
6.10.15
) are represented in (
6.10.24
-
6.10.26
). The terms in (
6.10.14
) and (

) are represented in (
6.10.34
-
6.10.37
). We rename the terms in (
6.10.24
) and (
6.10.35
) such that

\displaystyle(\ref{e:98:0:reEXP2})+(\ref{e:124:2:reEXP2})=\sum_{a=0}^{K+Q}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{a}\sum_{l=1}^{\mathfrak{I}}\ddot{h}_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\ddot{\eta}_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\ddot{\chi}_{a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi),

(6.10.41)

which gives (
6.10.5
) with the remainder term given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{F}:=(\ref{e:98:2:reEXP2})+(\ref{e:98:4:reEXP2})+(\ref{e:124:4:reEXP2})+(\ref{e:124:6:reEXP2})+\tilde{\rho}.

(6.10.42)

This proves (
6.10.5
). The estimates (

), (

) and (

) follow from (
6.10.38
), (
6.10.39
) and (

). The estimate (

) and transmission condition follow from (
