6.10.3.
Proof of Lemma
6.48
: Estimating macroscopic states

Define

\displaystyle\ddot{h}_{F,k,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}:=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mathring{\lambda}}\sum_{l=1}^{J}h_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\langle\eta_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\tau}\langle\chi_{F,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi},

(6.10.43)

\displaystyle\ddot{h}_{F,k,n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}:=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\bar{h}_{F,k,n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})},\qquad n\geq 1.

(6.10.44)

The macroscopic states
\{\ddot{\rho}_{n}\}_{n}
satisfy the following equations

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\ddot{\rho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\Big(\ddot{h}_{F,k,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\Big),

(6.10.45)

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\ddot{\rho}_{n}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)+1}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\Big(\ddot{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n-a}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\ddot{\rho}_{a}\Big)+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2n+Q}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\Big(\ddot{h}_{F,k,n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{F,k}\Big)

(6.10.46)

with
\ddot{h}_{F,k,n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}(2Q+NQ)
and

\displaystyle\sum_{k,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\ddot{h}_{F,k,0}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\lambda^{-2b\gamma},

(6.10.47)

\displaystyle\sum_{k,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\ddot{h}_{F,k,n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\kappa}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\lambda^{-2nb\gamma},\quad n\geq 1.

(6.10.48)

Here, (
6.10.47
) follows from (
6.5.46
), (
6.10.43
) and
\bar{\kappa}>\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}
. (
6.10.48
) follows from (
6.5.73
), (
6.10.1
) and (
6.10.44
).

Note that from (
6.5.71
) and (
6.10.23
),
\ddot{\rho}_{0}(x,0)=0
. We apply Lemma
6.15
with (
6.10.47
) to deduce

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\ddot{\rho}_{0}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}+1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-(s-1)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.10.49)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\ddot{\rho}_{0}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}+1}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(s-1)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.10.50)

Here, note that (
6.10.47
) involves
\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}
and
\lambda
, while the estimate in Lemma
6.15
involves
\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}
and
\bar{\lambda}
. This gives the rise of the factor
\lambda^{-(s-1)b\gamma}
. It will help us to control certain constants.
From (
6.8.1
), (
6.8.2
) and (
6.10.9
), we can deduce

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\ddot{\rho}_{1}(\cdot,0)]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-sb\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.10.51)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\ddot{\rho}_{1}(\cdot,0)]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa}}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-sb\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.10.52)

Applying Lemma
6.15
again with (
6.10.48
) and (
6.10.51
), we have

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\ddot{\rho}_{1}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}+2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-(s-1)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.10.53)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\ddot{\rho}_{1}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}+2}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(s-1)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.10.54)

Here, note that
s-1\geq 0
and
\ddot{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n-a}
is given in Lemma
6.45
, according to Remark
6.36
.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
6.45
in Section
6.8.2
, inductively we can show

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\ddot{\rho}_{n}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}+n+1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-(s+n-2)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},\quad\forall\,n\geq 2.

(6.10.55)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\ddot{\rho}_{n}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},n_{0}+n+1}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(s+n-2)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},\quad\forall\,n\geq 2.

(6.10.56)

Then (
6.10.49
), (
6.10.53
) and (
6.10.55
) give (
6.10.10
).

6.10.4.
Proof of Lemma
6.48
: Estimating the remainder

This is analogous to Section
6.8.3
. To estimate
\tilde{\rho}
, we appeal to the equation satisfied by
\tilde{\rho}
with initial datum given in (
6.5.75
). Hence, the initial datum is estimated by Remark
6.3
and Lemma
6.38
, and the force
\tilde{f}
is estimated by Lemma
6.44
. With such estimates, we use the energy estimates in Lemma
5.10
and Lemma
5.13
to estimate
\tilde{\rho}
. We remark here we use (
6.10.55
) to verify the assumptions in Lemma
6.44
. The estimates on (
6.10.25
), (
6.10.26
), (
6.10.36
) and (
6.10.37
) are similar to Section
6.8.3
, so we omit the details.

Above together with (
6.10.42
) gives (
6.10.12
).

6.11.
Inductive expansion at step
q+1
: Proof of Theorem
6.10

In this section we prove Theorem
6.10
. The key element is the following proposition, which shows how to propagate the expansion structure from step
n
to step
n+1
. Then, Theorem
6.10
follow from the expansions and direct computations.

Proposition 6.49
.

Fix some
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and
\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}>0
. Given (
6.8.1
)-(
6.8.1
) and Assumption
6.22
, consider the equations (
6.4.4
) and (
6.4.5
). Fix any
n\leq Q-1
. Suppose
\varrho_{n}
admits the following expansion

\displaystyle\varrho_{n}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{(Q+1)(n+1)}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{n,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{n}}h_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)+\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\bar{\rho}_{n,a}+\tilde{\rho}_{n},

(6.11.1)

with
I_{n}\lesssim 1
,

\displaystyle h_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}(Q),\quad\sum_{a,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}},

(6.11.2)

and

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+1),

(6.11.3)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+1).

(6.11.4)

For any
a,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l
,
\big(\chi_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\eta_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\big)
satisfies the generalized shear condition. The collection
\{\bar{\rho}_{n,a}\}_{a}
satisfy the transmission condition. Suppose that for any
0\leq a\leq(Q+1)(n+1)
,

\displaystyle\|\bar{\rho}_{n,a}(\cdot,0)\|_{2}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\bar{\rho}_{n,a}(\cdot,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.11.5)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{n,a}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},(Q+1)(n+1)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.11.6)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{n,a}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},(Q+1)(n+1)}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}

(6.11.7)

and

\begin{split}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{\rho}_{n}\|+\kappa\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\tilde{\rho}_{n}\|_{2}\lesssim&\,\lambda^{-3-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}\quad\text{for }[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2Q+3Q(Q-n).\end{split}

(6.11.8)

Then
\varrho_{n+1}
admits the following expansion

\displaystyle\varrho_{n+1}=\sum_{a=0}^{(Q+1)(n+2)}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{n+1}}h_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)+\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}+\tilde{\rho}_{n+1}

(6.11.9)

with
I_{n+1}\lesssim 1
,

\displaystyle h_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}(Q),\quad\sum_{a,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}

(6.11.10)

and

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+2),

(6.11.11)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+2).

(6.11.12)

For any
a,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l
,
\big(\chi_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\eta_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\big)
satisfies the generalized shear condition. Moreover, for any
0\leq a\leq(Q+1)(n+2)
,

\displaystyle\|\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}(\cdot,0)\|_{2}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}(\cdot,0)|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.11.13)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},(Q+1)(n+2)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}

(6.11.14)

\displaystyle{\langle\kern-1.50696pt\langle\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}\rangle\kern-1.50696pt\rangle}_{\bar{u},\bar{\kappa},(Q+1)(n+2)}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}

(6.11.15)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{\rho}_{n+1}\|+\kappa\mathring{\lambda}\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathring{\nabla}\tilde{\rho}_{n+1}\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-3-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}\quad\text{for }[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2Q+3Q(Q-n-1).

(6.11.16)

Remark 6.50
.

Comparing (
6.11.6
)-(
6.11.7
) with (
6.11.14
)-(
6.11.15
), we see that the factor
\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}
appears in the estimates for
\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}
, which is not present in the estimates for
\bar{\rho}_{n,a}
. This is an actual gain in each step of the expansion, which helps to control the constants in the estimates.

We also need the following corollaries.

Corollary 6.51
.

For
\varrho_{0}
in Lemma
6.45
, we have that, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-\alpha-[\boldsymbol{\beta}]b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.11.17)

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\bigg(1-\frac{4}{q}+\lambda^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}\bigg)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.11.18)

Corollary 6.52
.

For
n\leq Q-1
and the expansion (
6.11.9
) with (
6.11.10
)-(
6.11.16
) in Proposition
6.49
, we have that, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{n+1}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-b\gamma}\lambda^{-\alpha-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},

(6.11.19)

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.11.20)

From above results, we can prove Theorem
6.10
. We postpone the proof of Proposition
6.49
, Corollary
6.51
and Corollary
6.52
to the end of this section.

Proof of Theorem
6.10
.

Given Proposition
6.28
, it suffices to prove that, for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{v,\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{v,\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\bigg(1-\frac{4}{q}+\lambda^{-\frac{(b-1)\gamma}{2}}\bigg)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

In this proof, we only prove the estimate for
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\cdot]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{v,\kappa}
in the setting of Assumption
6.22
. The Hölder estimate for
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\cdot]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{v,\kappa}
is similar.

It suffices to prove that for any
0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}
and any
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 11Q^{3}-3Qn
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-\alpha-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.11.21)

When
n=0
and
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q
, (
6.11.21
) follows from Lemma
6.45
and Corollary
6.51
.

When
n\leq Q
and
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q
, notice that Lemma
6.45
and Proposition
6.49
form an inductive proof, which verifies that the solution
\varrho_{n}
to (
6.4.2
-
6.4.5
) admits the expansion (
6.11.1
). Here, we set

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}=|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}.

Then (
6.11.21
) with
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q
follows from Corollary
6.52
.

When
n\leq Q
or
[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\geq Q
, (
6.11.21
) follows from Proposition
6.26
and Remark
6.27
. Here, the constants in
\lesssim
of (
6.4.15
) are absorbed by the additional negative power of
\lambda
. This finishes the proof of (
6.11.21
) for
0\leq n\leq 2Q^{2}
.
∎

The proofs of Corollary
6.52
and Corollary
6.51
given below are analogous.

Proof of Corollary
6.52
.

To prove (
6.11.19
) and (
6.11.20
), we use the expansion (
6.11.9
). We need to estimate the three terms in (
6.11.9
). Moreover, here we need to control the constants in
\lesssim
carefully.

Step 6.11.1

(The term
\tilde{\rho}_{n+1}
)
.

The
L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
-estimate on
\nabla^{p}\tilde{\rho}_{n+1}
is obvious from (
6.11.16
), and we have gained a factor
\lambda^{-3}
. The
L^{\infty}_{x,t}
-estimate and
L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}
-estimate on
\tilde{\rho}_{n+1}
follow from
L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
-estimate on
\nabla^{p}\tilde{\rho}_{n+1}
and interpolation inequality in the spirit of Proposition
6.23
. The substantial gain
\lambda^{-3}
compensates the derivative loss in interpolation inequality and also absorbs constants in
\lesssim
.

Step 6.11.2

(The terms
\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}
)
.

For terms like
\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}
, we use (
6.11.14
) and Lemma
6.42
to deduce that for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mathring{\lambda}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\lambda}}{\mathring{\mu}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.11.22)

From the parameter setting, in particular (
2.1.13
) in Section
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha}\cdot\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mathring{\lambda}}\leq\lambda^{-\alpha-b\gamma}.

(6.11.23)

(
6.11.22
) and (
6.11.23
) together yield the desired
L^{\infty}_{x,t}
-estimate for
\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}
.

The
L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}
-estimate of
\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}
follows from (
6.11.15
). The factor
\lambda^{-(b-1)\gamma}
in (
6.11.15
) absorbs the constant in
\lesssim
.

Step 6.11.3

(The main oscillatory terms)
.

For terms like

\displaystyle\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}h_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi),

(6.11.24)

we apply Lemma
6.42
to deduce, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\ref{e:6:ind_resolve})\|_{\infty}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\gamma}\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\cdot\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\lambda}{\mathring{\lambda}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{x}}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\lambda}}{\mathring{\mu}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{t}}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.

(6.11.25)

From the parameter setting including (
2.1.13
) in Section
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}^{-\alpha-b\gamma}\cdot\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\frac{\lambda}{\mathring{\lambda}}\leq\lambda^{-\alpha-b\gamma}\cdot\bar{\lambda}^{-(b-1)^{2}b\gamma}.

(6.11.26)

Now we use
\bar{\lambda}^{-(b-1)^{2}b\gamma}
to absorb the constant in
\lesssim
of (
6.11.25
). This finishes the
L^{\infty}_{x,t}
-estimate for the terms of form (
6.11.24
).

To derive the
L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}
-estimate of
(\ref{e:6:ind_resolve})
, we have that, similar to (
6.11.25
) and (
6.11.26
)

\begin{split}\|(\ref{e:6:ind_resolve})\|_{\infty}\lesssim&\,\lambda^{-\alpha-b\gamma}\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},\\
\|\nabla(\ref{e:6:ind_resolve})\|_{\infty}\lesssim&\,\mathring{\lambda}\lambda^{-\alpha-b\gamma}\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{split}

(6.11.27)

Then we interpolate between
\|(\ref{e:6:ind_resolve})\|_{\infty}
and
\|\nabla(\ref{e:6:ind_resolve})\|_{\infty}
to get the
L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}
-estimate, i.e.

\begin{split}\|(\ref{e:6:ind_resolve})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\leq&\,\|\nabla(\ref{e:6:ind_resolve})\|_{\infty}^{\alpha_{0}}\|(\ref{e:6:ind_resolve})\|_{\infty}^{1-\alpha_{0}}\\
\lesssim&\,\lambda^{-\alpha-b\gamma}\lambda^{-\big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\mathring{\lambda}^{\alpha_{0}}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}},\end{split}

(6.11.28)

which indeed follows from (
6.11.27
) and the parameter relations in Section
2.1
, in particular (
2.1.2
), (
2.1.5
) and (
2.1.6
).

∎

Proof of Corollary
6.51
.

The proof of (
6.11.17
) is analogous to (
6.11.19
) in Corollary
6.52
. For the proof of (
6.11.18
), the only difference is the control of constants in
\lesssim
. In the expansion formula (
6.8.3
) of
\varrho_{0}
in Lemma
6.45
, we deduce from (
6.8.10
) and (
6.8.9
) that

\displaystyle\|\bar{\rho}_{0,0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\bigg(1-\frac{4}{q}\bigg)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(6.11.29)

\displaystyle\|\bar{\rho}_{0,a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-ab\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(v,\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(6.11.30)

The
\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C^{\alpha_{0}}_{x}}
of other terms in (
6.8.3
) are estimated similarly as in Step
6.11.3
and Step
6.11.1
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.49
.

We apply Lemma
4.2
to
\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\varrho_{n}
. Note that
[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}]\leq 3Q
. Then we get

\displaystyle\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\varrho_{n}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{(Q+1)(n+1)}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{n}}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}]}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{n,a}\sum_{k=1}^{(3N^{2})^{[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}]}}h_{n,a,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}\eta_{n,a,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{n,a,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.11.31)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\tilde{\rho}_{n}

(6.11.32)

with
|\boldsymbol{\beta}\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq 4Q
,
h_{n,a,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}(7NQ)
and

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{n,a,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\bigg(\frac{\lambda}{\mathring{\lambda}}\bigg)^{|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}|_{x}}+\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\mathring{\lambda}}.

(6.11.33)

The corrector components satisfy

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi_{n,a,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+1)-3Q,

(6.11.34)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta_{n,a,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+1)-3Q.

(6.11.35)

Here, for any
a,k,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l
,
\big(\chi_{n,a,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l)},\eta_{n,a,k}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta},l)}\big)
satisfies the generalized shear condition.

Then we apply Corollary
4.3
to
g_{n}\in\mathcal{\bar{P}}(2NQ)
, to deduce

\displaystyle g_{n}=g_{n,0}+\sum_{j=1}^{(10N)^{8NQ}}\ddot{\eta}_{n,j}(\mu t)\ddot{\chi}_{n,j}(\lambda\Phi)g_{n,j}

(6.11.36)

with
g_{n,j}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}\big(8N^{2}Q\big)
and

\displaystyle\sum_{j}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|g_{n,j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\kappa\mathring{\lambda}^{2}\lambda^{-s_{n}b\gamma},

(6.11.37)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\ddot{\chi}_{n,j}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3},

(6.11.38)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\ddot{\eta}_{n,j}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}.

(6.11.39)

Here, for any
j
,
\big(\ddot{\chi}_{n,j},\ddot{\eta}_{n,j}\big)
satisfies the generalized shear condition.

Next we expand the product of
g_{n}
and
\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\varrho_{n}
and renumber all terms in the following form

\displaystyle g_{n}\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\varrho_{n}=F_{n}+\tilde{f}_{n}+g_{n}\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\tilde{\varrho}_{n}

(6.11.40)

with

\displaystyle F_{n}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{(Q+1)(n+1)}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{n,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I^{\prime}_{n}}\mathcal{\mathbb{P}}_{Q}h_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi),

(6.11.42)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{n}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{(Q+1)(n+1)}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{n,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I^{\prime}_{n}}\Big(h_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\mathcal{\mathbb{P}}_{Q}h_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\Big)\eta_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)

(6.11.43)

\displaystyle\,+\sum_{a=0}^{(Q+1)(n+1)}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=Q,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q+[\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}]}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{n,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I^{\prime}_{n}}h_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi),

(6.11.44)

I^{\prime}_{n}\lesssim 1
,
h_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}(9NQ)
and

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\kappa\lambda\mathring{\lambda}\cdot\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-s_{n}b\gamma},

(6.11.45)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+1)-3Q,

(6.11.46)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+1)-3Q.

(6.11.47)

Naturally, for any
a,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l
,
\big(\chi_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\eta_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\big)
satisfies the generalized shear condition.

Now we define
\varrho_{n+1,0}
,
\varrho_{n+1,1}
and
\varrho_{n+1,2}
to be the solutions of

\displaystyle O_{t}\varrho_{n+1,0}-\kappa\Delta\varrho_{n+1,0}=

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\operatorname{div}}F_{n},

(6.11.48)

\displaystyle\varrho_{n+1,0}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.11.49)

\displaystyle O_{t}\varrho_{n+1,1}-\kappa\Delta\varrho_{n+1,1}=

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\operatorname{div}}\tilde{f}_{n},

(6.11.50)

\displaystyle\varrho_{n+1,1}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,0,

(6.11.51)

\displaystyle O_{t}\varrho_{n+1,2}-\kappa\Delta\varrho_{n+1,2}=

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\operatorname{div}}\big(g_{n}\mathring{D}^{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{n}}\tilde{\varrho}_{n}\big),

(6.11.52)

\displaystyle\varrho_{n+1,2}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,0.

(6.11.53)

From Corollary
3.6
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}h_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\kappa\lambda\mathring{\lambda}\lambda^{-s_{n}b\gamma}\quad\text{for }[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q^{3}

(6.11.54)

and subsequently from (
6.11.6
), Lemma
6.42
,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{f}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-\frac{Q\gamma}{2}-\alpha-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}\quad\text{for }[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q+3Q(Q-n-1).

(6.11.55)

Here, we gain the negative power
-\frac{Q\gamma}{2}
for the terms in (
6.11.43
) and (
6.11.44
). For (
6.11.43
), we gain this power because of Corollary
3.6
and the lower bound
Q
on the differentiation order of
h_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\mathcal{\mathbb{P}}_{Q}h_{F,n,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}
. For (
6.11.44
), we gain this power because of the lower bound
Q
on
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|
and Lemma
6.42
.

With (
6.11.55
), we apply energy estimates in Lemma
5.13
to deduce

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{n+1,1}\|\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-3-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}\quad\text{for }[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2Q+3Q(Q-n-1).

(6.11.56)

From (
6.11.8
) and energy estimate in Corollary
5.14
, we also have

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\varrho_{n+1,2}\|\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-3-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}\quad\text{for }[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2Q+3Q(Q-n-1).

(6.11.57)

For
\varrho_{n+1,0}
, we apply the expansion in Lemma
6.48
to (
6.11.48
-
6.11.49
). Here we briefly verify the assumptions. The conditions in Assumption
6.34
follow from (
6.11.45
)-(
6.11.47
) and the definition of the projection
\mathbb{P}
. Suitably redistributing constants for
h_{F,\cdot}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}
and
\bar{\rho}_{F,\cdot}
, we can verify (
6.10.1
)-(
6.10.4
) from (
6.11.2
) and (
6.11.5
)-(
6.11.7
). Then we get the following expansion

\displaystyle\varrho_{n+1,0}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{(Q+1)(n+2)}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{Q}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}\sum_{l=1}^{I_{n+1}}h_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\eta_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu t)\chi_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\lambda\Phi)+\sum_{a=0}^{Q}\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}+\tilde{\rho}_{F}

with
I_{n+1}\lesssim 1
,
h_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\in\bar{\mathcal{P}}\big(Q\big)
and

\displaystyle\sum{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}.

For the correctors, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\chi_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+1)-4Q,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\eta_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 8Q^{3}-6Q(n+1)-4Q.

Here, for any
a,\boldsymbol{\alpha},l
,
\big(\chi_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\eta_{n+1,a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\big)
satisfies the generalized shear condition.
\bar{\rho}_{n+1,a}
satisfies (
6.11.14
) for any
0\leq a\leq(Q+1)(n+2)
. We also have

\displaystyle\|\mathring{O}^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{\rho}_{F}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{-3-\big([\boldsymbol{\beta}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n}(s_{i}-1)\big)b\gamma}\Upsilon_{\mathrm{in}}\quad\text{for }[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq Q+4Q^{2}(Q-n-1).

Finally, setting

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{n+1}=\tilde{\rho}_{F}+\varrho_{n+1,1}+\varrho_{n+1,2}

concludes the proof.

∎

7.
Homogenization in dissipative range

In this section, we prove the inductive homogenization estimates from step
q
to step
q+1
for dissipative range. In dissipative range, the difference between eddy diffusivity and molecular diffusivity is very small and can be treated as a perturbation. Throughout this section, we work with diffusivity
\kappa\geq 0
satisfying

\displaystyle\kappa\geq\kappa_{q}.

(7.0.1)

7.1.
Main results of this section

Using the notations introduced in Section
6.2
, we state the main results as follows

Theorem 7.1
.

Fix some
q_{*}\leq q
with (
7.0.1
). Suppose that, for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q}^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(7.1.1)

then

\displaystyle\left||\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa)}-|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q+1},\kappa)}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q+1}^{-\gamma}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(7.1.2)

Theorem 7.2
.

Fix some
q_{*}\leq q
with (
7.0.1
). Suppose that, for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q}^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(7.1.3)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u_{q},\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\left(1-\frac{4}{q}\right)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(7.1.4)

then for any
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q+1},\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q+1},\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(7.1.5)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}_{u_{q+1},\kappa}\leq

\displaystyle\,\left(1-\frac{4}{q+1}\right)\bigg(|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q+1},\kappa)}+\sum_{j=q_{*}}^{q+1}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(7.1.6)

The proof strategy of Theorem
7.2
is the same as Theorem
6.10
. Since we have a relatively large diffusivity
\kappa
, the expansion lemma and its proof can be simplified. At heuristic level, since the difference between eddy diffusivity and molecular diffusivity is very small, we treat this difference as a perturbation. Hence, we do not need to solve cell problem in time in homogenization.

7.2.
Notations of this section

Consider the following equation for
\varrho:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}O_{q+1,t}\varrho-\kappa\Delta\varrho=&\,0,\\
\varrho(\cdot,0)=&\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{split}

(7.2.1)

As in Section
6.3
, we use the notation introduced in Definition
6.18
. We also introduce the following simplified notations, slightly different from those in Definition
6.17
.

Definition 7.3

(Parameter)
.

Define

\displaystyle\lambda:=\lambda_{q+1},\quad\delta:=\delta_{q+1},\quad\mu:=\mu_{q+1},\quad\bar{\lambda}:=\lambda_{q},\quad\bar{\delta}:=\delta_{q},\quad\bar{\mu}:=\mu_{q},

(7.2.2)

\displaystyle\mathring{\lambda}:=\mathring{\lambda}_{q+1},\quad\mathring{\mu}:=\mathring{\mu}_{q+1},\quad\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}:=\mathring{\lambda}_{q},\quad\mathring{\bar{\mu}}:=\mathring{\mu}_{q},

(7.2.3)

\displaystyle\varepsilon:=\frac{\bar{\delta}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\bar{\lambda}^{1+2b\gamma_{R}}}{\mu}

(7.2.4)

From (
2.1.10
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mu}{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}\leq\lambda^{\gamma_{S}}.

(7.2.5)

From (
2.1.6
) and (
2.1.9
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mu}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{2b\gamma+\gamma_{S}}\leq 1.

(7.2.6)

7.3.
Expansion lemma

Lemma 7.4

(The expansion lemma)
.

For
N,Q
chosen in Section
2.1
, consider the equation (
7.2.1
), under Definition
7.3
and Definition
6.18
. Then the solution
\varrho
admits the expansion

\begin{split}\varrho(x,t)=&\sum_{n=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\Big(\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t))+\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,t)\Big)+\tilde{\rho}(x,t),\end{split}

(7.3.1)

with
\hat{\rho}_{n},\bar{\rho}_{n},\tilde{\rho}
given by the following.

(C.1)

(Spatial correctors)

\hat{\rho}_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
has the form

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(7.3.2)

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\xi)=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(x,t)\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\mu t)\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\xi)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}(x,t)

(7.3.3)

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n}\in

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{\bar{P}}(N(n-1)+2-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}],0)

(7.3.4)

with
\hat{h}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}_{\cdot}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
\hat{\chi}_{\cdot}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\hat{\eta}_{\cdot}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying the estimates for
k\geq 0

\displaystyle\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\,\leq 2(3N)^{k},

(7.3.5)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}(\varepsilon\lambda)^{k},

(7.3.6)

\displaystyle\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1}^{2k+1}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{k+1}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}(\varepsilon\lambda)^{k},

(7.3.7)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}^{p}\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 11Q^{3},\quad\langle\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}\rangle_{\xi}=0,

(7.3.8)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 11Q^{3}-k-1.

(7.3.9)

Here the sum of differentiation index
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
in (
7.3.3
) is among all indices in
\mathcal{I}_{*}
such that
1\leq[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]\leq 2(n-a)-1
. Moreover,
(\hat{\chi}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n},\hat{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n})
satisfy the shear condition in Definition
2.5
for any
n
,
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
and
l
.

(C.2)

(Residual correctors)

\bar{\rho}_{0},\{\bar{\rho}_{n}\}_{1\leq n\leq N}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
solve

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(7.3.10)

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}\bar{\rho}_{n}=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{2(n-a)+1}\sum_{l=1}^{\bar{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\mathring{\bar{\operatorname{div}}}\Big(\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}\odot\bar{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{n-a}(\mu t)\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{a}\Big)

(7.3.11)

\displaystyle\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}_{n}\in

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{\bar{P}}(Nn+3-[\boldsymbol{\alpha}],0)

(7.3.12)

with
\bar{L}_{0}
given by

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{0}=\bar{D}_{t}-\kappa\Delta

(7.3.13)

and initial datum given by

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{0}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}},

(7.3.14)

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{n}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,0,\quad n\geq 1.

(7.3.15)

Here,
the vector-valued functions

\bar{h}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}_{\cdot}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\bar{\eta}^{(\cdot,\cdot)}_{\cdot}:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfy the estimates for
k\geq 0

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\kappa\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\bigg)^{2}(\varepsilon\lambda)^{k}.

(7.3.16)

\displaystyle\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1}^{2k+3}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bar{h}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\kappa\bigg(\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\bigg)^{2}(\varepsilon\lambda)^{k}.

(7.3.17)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}^{p}\bar{\eta}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}_{k+1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim 1,\quad p\leq 11Q^{3}-k-1.

(7.3.18)

(C.3)

(remainder)

\tilde{\rho}
solves the following equation

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{t}\tilde{\rho}-\operatorname{div}(A\nabla\tilde{\rho})=

\displaystyle\,\tilde{f},

(7.3.19)

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}(x,0)=

\displaystyle\,0.

(7.3.20)

Here,
\tilde{f}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{f}:=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}L_{0}(\hat{\rho}_{Q}+\bar{\rho}_{Q})

(7.3.21)

and differential operators
L_{1},L_{0},L_{-1}
acting on general functions
\rho
taking arguments
(x,t,\xi,\tau)
are given by

\displaystyle L_{0}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\partial_{\xi_{i}}H_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\bar{D}_{t}\rho-\kappa\Delta_{x}\rho-\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\sum_{m}\sigma_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{x_{i}}\Omega_{m,ij}H_{12}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho

(7.3.22)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{\xi_{i}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{x_{j}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho

(7.3.23)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}\phi_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{x_{j}}B_{m,ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho-2\kappa\lambda\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{i}}\rho-2\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}\phi_{m}(\mu\cdot)B_{m,ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{j}}\rho

(7.3.24)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\kappa\lambda^{2}\sum_{m}S_{m,ij}\vartheta_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{\xi_{i}\xi_{j}}\rho+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\phi_{*}(\mu\cdot)\omega_{*}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho

(7.3.25)

Sketch of proof to Theorem
7.2
.

Compared with Theorem
6.10
, the difference in Theorem
7.2
is only due to the larger diffusivity
\kappa
.

To prove Theorem
7.2
, it suffices to verify two main ingredients. The first ingredient is unconditional energy estimates, in the form of Proposition
6.23
and Proposition
6.28
. From Lemma
5.10
, Lemma
5.13
and Lemma
5.14
, these obvious hold, since the larger diffusivity yields stronger energy estimates.

The second ingredient is the homogenization estimates via asymptotic expansion. The whole homogenization analysis is essentially consequences of Lemma
6.29
and Lemma
6.35
. And Lemma
6.35
is a variant of Lemma
6.29
. Therefore, it boils down to prove Lemma
7.4
, which is the variant of Lemma
6.29
in the dissipative range.
∎

7.4.
Proof of Lemma
7.4

In this section, we prove Lemma
7.4
. The proof is largely similar to the proof of Lemma
6.29
, except for two different points. The first point is that we choose to deal with easier cell problems, which are set up in Section
7.4.1
below. The second point is the different parameter relations, hence we need to verify the expansion converges under these parameter relations. This is done in Section
7.4.2
, Section
7.4.3
and Section
7.4.4
. We shall omit many other similar details.

7.4.1.
Set up the asymptotic system for Lemma
7.4

As in inertial range homogenization, we have

\displaystyle L\varrho=\bar{D}_{t}\varrho-\operatorname{div}(A\nabla\varrho)=0.

(7.4.1)

We make the following
ansatz

\displaystyle\varrho(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\rho_{n}(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t))+\tilde{\rho}(x,t).

(7.4.2)

for some function
\rho_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
.

Define
\hat{\rho}_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with arguments
(x,t,\xi)
via

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,t):=

\displaystyle\,\langle\rho_{n}(x,t,\cdot)\rangle_{\xi},

(7.4.3)

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\xi):=

\displaystyle\,\rho_{n}(x,t,\xi)-\langle\rho_{n}(x,t,\cdot)\rangle_{\xi}.

(7.4.4)

Then we have the natural decomposition for
\rho_{n}
with
n\geq 0

\displaystyle\rho_{n}(x,t,\xi)=\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\xi)+\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,t)

(7.4.5)

and subsequently

\displaystyle\varrho(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{Q}\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}}\big(\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t))+\bar{\rho}_{n}(x,t)\big)+\tilde{\rho}(x,t).

(7.4.6)

We have, for any
x,t
,

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\cdot)\rangle_{\xi}=0.

(7.4.7)

An analogue of Lemma
6.39
is given as follows. There are two crucial differences. Since we do not homogenize in time variable
\mu t
, the derivatives in variable
\mu t
are treated as slow/macroscopic derivatives. The second difference is that the operators in cell problems are different, because of the simplification given by stronger diffusion.

Lemma 7.5
.

Under Definition
7.3
and Definition
6.18
, for a general function
\varrho:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
given by

\displaystyle\varrho(x,t)=\rho(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t)),

(7.4.8)

not necessarily solving (
7.4.1
), we have

\displaystyle L\varrho=\lambda L_{1}\rho+L_{0}\rho

(7.4.9)

with

\displaystyle L_{1}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\kappa\lambda\Delta_{\xi}\rho-\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\det\nabla\Phi)^{2}\operatorname{div}_{\xi}H\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho+\sum_{m}z_{m}(\mu\cdot)\omega_{m}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho,

\displaystyle L_{0}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\partial_{\xi_{i}}H_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\bar{D}_{t}\rho-\kappa\Delta_{x}\rho-\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\sum_{m}\sigma_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{x_{i}}\Omega_{m,ij}H_{12}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{\xi_{i}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{x_{j}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho-\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}\phi_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{x_{j}}B_{m,ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,2\kappa\lambda\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{i}}\rho-2\varepsilon\kappa\lambda\sum_{m}\phi_{m}(\mu\cdot)B_{m,ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{j}}\rho+\varepsilon\kappa\lambda^{2}\sum_{m}S_{m,ij}\vartheta_{m}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{\xi_{i}\xi_{j}}\rho+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\phi_{*}(\mu\cdot)\omega_{*}\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{0\leq k\leq 9}L_{0k}\rho

Here, we omit the argument
(\xi,\mu t)
of
H
. We define
L_{00}:=-\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\partial_{\xi_{i}}H_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho
,
L_{01}\rho:=D_{t}\rho-\kappa\Delta_{x}\rho
, and
L_{0k},k\geq 2
is defined by the order of relevant terms.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
6.39
. Computing the terms
\operatorname{div}(A\nabla\varrho)
, namely
\operatorname{div}_{\xi}(\nabla\Phi A\nabla\Phi^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\rho)
,
\operatorname{div}_{\xi}(\nabla\Phi A\nabla_{x}\rho)
,
\operatorname{div}_{x}(A\nabla\Phi^{T}\nabla_{\xi}\rho)
and
\operatorname{div}_{x}(A\nabla_{x}\rho)
is done in (
6.6.11
-
6.6.17
).

For the transport term, we deduce from (
6.6.9
) and (
6.3.16
), that

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{t}\varrho=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{t}\rho+\lambda\partial_{t}\Phi_{i}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho+\bar{u}\cdot\nabla_{x}\rho+\lambda\bar{u}\cdot\nabla\Phi_{i}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\bar{D}_{t}\rho+\lambda\bar{D}_{t}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\bar{D}_{t}\rho+\lambda Z\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho+\lambda^{-2}\Omega\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho.

(7.4.10)

From (
7.4.1
), (
7.4.8
), (
6.6.11
-
6.6.17
) and (
6.3.19
), we have (
6.6.10
) with

\displaystyle L_{1}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\kappa\lambda\Delta_{\xi}\rho-\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\det\nabla\Phi)^{2}\operatorname{div}_{\xi}H\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho+Z\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho,

(7.4.11)

\displaystyle L_{0}\rho=

\displaystyle\,-\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\partial_{\xi_{i}}H_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\bar{D}_{t}\rho-\kappa\Delta_{x}\rho-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda}\partial_{x_{i}}(\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi H\operatorname{adj}\nabla\Phi^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho

(7.4.12)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}\partial_{\xi_{i}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{x_{j}}\rho+\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\sum_{m}\varphi_{m}\partial_{x_{j}}(HE_{m}^{T})_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho-\kappa\lambda\partial_{x_{j}}\nabla\Phi_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\rho

(7.4.13)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,2\kappa\lambda\nabla\Phi_{ij}\partial_{\xi_{i}x_{j}}\rho+\varepsilon\kappa\lambda^{2}\operatorname{div}_{\xi}(\Xi\nabla_{\xi}\rho)+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\Omega\cdot\nabla_{\xi}\rho,

(7.4.14)

Now we use (
6.3.4
), (
6.3.18
) and (
6.3.19
) and above to conclude the proof.

∎

From Lemma
7.5
, we have

\begin{split}L\varrho=&\,\lambda L_{1}\rho_{0}+L_{1}\rho_{1}+L_{0}\rho_{0}+\frac{1}{\lambda}(L_{1}\rho_{2}+L_{0}\rho_{1})+\ldots\\
+&\,\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q-1}}(L_{1}\rho_{Q}+L_{0}\rho_{Q-1})+\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}L_{0}\rho_{Q}+L\tilde{\rho}\end{split}

(7.4.15)

Then we seek for
\{\rho_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq Q}
such that

\displaystyle L_{2}\rho_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(7.4.16)

\displaystyle L_{1}\rho_{1}+L_{0}\rho_{0}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(7.4.17)

\displaystyle L_{1}\rho_{n+1}+L_{0}\rho_{n}=

\displaystyle\,0

(7.4.18)

with initial conditions

\displaystyle\rho_{0}(x,0,\xi)=

\displaystyle\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}(x),

(7.4.19)

\displaystyle\rho_{n}(x,0,\xi)=

\displaystyle\,0,\quad\text{for any }n\geq 1.

(7.4.20)

Then
\tilde{\rho}
satisfies

\begin{split}\bar{D}_{t}\tilde{\rho}-\operatorname{div}(A\nabla\tilde{\rho})=&\,\tilde{f}:=-\frac{1}{\lambda^{Q}}(L_{0}\rho_{Q})(x,t,\lambda\Phi(x,t)),\\
\tilde{\rho}(x,0)=&\,0.\end{split}

(7.4.21)

If we have
\{\rho_{n}\}_{0\leq n\leq Q}
and
\tilde{\rho}
such that (
7.4.16
)-(
7.4.21
) hold, the function
\varrho
given by (
7.4.6
) solves (
7.4.1
). Thus, to prove Lemma
7.4
, it remains to solve (
7.4.16
)-(
7.4.21
).

Remark 7.6
.

For
\rho(x,t,\tau)=\hat{\rho}(x,t,\tau)+\bar{\rho}(x,t)
with

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\rho}(x,t,\cdot)\rangle_{\xi}=0\quad\text{for any }x,t,

we have

\displaystyle\langle L_{1}\rho\rangle_{\xi}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(7.4.22)

\displaystyle\langle L_{0}\rho\rangle_{\xi}=

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{00}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi}+L_{01}\bar{\rho}+\langle L_{02}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi}+\langle L_{03}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi}+\langle L_{04}\hat{\rho}\rangle_{\xi},

(7.4.23)

\displaystyle L_{0}\rho=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k=0}^{3}L_{0k}(\hat{\rho}+\bar{\rho})+\sum_{k=4}^{9}L_{0k}\hat{\rho}.

(7.4.24)

7.4.2.

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{0},\hat{\rho}_{1},\bar{\rho}_{0}.

7.4.3.
Induction step for residual correctors

In this induction step, we assume we have
\bar{\rho}_{0}
,
\bar{\rho}_{1},\ldots,

and

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{n}(x,t,\xi)=

The goal is to deduce the structures of

Similar to the proof of Lemma

and Lemma

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle+

symmetric terms

(7.4.55)

\displaystyle\,-\mathring{\bar{\mu}}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu\cdot)\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.58)

\displaystyle\,+\kappa\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu\cdot)\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{ii}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.59)

\displaystyle\,+\kappa\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu\cdot)\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.60)

\displaystyle\,-\mu\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\partial_{\tau}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu\cdot)\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.61)

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{02}\hat{\rho}_{n,0}\rangle_{\xi}-L_{02}\hat{\rho}_{n,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}}{\lambda}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\sum_{m}[\eta_{1}\sigma_{m}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}](\mu\cdot)\big(\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\langle\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi}\big)\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\Omega_{m,ij}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{j}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.62)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}}{\lambda}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\sum_{m}[\eta_{1}\sigma_{m}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}](\mu\cdot)\big(\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\langle\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi}\big)\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\Omega_{m,ij}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{j}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.63)

\displaystyle+

symmetric terms

(7.4.64)

\displaystyle\,\langle L_{03}\hat{\rho}_{n,0}\rangle_{\xi}-L_{03}\hat{\rho}_{n,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\sum_{m}[\eta_{1}\varphi_{m}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}](\mu\cdot)\big(\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\langle\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi}\big)E_{m,j2}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{j}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.65)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\sum_{m}[\eta_{1}\varphi_{m}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}](\mu\cdot)\big(\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\langle\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi}\big)E_{m,j2}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{j}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.66)

\displaystyle+

symmetric terms

(7.4.67)

\displaystyle-L_{06}\hat{\rho}_{n,0}=

\displaystyle\,2\kappa\lambda\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\hat{h}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\eta}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{\xi_{i}}\hat{\chi}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.68)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,2\kappa\lambda\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\eta}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{\xi_{i}}\hat{\chi}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.69)

\displaystyle-L_{08}\hat{\rho}_{n,0}=

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\kappa\lambda^{2}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\hat{h}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\eta}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{\xi_{i}}\hat{\chi}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}

(7.4.70)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\kappa\lambda^{2}\sum_{[\boldsymbol{\alpha}]=1,\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{I}_{*}}^{B}\sum_{l=1}^{\hat{I}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}}\hat{h}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\hat{\eta}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}(\mu\cdot)\partial_{\xi_{i}}\hat{\chi}_{n-a}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\mathring{\bar{D}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bar{\rho}_{0}.

(7.4.71)

For
a=0
and a fixed
\boldsymbol{\beta}
, we consider all contributions above and renumber them with index
1\leq l\leq\hat{I}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta})}
, in the form of (
7.4.51
).

Case 7.4.2
.

If
2\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2n
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}=2\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, the contribution from (
7.4.54
) gives at most
\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
correctors with

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\quad\hat{\eta}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=\eta_{1}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\Delta_{\xi}^{-1}\big(\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\langle\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi}\big).

The contributions from (
7.4.53
) and (
7.4.55
) are similar to above.

Case 7.4.3
.

If
1\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2n-1
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, the contribution from (
7.4.56
) gives at most
\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
correctors with

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathring{\bar{\mu}}}{\kappa\lambda}\mathring{\bar{D}}_{t}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\quad\hat{\eta}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\Delta_{\xi}^{-1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}.

Case 7.4.4
.

If
1\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2n-1
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, the contribution from (
7.4.57
) gives at most
\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
correctors with

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}^{2}}{\lambda}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{ii}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\quad\hat{\eta}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\Delta_{\xi}^{-1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}.

Case 7.4.5
.

If
1\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2n-1
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, the contribution from (
7.4.61
) gives at most
\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
correctors with

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mu}{\kappa\lambda}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\quad\hat{\eta}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=\partial_{\tau}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\Delta_{\xi}^{-1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}.

Case 7.4.6
.

If
1\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2n-1
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, the contribution from (
7.4.65
) gives at most
\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
correctors with

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\frac{\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}}{\kappa\lambda}E_{m,j2}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{j}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\quad\hat{\eta}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=\eta_{1}\varphi_{m}\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\Delta_{\xi}^{-1}\big(\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\langle\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi}\big).

Case 7.4.7
.

If
1\leq[\boldsymbol{\beta}]\leq 2n-1
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, the contribution from (

) gives at most
\hat{I}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}
correctors with

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,2\mathring{\bar{\lambda}}\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\quad\hat{\eta}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\Delta_{\xi}^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{i}}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}.

Case 7.4.8
.

If

and
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, the contribution from (

) gives at most
