correctors with

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\lambda\mathring{\bar{\partial}}_{i}\hat{h}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},\quad\hat{\eta}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=\hat{\eta}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)},

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{n+1}^{(\boldsymbol{\beta},\cdot)}=

\displaystyle\,\Delta_{\xi}^{-1}\big(\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}-\langle\partial_{\xi_{1}}\Pi_{1}\hat{\chi}_{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},l)}\rangle_{\xi}\big).

This closes our derivation on
\hat{\rho}_{n+1}
.

8.
Proof of the main result

8.1.
Choice of parameters

Let us start with
\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}
as specified in the Theorem, i.e.
0<\beta_{0}<\frac{1}{3}
and
\alpha_{0}>0
such that
2\alpha_{0}+\beta_{0}<1
, and
s>\frac{1+\beta_{0}}{2}
,
\alpha^{\prime}\in(\alpha_{0},1)
. Choose
\beta>\beta_{0}
so that
\beta<\frac{1}{3}
,
2\alpha_{0}+\beta<1
and
\frac{1+\beta}{2}<s
. We then choose the parameters
b
,
\gamma_{I}
,
\gamma_{R}
,
\gamma_{S}
,
\gamma
,
\lambda_{0}
,
N_{*}
,
N
and
Q
according to Section
2.1
. Our aim is then to construct a divergence-free velocity field
u\in C^{\beta_{0}}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,1])
such that (
1.1.2
) and (
1.1.3
) with
\alpha=\alpha_{0}
holds.

8.2.
The velocity field

We construct the vector field
u
as in Section
2.3
. In particular, estimate (
3.0.18
) (with
q+1
replaced with
q
) yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}_{q}=\lambda^{-\beta}_{q}

\displaystyle\|\nabla\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}_{q}\lambda_{q}=\lambda^{1-\beta}_{q}

\displaystyle\|D_{q}\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q}\lambda_{q}=\lambda^{1-2\beta}_{q}

Since

\partial_{t}\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}=D_{q}\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}-u_{q}\cdot\nabla\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q},

the above estimates give also

\|\partial_{t}\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\lambda^{1-2\beta}_{q}+\|u_{q}\|_{\infty}\lambda^{1-\beta}_{q}\lesssim\lambda^{1-\beta}_{q},

with the last estimate valid in view of (
3.0.17
).
Using half of the gap
g:=\beta-\beta_{0}
and the base of our superexponential sequence to consume the constants of
\lesssim
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{-\nicefrac{{g}}{{2}}}_{q}\lambda^{-\beta_{0}}_{q}

\displaystyle\|\nabla\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}\|_{\infty}+\|\partial_{t}\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{-\nicefrac{{g}}{{2}}}_{q}\lambda^{1-\beta_{0}}_{q}.

Consequently by interpolation

\|\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}\|_{C_{xt}^{\beta_{0}}}\leq\lambda^{-\nicefrac{{g}}{{2}}}_{q}

Since
u_{m}=\sum_{q=0}^{m}\nabla^{\perp}\psi_{q}
and our
\{\lambda_{q}\}_{q}
is at least geometric, this gives for any
n\geq m\geq q_{0}

\|u_{m}-u_{n}\|_{C_{xt}^{\beta_{0}}}\leq\lambda^{-\nicefrac{{g}}{{4}}}_{m},

so
\{u_{q}\}_{q\geq q_{0}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
{C_{xt}^{\beta_{0}}}
.

8.3.
Proof of (
1.1.3
) in Theorem
1.1

For the sequence
\{\kappa_{q}\}_{q}
set in Section
2.1
, our goal is to estimate the solution to the following equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{n}+u\cdot\nabla\rho_{n}-\kappa_{n}\Delta\rho_{n}=

\displaystyle 0,

(8.3.1)

\displaystyle\rho_{n}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}

for any initial datum
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in H^{1+\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and for an arbitrary
n\geq 1
. In the rest of the proof, we fix an arbitrary
n\geq 1
. For this fixed
n
, we will decompose the problem with respect to parameter
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
In this proof, we will use the subscript
q
as an additional parameter needed for iterations. Thus the final result concerns (
8.3.1
) with
n
, which is replaced in formulation of Theorem
1.1
by
q
.

Step 8.3.1

(Decomposition of the initial datum)
.

Consider the Littlewood-Paley decomposition of the initial datum

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathrm{in}}=\sum_{j\geq-1}\Delta_{j}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}.

(8.3.2)

We define

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathrm{in},k}:=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{k}}\Delta_{j}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}.

(8.3.3)

Here,
\Lambda_{k}=\{j:\lambda_{k-1}<2^{j}\leq\lambda_{k}\}
for
k\geq 1
and
\Lambda_{0}=\{j:2^{j}\leq\lambda_{0}\}
. From Lemma
9.1
, we have the following standard estimate for any integer,

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\nabla}_{k}^{p}\rho_{\mathrm{in},k}\|_{2}\lesssim_{p}

\displaystyle\,\bigg(\frac{\lambda_{k}}{\mathring{\lambda}_{k}}\bigg)^{p}\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{k}}\|\Delta_{j}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2},

(8.3.4)

then we have, using the quantities defined in Section
6.1

\displaystyle\|\rho_{\mathrm{in},k}\|_{2}+|\rho_{\mathrm{in},k}|_{\mathfrak{P}(k)}+\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\rho_{\mathrm{in},k}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{k}}\|\Delta_{j}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}.

(8.3.5)

Step 8.3.2

(Iteration for large-scale datum (
k\leq n
))
.

Fix
k\leq n
. From direct energy estimates in Proposition
6.26
, we have that, for any
\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{k},\kappa_{k}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{1-2\gamma}\bigg(|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{k},\kappa_{k})}+|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(k)}\bigg),

(8.3.6)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{k},\kappa_{k}}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{(1-2\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\bigg(|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{k},\kappa_{k})}+|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(k)}\bigg).

(8.3.7)

Then we use (
6.1.7
) and absorb the constant in
\lesssim
by a small negative power of
\lambda_{k}
to deduce the following: for any
\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
,

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{k},\kappa_{k}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{1-\gamma}\bigg(|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{k},\kappa_{k})}+\lambda_{k}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(k)}\bigg),

(8.3.8)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{k},\kappa_{k}}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{(1-\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\cdot\bigg(1-\frac{4}{k}\bigg)\bigg(|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{k},\kappa_{k})}+\lambda_{k}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(k)}\bigg).

(8.3.9)

Now we apply homogenization estimates of Theorem
6.10
inductively over
q
from
q=k
(so current
k=q_{*}
of Theorem
6.10
) up to
q=n
to deduce the following: for any
q
with
k\leq q\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa_{q}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{1-\gamma}\bigg(|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{q})}+\sum_{j=k}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(8.3.10)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa_{q}}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{(1-\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\bigg(1-\frac{4}{q}\bigg)\bigg(|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{q})}+\sum_{j=k}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(8.3.11)

Here, we use the linearity in Remark
6.11
.

Using (
8.3.10
) and (
8.3.11
) with
q=n
, we apply homogenization estimates of Theorem
7.2
inductively to deduce the following: for any
q\geq n
,

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa_{n}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{1-\gamma}\bigg(|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{n})}+\sum_{j=k}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg),

(8.3.12)

\displaystyle{|\kern-0.86108pt[\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa_{n}}^{(H,\alpha_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{(1-\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\bigg(1-\frac{4}{q}\bigg)\bigg(|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{n})}+\sum_{j=k}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\bigg).

(8.3.13)

Next, we apply the above estimates for
\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}=\rho_{\mathrm{in},k}
to the solution to

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bar{\rho}_{k,q}+u_{q}\cdot\nabla\bar{\rho}_{k,q}-\kappa_{n}\Delta\bar{\rho}_{k,q}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(8.3.14)

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{k,q}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,\rho_{\mathrm{in},k}.

(8.3.15)

This yields, using definition of
{|\kern-0.86108pt[\cdot]\kern-0.86108pt|}_{u_{q},\kappa_{n}}
(preceding Theorem
6.9
) and (
6.1.2
), the following estimate uniformly in
q
for any
q\geq n
,

\displaystyle\|\bar{\rho}_{k,q}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}C_{x}^{\alpha_{0}}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{(1-\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{k}}\|\Delta_{j}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}.

(8.3.16)

Similarly as in Section
8.2
, Lemma
3.3
gives
u_{q}\rightarrow u
in
C^{\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}_{t}C^{\gamma}_{x}
. In particular, we have that
\{u_{q}\}_{q}
is uniformly in
C^{\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}_{t}C^{\gamma}_{x}
(i.e. with q-independent bound). Therefore, since
\kappa_{n}
of (
8.3.16
) is a fixed positive number, from standard Schauder theory we know that
\{\bar{\rho}_{k,q}\}_{q}
is
q
-uniformly bounded in
C_{t}^{\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}C_{x}^{1,\gamma}
. Thus, sending
q\to\infty
we see that
\{\bar{\rho}_{k,q}\}_{q}
, up to a subsequence, converges strongly in
C_{t}^{0}C_{x}^{\alpha_{0}}
to
\rho_{k,n}
uniquely solving the following equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{k,n}+u\cdot\nabla\rho_{k,n}-\kappa_{n}\Delta\rho_{k,n}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(8.3.17)

\displaystyle\rho_{k,n}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,\rho_{\mathrm{in},k}.

(8.3.18)

Knowing the strong convergence, we have from (
8.3.16
) the following estimate for the limiting
\rho_{k,n}

\displaystyle\|\rho_{k,n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}C_{x}^{\alpha_{0}}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{(1-\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{k}}\|\Delta_{j}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2},

(8.3.19)

which is uniform in
k
for any
k\leq n
.

Let us remark that we used Schauder theory only to obtain the qualitative convergence, whilst the estimate (
8.3.19
) follows from the uniform estimate (
8.3.16
) and the qualitative convergence. Obtaining (
8.3.19
) directly from Schauder estimates is unlikely, since they have typically bad constants dependence on
\kappa_{n}
and
u
.

Step 8.3.3

(Iteration for small-scale datum (
k\geq n
))
.

Fix
k\geq n
. We proceed as in last step. Instead of applying Theorem
6.10
and Theorem
7.2
, in this step we only apply Theorem
7.2
. We deduce the estimate (
8.3.19
) uniform in
k
for any
k\geq n
.

Step 8.3.4

(Summing up all frequencies)
.

From Step
8.3.2
and Step
8.3.3
, for fixed
n
, we have the estimate (
8.3.19
) for
\rho_{k,n}
uniform in
k
for any
k\geq 0
. From the definition of
\rho_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\rho_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}C_{x}^{\alpha_{0}}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k\geq 0}\|\rho_{k,n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}C_{x}^{\alpha_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\sum_{k\geq 0}\mathring{\lambda}_{k}^{(1-\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{k}}\|\Delta_{j}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j\geq-1}2^{jb(1-\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})}\|\Delta_{j}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{H^{(1-\gamma)(1+\alpha_{0})b}}

Inspecting the preceding computations, we see that all of the functions are pointwisely defined in time, so we can replace
L_{t}^{\infty}
norm appearing in the preceding computations with
C_{t}^{0}
norm.
This concludes the proof of (
1.1.3
) in Theorem
1.1
.

8.4.
Proof of (
1.1.2
) in Theorem
1.1

Our goal now is the justify the remaining (
1.1.2
).
Recall that the velocity field
u
was constructed in Section
2.3
. For sufficiently large
n
, our goal now is to study the dissipation

\displaystyle\kappa_{n}\iint|\nabla\rho_{n}|^{2}dxdt

(8.4.1)

for the solution to

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\rho_{n}+u\cdot\nabla\rho_{n}-\kappa_{n}\Delta\rho_{n}=&\,0,\\
\rho_{n}(\cdot,0)=&\,\rho_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{split}

(8.4.2)

Step 8.4.1

(Mollification of the initial datum)
.

Define the ratio given by the initial datum

\ell:=\frac{\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}}{\|\nabla^{s}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}},

and mollify the initial datum at length scale
r\ell
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}=\rho_{\mathrm{in}}*m_{r\ell},

(8.4.3)

where
m
is a standard symmetric mollifier (see e.g. Appendix) and a suitably small
0<r<1
is to be fixed. Then, for all
0\leq n\leq 12Q^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}-\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(r\ell)^{s}\|\nabla^{s}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}=Cr^{s}\ell^{s-1}\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2},

(8.4.4)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{n+1}\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(r\ell)^{-(n+1-s)}\|\nabla^{s}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}\leq C(r\ell)^{-(n+1)}r^{s}\ell^{s-1}\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}.

(8.4.5)

In (
8.4.4
), we impose the first condition on
r
:

\displaystyle 2Cr^{s}\ell^{s-1}\leq 1.

(8.4.6)

Step 8.4.2

(The initial step of homogenization: choosing
q_{0}
,
\ell
and
r
)
.

For
q_{0}
to be fixed, we consider the solution to

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}_{q}+u_{q}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{q}-\kappa_{q}\Delta\tilde{\rho}_{q}=

\displaystyle\,0,

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{q}(\cdot,0)=

\displaystyle\,\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}

for any
q\geq q_{0}
. From Poincaré inequality, there exists absolute constant
c_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\tilde{\rho}_{q}(\cdot,t)\|^{2}_{2}dt\leq-2\kappa_{q}\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{q}(\cdot,t)\|^{2}_{2}\leq-c_{0}\kappa_{q}\|\tilde{\rho}_{q}(\cdot,t)\|^{2}_{2}.

(8.4.7)

Integrating (
8.4.7
) from
0
to
T
, we have that, for any
q\geq q_{0}
,

\begin{split}\kappa_{q}\iint|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{q}|^{2}dxdt\geq&\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\exp(-c_{0}\kappa_{q}T)\right)\|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 16c_{1}\kappa_{q}T\|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 4c_{1}\kappa_{q}T\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(8.4.8)

Here
c_{0},c_{1}>0
are absolute constants.

For a small constant
c>0
to be fixed, we require

\displaystyle(r\ell)^{-1}\leq

\displaystyle\,c\mathring{\lambda}_{q_{0}}^{1-b\gamma},

(8.4.9)

\displaystyle r^{s}\ell^{s-1}\leq

\displaystyle\,c(\kappa_{q_{0}}T)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

(8.4.10)

Note that (
8.4.6
) follows from (
8.4.10
) for suitably small
c
. And an equivalent set of conditions for (
8.4.9
)-(
8.4.10
) is

\displaystyle c^{-s}\mathring{\lambda}_{q_{0}}^{(b\gamma-1)s}\ell^{-1}\leq r^{s}\ell^{s-1}\leq c(\kappa_{q_{0}}T)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

(8.4.11)

We use (
8.4.11
) to determine
r
and
q_{0}
. First, notice that that our choices of section
2.1
yield

\displaystyle c^{-1}(\kappa_{q}T)^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}<c^{s}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{(1-b\gamma)s}\ell\quad\text{for sufficiently large }q,

(8.4.12)

provided
s>\nicefrac{{(1+\beta)}}{{2}}
, which is our assumption.
Then we choose
q_{0}
sufficiently large so that (
8.4.12
) holds for
q=q_{0}
. Increasing
q_{0}
if needed we ensure that
\ell^{s-1}>c(\kappa_{q_{0}}T)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
holds; consequently we can choose
r
such that (
8.4.11
) holds.

As a result of (
8.4.9
) and (
8.4.10
), we have the following relations for any
q\geq q_{0}
and
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle(r\ell)^{-1}\leq\,c\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{1-b\gamma},\quad(r\ell)^{-p}r^{s}\ell^{s-1}\leq\,c(\kappa_{q}T)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathring{\lambda}_{q}^{(1-b\gamma)p}.

(8.4.13)

Step 8.4.3

(Homogenization in inertial range)
.

First, we estimate the quantity
\mathfrak{P}(q)
for any
q\geq q_{0}
. Using (
8.4.5
), (
8.4.8
) and (
8.4.11
), we have for any
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{p}\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}\lesssim_{p}\mathring{\lambda}_{q_{0}}^{(1-b\gamma)p}\bigg(\kappa_{q}\iint|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{q}|^{2}dxdt\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(8.4.14)

from which we deduce, for any
q\geq q_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{P}(q)}+\sum_{j=q_{0}}^{q}\lambda_{j}^{-\nicefrac{{\gamma}}{{2}}}|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{S}(j)}\leq

\displaystyle\,|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}|_{\mathfrak{D}(u_{q},\kappa_{q})}.

(8.4.15)

Here, we impose the first condition on
c
in (
8.4.9
)-(
8.4.10
) to ensure (
8.4.15
) holds, i.e. absorbing the constant when deriving (
8.4.15
).

Using (
8.4.15
), we apply Theorem
6.9
inductively to deduce the following: for any
q\geq q_{0}
,

\displaystyle\kappa_{q_{0}}\iint|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{q_{0}}|^{2}dxdt\prod_{j=q_{0}+1}^{q}(1-\lambda_{j}^{-\gamma})\leq\,\kappa_{q}\iint|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{q}|^{2}dxdt.

(8.4.16)

Step 8.4.4

(Homogenization in dissipative range)
.

We consider the solution to

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}^{(n)}+u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\rho}^{(n)}-\kappa_{n}\Delta\tilde{\rho}^{(n)}=&\,0,\\
\tilde{\rho}^{(n)}(\cdot,0)=&\,\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{split}

(8.4.17)

Below
\psi
and
\psi_{n}
are the stream functions associated to
u
and
u_{n}
respectively, as defined in Section
2.3
. Note that from Lemma
3.3
we have

\displaystyle\|\psi-\psi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{n+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{n+1}}\leq\kappa_{n}\lambda_{n}^{-2b\gamma}.

(8.4.18)

Then we apply basic
L^{2}
-stability estimate (see e.g. Proposition 4.3 of
[
burczak2023anomalous
]
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\bigg|\kappa_{n}\iint|\nabla\tilde{\rho}^{(n)}|^{2}dxdt-\kappa_{n}\iint|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{n}|^{2}dxdt\bigg|\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{n}^{-b\gamma}\kappa_{n}\iint|\nabla\tilde{\rho}_{n}|^{2}dxdt.

(8.4.19)

Now we use (
8.4.8
) for
q_{0}
, (
8.4.16
) for
q=n
and (
8.4.19
) to deduce

\displaystyle\kappa_{n}\iint|\nabla\tilde{\rho}^{(n)}|^{2}dxdt\geq 2c_{1}\kappa_{q_{0}}T\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|^{2}_{2}.

(8.4.20)

Finally, we compare (
8.4.2
) and (
8.4.17
). The basic energy estimate and (
8.4.4
) yields

\displaystyle\kappa_{n}\iint|\nabla(\tilde{\rho}^{(n)}-\rho_{n})|^{2}dxdt\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{in}}-\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C^{2}r^{2s}\ell^{2s-2}\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}^{2}.

(8.4.21)

From (
8.4.20
), (
8.4.21
) we have

\displaystyle\bigg(\kappa_{n}\iint|\nabla\rho_{n}|^{2}dxdt\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq

\displaystyle\,\big((c_{1}\kappa_{q_{0}}T)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}-Cr^{s}\ell^{s-1}\big)\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}(c_{1}\kappa_{q_{0}}T)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\|\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{2},

(8.4.22)

where the latter inequality of (
8.4.22
) is valid by imposing in (
8.4.10
) the second condition on
c
.

Notice that (
8.4.22
) is independent of
n\geq q_{0}
, hence we finish the proof of (
1.1.2
).

Proof of Theorem
1.1
is now complete.

9.
Appendix

9.1.
Littlewood-Paley decomposition

Here we collect some standard estimates. We refer to Chapter 2 in
[
BaChDa11
]
and Chapter 3 in
[
GuPer15
]
for Littlewood-Paley theory and Besov spaces. For a function
f
defined on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, we denote its Littlewood-Paley decomposition by

\displaystyle f=\sum_{j\geq-1}\Delta_{j}f.

(9.1.1)

Below we cite the characterization of Hölder spaces by Littlewood-Paley decomposition, see page 38 in
[
GuPer15
]
.

Lemma 9.1

(Littlewood-Paley decomposition)
.

For the decomposition (
9.1.1
), we have the following estimates for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
1\leq p\leq\infty
,

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla^{m}\Delta_{j}f\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim 2^{mj}\|\Delta_{j}f\|_{L^{p}}.

Moreover, the following characterization of Hölder spaces holds for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{\alpha}}\sim\sup_{j\geq-1}2^{j\alpha}\|\Delta_{j}f\|_{L^{\infty}}.

9.2.
Interpolation inequality

We also cite the following Gagliardo-Nirenberg interpolation inequality without proof.

Lemma 9.2

(Gagliardo-Nirenberg interpolation inequality)
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that for any
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla^{m}f\|_{L^{2}}^{a_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1-a_{0}},\quad a_{0}=\frac{2}{2m-n},m>\frac{n}{2},

(9.2.1)

\displaystyle\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla^{m}f\|_{L^{2}}^{a_{1}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-a_{1}},\quad a_{1}=\frac{n+2}{2m},m\geq\frac{n+2}{2},

(9.2.2)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla^{m}f\|_{L^{2}}^{a_{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-a_{2}},\quad a_{2}=\frac{n}{2m},m>\frac{n}{2}.

(9.2.3)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle[f]_{C^{\alpha}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla^{m}f\|_{L^{2}}^{a_{3}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-a_{3}},\quad a_{3}=\frac{n+2\alpha}{2m},m>\alpha+\frac{n}{2}.

(9.2.4)

\displaystyle[f]_{C^{\alpha}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla^{m}f\|_{L^{2}}^{a_{4}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1-a_{4}},\quad a_{4}=\frac{2\alpha}{2m-n},m>\alpha+\frac{n}{2}.

(9.2.5)

9.3.
Mollification

For
N_{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we need mollifiers which are deep smoothing operators of depth
N_{m}
, see
[
GromovBook
, Section 2.3.4]
. The case of infinite depth was introduced by Nash
[
Nash56
]
. For this purpose, we fix a mollification kernel
\mathfrak{m}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
which, in addition to
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathfrak{m}\,dx=1
, also satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathfrak{m}(x)x^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\,dx=0,\quad\textrm{ for any multiindex $\boldsymbol{\alpha}$ with $1\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq N_{m}$ }.

(9.3.1)

If
N_{m}\geq 2
, then
\mathfrak{m}
cannot be nonnegative. As a variant of Lemma 7.2 in
[
burczak2023anomalous
]
, we have the following mollification estimates.

Lemma 9.3

(Mollification)
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a smoothing operator of depth
N_{m}\geq 1
and such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathfrak{m}\,dx=1
. Then for any real
r,s\geq 0
and any
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{n})

\|f*\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\|_{H^{r+s}}\lesssim\ell^{-s}\|f\|_{H^{r}}

(9.3.2)

and for any
r\geq 0
,
0\leq s\leq N_{m}

\|f-f*\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\|_{H^{r}}\lesssim\ell^{s}\|f\|_{H^{r+s}}\\

(9.3.3)

The absolute constants depend on
r,s
and
\mathfrak{m}
.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma 7.2 in
[
burczak2023anomalous
]
.
